M_{\mathrm{set}}
, we choose
k\in M_{\mathrm{set}}
with
k>k_{0}
and
k-k_{0}
sufficiently small so that (
59
) holds. This completes the proof of Lemma
3.2
.
∎

We are now in a position to establish upper and lower bounds of the density. To this end, we state the key Proposition
3.5
, which supplies an estimate for the velocity field and the derivative of the density.

Proposition 3.5
.

Assume that (
12
) holds. Let
k
be as defined in Lemma
3.2
. Then there exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho u^{2k}rdr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\Big(\rho^{\alpha}\frac{u^{2k}}{r}+\rho^{\alpha}(\partial_{r}u)^{2}u^{2k-2}r\Big)drdt\leq C(T)

(62)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2k}}|^{2k}rdr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2k}}|^{2k}rdrdt\leq C(T).

(63)

Proof.

We first establish (
62
).
Using (
56
), we obtain from (
46
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho u^{2k}rdr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\Big(\rho^{\alpha}\frac{u^{2k}}{r}+\rho^{\alpha}(\partial_{r}u)^{2}u^{2k-2}r\Big)drdt\\
&\leq C+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{2k(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha}r^{2k-1}drdt\\
&\leq C+C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\xi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}^{\frac{2k(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha}{\alpha-1/2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}r^{2k-1-\frac{2k(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha}{\alpha-1/2}\xi}drdt\\
&\leq C(T),\end{split}

where
\xi>0
is chosen sufficiently small so that

\displaystyle 2k-1-\frac{2k(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha}{\alpha-1/2}\xi>-1.

We next prove (
63
). Invoking (
56
) and (
62
), one derives from (
53
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2k}}|^{2k}rdr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2k}}|^{2k}rdrdt\\
&\leq C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho u^{2k}rdr+C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho(u+\rho^{-1}\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha})^{2k}rdr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2k}}|^{2k}rdrdt\\
&\leq C(T)+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\gamma-\alpha+1}u^{2k}rdrdt\\
&\leq C(T)+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\alpha}\frac{u^{2k}}{r}\rho^{\gamma-2\alpha+1}r^{2}drdt\\
&\leq C(T)+C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\xi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}^{\frac{\gamma-2\alpha+1}{\alpha-1/2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\alpha}\frac{u^{2k}}{r}drdt\\
&\leq C(T),\end{split}

where we have used

\displaystyle\gamma-2\alpha+1>0,

and
\xi
is taken sufficiently small so that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\gamma-2\alpha+1}{\alpha-1/2}\right)\xi<2.

This completes the proof of Proposition
3.5
.
∎

Finally, we establish the upper and positive lower bounds for the density in two dimensions. Denote that

\displaystyle\begin{split}R_{T}:=\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+1,\quad V_{T}:=\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho^{-1}(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+1.\end{split}

Proposition 3.6
.

Assume that (
12
) holds. There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

\displaystyle R_{T}\leq C(T).

(64)

Proof.

Using (
55
) and (
63
), we obtain from the one-dimensional Sobolev embedding that

\displaystyle\left\|\rho^{\alpha}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}\leq C\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\alpha}dr+C\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha}|dr

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\alpha}r^{\frac{1}{3}}r^{-\frac{1}{3}}dr+C\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2k}}|\rho^{1-\frac{1}{2k}}dr

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{3\alpha}rdr\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(\int_{0}^{R}r^{-\frac{1}{2}}dr\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}+C\left(\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2k}}|^{2k}rdr\right)^{\frac{1}{2k}}\left(\int_{0}^{R}\rho r^{-\frac{1}{2k-1}}dr\right)^{\frac{2k-1}{2k}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)+C(T)\int_{0}^{R}\rho r^{-\frac{1}{2k-1}}dr

\displaystyle\leq C(T)+C(T)\left(\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\frac{2k}{k-1}}rdr\right)^{\frac{k-1}{2k}}\left(\int_{0}^{R}r^{-\frac{2k^{2}-k+1}{2k^{2}+k-1}}dr\right)^{\frac{k+1}{2k}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T).

This completes the proof of Proposition
3.6
.
∎

Proposition 3.7
.

Assume that (
12
) holds. There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

\displaystyle V_{T}\leq C(T).

(65)

Proof.

Let
v(y,\tau)=\frac{1}{\rho(y,\tau)}
. By
\eqref{Equ 3}_{1}
, we know that
v
satisfies the equation
v_{\tau}=(ru)_{y}
. Integrating this equation over
(0,1)\times(0,\tau)
and using the boundary condition (
44
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}v(y,\tau)dy=\int_{0}^{1}v_{0}(y)dy\leq C.

(66)

Choose
\xi
sufficiently small so that
0<\xi<\frac{k-1}{2k}
. Using (
63
), (
66
) and the one-dimensional Sobolev embedding, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&v(y,\tau)\leq\int_{0}^{1}vdy+\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{y}v|dy\leq C+C\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{y}\rho^{\alpha}|v^{\alpha+1}dy\\
&\leq C+C\left(\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{y}\rho^{\alpha}|^{2k}r^{2k}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2k}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}vr^{-\frac{2}{2\xi+1}}dy\right)^{\xi+\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}v^{(\alpha+\frac{1}{2}-\xi)\frac{2k}{k-1-2k\xi}}dy\right)^{\frac{k-1-2k\xi}{2k}}\\
&\leq C+C(T)\left(\int_{0}^{1}v^{(\alpha+\frac{1}{2}-\xi)\frac{2k}{k-1-2k\xi}-1+1}dy\right)^{\frac{k-1-2k\xi}{2k}}\\
&\leq C(T)V_{T}^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2k}}.\end{split}

(67)

Taking the supremum of (
67
) over
[0,1]\times[0,T]
and applying Young’s inequality together with (
59
), we obtain (
65
).
∎

3.4
Upper bound and positive lower bound of the density
(N=3)

We begin by stating a technical lemma.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
N=3
. For any
(\alpha,\gamma)
satisfying (
13
), there exist an
\alpha,\gamma
-dependent constant
k\in M_{\text{set}}
with
k>1.91
such that

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\sqrt{4k(4k^{2}-k-1)+1}-6k+3}{4k^{2}-8k+4}<\alpha<1-\frac{1}{2k}

(68)

and

\displaystyle 1<\gamma<6\alpha-3+\frac{3-5\alpha}{2k}.

(69)

Remark 3.2
.

The first inequality in (
68
) is equivalent to
k<n_{3}(\alpha)
.

Proof of the Lemma
3.3
.

One readily checks

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\sqrt{4n(4n^{2}-n-1)+1}-6n+3}{4n^{2}-8n+4}<1-\frac{1}{2n}\Leftarrow n>1.55.

(70)

Since
n_{3}(\alpha)
is strictly increasing on
(\frac{2}{3},1)
,
\alpha>0.686
implies
n_{3}(\alpha)>1.91
. Let
k_{0}=n_{3}(\alpha)
. Then (
70
) gives

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\sqrt{4k_{0}(4k_{0}^{2}-k_{0}-1)+1}-6k_{0}+3}{4k_{0}^{2}-8k_{0}+4}=\alpha<1-\frac{1}{2k_{0}}

and (
13
) gives

\displaystyle 1<\gamma<6\alpha-3+\frac{3-5\alpha}{2k_{0}}.

By the continuity of the functions and the density of
M_{\mathrm{set}}
in
(1,\infty)
, we can choose
k\in M_{\mathrm{set}}
with
k<k_{0}
and
k_{0}-k
sufficiently small so that (
68
) and (
69
) hold.
∎

Proposition 3.8
.

Assume that (
13
) holds. Let
k
be as defined in Lemma
3.3
. Then there exists a constant
0\leq\sigma<(3\alpha-2)(1-\frac{1}{k})
such that

\displaystyle\alpha-\frac{\alpha}{2k}+\sigma<\gamma<3\alpha-1+\frac{\alpha-1}{2k}+\sigma.

(71)

Furthermore, it holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho u^{2k}r^{2}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\Big(\rho^{\alpha}u^{2k}+\rho^{\alpha}(\partial_{r}u)^{2}u^{2k-2}r^{2}\Big)drdt\leq C(T)R_{T}^{2k\sigma}.

(72)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2k}}|^{2k}r^{2}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2k}}|^{2k}r^{2}drdt\leq C(T)R_{T}^{2k\sigma}.

(73)

Proof.

It is readily verified that a constant
\sigma
satisfying the required condition exists, thanks to (
69
).

Next, we prove (
72
). We use Proposition
3.1
, (
58
) and (
71
) to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho u^{2k}r^{2}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\Big(\rho^{\alpha}u^{2k}+\rho^{\alpha}(\partial_{r}u)^{2}u^{2k-2}r^{2}\Big)drdt\\
&\leq C+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{2k(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha}r^{2k}drdt\\
&\leq C+C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\xi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}^{\frac{2k(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha-2k\sigma}{\alpha-1/2}}R_{T}^{2k\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}r^{2k-\frac{2k(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha-2k\sigma}{\alpha-1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\xi\right)}drdt\\
&\leq C(T)R_{T}^{2k\sigma},\end{split}

where
\xi>0
is a sufficiently small constant satisfying

\displaystyle 2k-\frac{2k(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha-2k\sigma}{\alpha-1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\xi\right)>-1.

Such a
\xi
exists because (
71
). We thus obtain (
72
).

Next we (
73
). Invoking (
58
) and (
72
), one derives from (
53
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2k}}|^{2k}r^{2}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2k}}|^{2k}r^{2}drdt\\
&\leq C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho u^{2k}r^{2}dr+C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho(u+\rho^{-1}\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha})^{2k}r^{2}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2k}}|^{2k}r^{2}drdt\\
&\leq C(T)R_{T}^{2k\sigma}+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\gamma-\alpha+1}u^{2k}r^{2}drdt\\
&\leq C(T)R_{T}^{2k\sigma}+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\alpha}u^{2k}\rho^{\gamma-2\alpha+1}r^{2}drdt\\
&\leq C(T)R_{T}^{2k\sigma}+C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\xi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}^{\frac{\gamma-2\alpha+1}{\alpha-1/2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\alpha}u^{2k}drdt\\
&\leq C(T)R_{T}^{2k\sigma},\end{split}

where we have used

\displaystyle\gamma-2\alpha+1>0,

and
\xi
is taken sufficiently small so that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\gamma-2\alpha+1}{\alpha-1/2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}+\xi\right)<2.

Such a
\xi
exists because
\gamma<6\alpha-3
. This completes the proof of Proposition
3.8
.
∎

We now establish the upper and positive lower bounds for the density in three dimensions.

Proposition 3.9
.

Assume that (
13
) holds. There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

\displaystyle R_{T}\leq C(T).

(74)

Proof.

Choose
\beta
such that

\sigma<\beta<(3\alpha-2)\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{k}\Bigr),

where
\sigma
is given in Proposition
3.8
.
In view of (
68
), one readily verifies that
\beta
satisfies

\displaystyle\max\Bigl\{0,\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2k}\Bigr\}<\beta<\min\Bigl\{2\alpha-1,(3\alpha-2)\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{k}\Bigr)\Bigr\}.

(75)

Therefore, combining the one-dimensional Sobolev embedding with (
57
), (
73
) and (
75
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\rho^{\beta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}\leq C\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\beta}dr+C\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\beta}|dr\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{R}(\rho^{6\alpha-3}r^{2})^{\frac{\beta}{6\alpha-3}}r^{-\frac{2\beta}{6\alpha-3}}dr+C\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2k}}|\rho^{\beta-\alpha+1-\frac{1}{2k}}dr\\
&\leq C\left(\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{6\alpha-3}r^{2}dr\right)^{\frac{\beta}{6\alpha-3}}\left(\int_{0}^{R}r^{-\frac{2\beta}{6\alpha-\beta-3}}dr\right)^{\frac{6\alpha-\beta-3}{6\alpha-3}}\\
&\quad+C\left(\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2k}}|^{2k}r^{2}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{2k}}\left(\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\frac{2k}{2k-1}(\beta-\alpha)+1}r^{-\frac{2}{2k-1}}dr\right)^{\frac{2k-1}{2k}}\\
&\leq C+C(T)R_{T}^{\sigma}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\xi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{\beta-\alpha+\frac{2k-1}{2k}}{\alpha-1/2}}\left(\int_{0}^{R}r^{-\frac{2}{2k-1}-\left(\frac{1}{2}+\xi\right)\frac{\frac{2k}{2k-1}(\beta-\alpha)+1}{\alpha-1/2}}dr\right)^{\frac{2k-1}{2k}}\\
&\leq C(T)R_{T}^{\sigma},\end{split}

where
\xi
is taken sufficiently small so that

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{2k-1}-\left(\frac{1}{2}+\xi\right)\frac{\frac{2k}{2k-1}(\beta-\alpha)+1}{\alpha-1/2}>-1.

Such a
\xi
exists by virtue of (
75
). Applying Young’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle R_{T}^{\beta}\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{T}^{\beta}+C(T),

which establishes (
74
).
∎

Proposition 3.10
.

Assume that (
13
) holds. There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

\displaystyle V_{T}\leq C(T).

(76)

Proof.

Let
v(y,\tau)=\frac{1}{\rho(y,\tau)}
. By
\eqref{Equ 3}_{1}
, we know that
v
satisfies the equation
v_{\tau}=(r^{2}u)_{y}
. Integrating this equation over
(0,1)\times(0,\tau)
and using the boundary condition (
44
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}v(y,\tau)dy=\int_{0}^{1}v_{0}(y)dy\leq C.

(77)

Choose
\xi
sufficiently small so that
0<\xi<\frac{2k-3}{4k}
. Using (
73
), (
77
) and the one-dimensional Sobolev embedding, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&v(y,\tau)\leq\int_{0}^{1}vdy+\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{y}v|dy\leq C+C\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{y}\rho^{\alpha}|v^{\alpha+1}dy\\
&\leq C+C\left(\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{y}\rho^{\alpha}|^{2k}r^{4k}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2k}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}vr^{-\frac{3}{\xi+1}}dy\right)^{\frac{2+2\xi}{3}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}v^{(\alpha+\frac{1}{3}-\frac{2\xi}{3})\frac{6k}{2k-3-4k\xi}}dy\right)^{\frac{2k-3-4k\xi}{6k}}\\
&\leq C+C(T)\left(\int_{0}^{1}v^{(\alpha+\frac{1}{3}-\frac{2\xi}{3})\frac{6k}{2k-3-4k\xi}-1+1}dy\right)^{\frac{2k-3-4k\xi}{6k}}\\
&\leq C(T)V_{T}^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2k}}.\end{split}

(78)

Taking the supremum of (
78
) over
[0,1]\times(0,T)
and applying Young’s inequality together with (
68
), we obtain (
76
).
∎

3.5
Uniform upper bound of the density
(N=2,3)

We first show that the
L^{4}
-integrability of the velocity field can be established independently of time.

Proposition 3.11
.

Assume that (
16
) or (
17
) holds. Then there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
T
such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}u^{4}dy+\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\rho^{\alpha-1}\frac{u^{4}}{r^{2}}+\rho^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}u)^{2}u^{2}r^{2(N-1)}\Big)dyd\tau\leq C.

(79)

Proof.

Multiplying
\eqref{Equ 3}_{2}
by
r^{N-1}u^{3}
and integrating the resulting equation over
(0,1)
, we obtain from (
47
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{4}\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{0}^{1}u^{4}dy+\int_{0}^{1}\Big((\alpha(N-1)^{2}-(N-1)(N-2))\rho^{\alpha-1}\frac{u^{4}}{r^{2}}\\
&\quad+3\alpha\rho^{1+\alpha}{(\partial_{y}u)}^{2}u^{2}r^{2(N-1)}+4(N-1)(\alpha-1)\rho^{\alpha}u^{3}\partial_{y}ur^{N-2}\Big)dy\\
&=\int_{0}^{1}\Big(3\rho^{\gamma}u^{2}\partial_{y}ur^{N-1}+(N-1)\rho^{\gamma-1}\frac{u^{3}}{r}\Big)dy.\end{split}

(80)

Observing that
2<n_{N}(\alpha)
, we apply Young’s inequality to obtain a universal constant
\varepsilon>0
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{0}^{1}u^{4}dy+2\varepsilon\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\rho^{\alpha-1}\frac{u^{4}}{r^{2}}+\rho^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}u)^{2}u^{2}r^{2(N-1)}\Big)dy\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\rho^{\gamma}u^{2}|\partial_{y}u|r^{N-1}dy+C\int_{0}^{1}\rho^{\gamma-1}\frac{|u|^{3}}{r}dy\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\left(\rho^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}u)^{2}u^{2}r^{2(N-1)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\rho^{2\gamma-\alpha-1}u^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}dy+C\int_{0}^{1}\left(\rho^{\alpha-1}\frac{u^{4}}{r^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\rho^{2\gamma-\alpha-1}u^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}dy\\
&\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{1}\rho^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}u)^{2}u^{2}r^{2(N-1)}dy+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{1}\rho^{\alpha-1}\frac{u^{4}}{r^{2}}dy+C\int_{0}^{1}\rho^{2\gamma-\alpha-1}u^{2}dy.\end{split}

When
N=2
, using (
56
), we obtain, for sufficiently small
\xi
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad C\int_{0}^{1}\rho^{2\gamma-\alpha-1}u^{2}dy=C\int_{0}^{1}\rho^{\alpha-1}\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}\rho^{2\gamma-2\alpha}r^{2}dy\\
&\leq C\left\|\rho^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\xi}\right\|^{\frac{2(\gamma-\alpha)}{\alpha-1/2}}_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}\int_{0}^{1}\rho^{\alpha-1}\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}dy\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\rho^{\alpha-1}\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}dy,\end{split}

where we used

\displaystyle\frac{2(\gamma-\alpha)}{\alpha-1/2}\geq 0\Leftrightarrow\gamma\geq\alpha.

When
N=3
, we use (
58
) to get that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad C\int_{0}^{1}\rho^{2\gamma-\alpha-1}u^{2}dy=C\int_{0}^{1}\rho^{\alpha-1}\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}\rho^{2\gamma-2\alpha}r^{2}dy\\
&\leq C\left\|\rho^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\xi}\right\|^{\frac{2(\gamma-\alpha)}{\alpha-1/2}}_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}\int_{0}^{1}\rho^{\alpha-1}\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}dy\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\rho^{\alpha-1}\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}dy,\end{split}

where we have used
\gamma<3\alpha-1
to choose a sufficiently small
\xi
such that

\displaystyle 0\leq\left(\frac{1}{2}+\xi\right)\frac{2(\gamma-\alpha)}{\alpha-1/2}\leq 2.

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{0}^{1}u^{4}dy+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\rho^{\alpha-1}\frac{u^{4}}{r^{2}}+\rho^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}u)^{2}u^{2}r^{2(N-1)}\Big)dy\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\rho^{\alpha-1}\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}dy.

Integrating the above inequality with respect to
\tau
and invoking (
45
) yields (
79
).
∎

Proposition 3.12
.

Assume that (
16
) or (
17
) holds. Then there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
T
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho u^{4}r^{N-1}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\Big(\rho^{\alpha}u^{4}r^{N-3}+\rho^{\alpha}(\partial_{r}u)^{2}u^{2}r^{N-1}\Big)drdt\leq C

(81)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{4\alpha-7}|\partial_{r}\rho|^{4}r^{N-1}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\gamma+3\alpha-7}|\partial_{r}\rho|^{4}r^{N-1}drdt\leq C.

(82)

Proof.

Rewriting (
79
) in Eulerian coordinates gives (
81
).

We next prove (
82
). Noting that
2\in M_{\mathrm{set}}
, so we can apply Proposition
3.2
to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{4\alpha-7}|\partial_{r}\rho|^{4}r^{N-1}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\gamma+3\alpha-7}|\partial_{r}\rho|^{4}r^{N-1}drdt\\
&\leq C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho u^{4}r^{N-1}dr+C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho(u+\rho^{-1}\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha})^{4}r^{N-1}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\gamma+3\alpha-7}|\partial_{r}\rho|^{4}r^{N-1}drdt\\
&\leq C+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\gamma-\alpha+1}u^{4}r^{N-1}drdt\\
&\leq C+C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho^{\gamma-2\alpha+1}r^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\alpha}u^{4}r^{N-3}drdt\\
&\leq C+C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho^{\gamma-2\alpha+1}r^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)},\end{split}

where we used (
81
) and

\displaystyle\gamma\geq 2\alpha-1.

Therefore, it suffices to obtain (
82
) by estimating
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho^{\gamma-2\alpha+1}r^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}
.

When
N=2
, using (
56
), we obtain,

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho^{\gamma-2\alpha+1}r^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}\leq C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\xi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}^{\frac{\gamma-2\alpha+1}{\alpha-1/2}}\leq C,

where
\xi
is taken sufficiently small so that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\gamma-2\alpha+1}{\alpha-1/2}\right)\xi\leq 2.

When
N=3
, we use (
58
) to obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho^{\gamma-2\alpha+1}r^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}\leq C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\xi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}^{\frac{\gamma-2\alpha+1}{\alpha-1/2}}\leq C,

where we have used
\gamma<6\alpha-3
to choose a sufficiently small
\xi
such that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}+\xi\right)\frac{\gamma-2\alpha+1}{\alpha-1/2}\leq 2.

This completes the proof of Proposition
3.12
.
∎

Next, we devote ourselves to obtaining a uniform upper bound for the density.

Proposition 3.13
.

Assume that (
16
) or (
17
) holds. Then there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
T
such that

\displaystyle R_{T}\leq C.

(83)

Proof.

When
N=2
, using (
55
) and (
82
), we obtain from the one-dimensional Sobolev embedding that

\displaystyle\left\|\rho^{\alpha}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}\leq C\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\alpha}dr+C\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha}|dr

\displaystyle\leq C+C\left(\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{4\alpha-7}|\partial_{r}\rho|^{4}rdr\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\int_{0}^{R}\rho r^{-\frac{1}{3}}dr\right)^{\frac{3}{4}}\leq C+C\int_{0}^{R}\rho r^{-\frac{1}{3}}dr

\displaystyle\leq C+C\left(\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{4}rdr\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\int_{0}^{R}r^{-\frac{7}{9}}dr\right)^{\frac{3}{4}}\leq C.

When
N=3
, we choose
\beta
satisfying

\displaystyle\max\Bigl\{0,\alpha-\frac{3}{4}\Bigr\}<\beta<\frac{3}{2}\alpha-1.

(84)

Combining the one-dimensional Sobolev embedding with (
57
), (
58
) and (
82
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\rho^{\beta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}\leq C\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\beta}dr+C\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\beta}|dr\\
&\leq C+C\left(\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{4\alpha-7}|\partial_{r}\rho|^{4}r^{2}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\int_{0}^{R}\rho^{\frac{4}{3}(\beta-\alpha)+1}r^{-\frac{2}{3}}dr\right)^{\frac{3}{4}}\\
&\leq C+C\left\|\rho^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\xi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}^{\frac{\frac{4}{3}(\beta-\alpha)+1}{\alpha-1/2}}\int_{0}^{R}r^{-\frac{2}{3}-\left(\frac{1}{2}+\xi\right)\frac{\frac{4}{3}(\beta-\alpha)+1}{\alpha-1/2}}dr\\
&\leq C,\end{split}

where we have used (
84
) to choose a sufficiently small
\xi>0
such that

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{3}-\left(\frac{1}{2}+\xi\right)\frac{\frac{4}{3}(\beta-\alpha)+1}{\alpha-1/2}>-1.

This completes the proof of Proposition
3.13
.
∎

Under the hypotheses of Theorem
2.2
we have thus established a uniform upper bound for the density on
\Omega\times[0,T]
. In two dimensions, (
12
) implies (
16
), so the positive lower bound follows directly from Proposition
3.7
under assumption (
16
).
In three dimensions, however, (
13
) alone does not guarantee (
17
). Consequently, an additional statement is required to treat the positive lower bound of density under assumption (
17
), and we provide it next.

Proposition 3.14
.

Assume that (
17
) holds. There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

\displaystyle V_{T}\leq C(T).

(85)

Proof.

For brevity, we only outline the proof. As stated in Lemma
3.3
, for
\alpha\in(0.689,1)
we can choose
k\in M_{set}
satisfying (
68
). Applying Proposition
3.1
and (
83
) to this
k
, we obtain a
T
-dependent bound on
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho u^{2k}r^{2}\mathrm{d}r
, which in turn guarantees the boundedness of
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2k}}|^{2k}r^{2}\mathrm{d}r
by an argument similar to the one used for (
82
). Once the estimate for the density derivative is available, repeating the proof of Proposition
3.10
yields (
85
).
∎

3.6
Higher-order estimates

In this subsection we carry out higher-order estimates for the local classical solution
(\rho,\mathbf{u})
. Throughout this subsection we always assume
N=2
or
3
. For brevity, we denote the standard Lebesgue and Sobolev spaces as follows:

\displaystyle L^{p}=L^{p}(\Omega),\quad W^{k,p}=W^{k,p}(\Omega),\quad H^{k}=W^{k,2}(\Omega).

Proposition 3.15
.

Let
N=2
with
l>1
, or
N=3
with
1.5<l<3
, and assume that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\Big(\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2l}}&+\left\|\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}\Big)\\
&+\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2l}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)dt\leq M.\end{split}

(86)

Then there exists a constant
C(M)>0
, independent of time
T
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho|\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dxdt\leq C(M).

(87)

Proof.

Note that in the radially symmetric setting,
\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u}=\nabla\mathbf{u}
. Therefore, we rewrite
\eqref{0}_{2}
as

\displaystyle\rho\dot{\mathbf{u}}+\nabla P=\div\left(\mu(\rho)\nabla\mathbf{u}\right)+\nabla(\lambda(\rho)\div\mathbf{u})

(88)

Multiplying the above equation by
\dot{\mathbf{u}}
and integrating the resulting equation over
\Omega
by parts gives

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\rho|\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx=-\int_{\Omega}\nabla P\cdot\dot{\mathbf{u}}dx-\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)\nabla\mathbf{u}:\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}dx-\int_{\Omega}\lambda(\rho)\div\mathbf{u}\div\dot{\mathbf{u}}dx:=\sum_{i=1}^{3}I_{i}.

(89)

Hölder’s inequality implies

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{1}&=-\int_{\Omega}\nabla P\cdot\dot{\mathbf{u}}dx\leq C(M)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\rho|\sqrt{\rho}|\dot{\mathbf{u}}|dx\\
&\leq\frac{1}{6}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(M)\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.\end{split}

(90)

From (
88
) we know that
\mathbf{u}
satisfies the elliptic system:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Delta\mathbf{u}+(\alpha-1)\nabla\div\mathbf{u}=\frac{1}{\rho^{\alpha}}(\rho\dot{\mathbf{u}}+\nabla P-\nabla\mu(\rho)\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u}-\nabla\lambda(\rho)\div\mathbf{u}),\\
\mathbf{u}|_{\partial\Omega}=0.\end{array}\right.

Standard
L^{p}
estimates for elliptic systems and (
86
) imply

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(M)(\left\|\rho\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\||\nabla\rho||\nabla\mathbf{u}|\right\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\leq C(M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2l}{l-1}}})

\displaystyle\leq C(M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\frac{N}{2l}}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{\frac{N}{2l}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{2}}+C(M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}),

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{2}}\leq C(M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}).

(93)

Using (
86
), (
93
) and Young’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&I_{2}=-\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)\nabla\mathbf{u}:\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}dx-\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)\nabla\mathbf{u}:\nabla(\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u})dx\\
&=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\mu(\rho)|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx-\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)\nabla\mathbf{u}:\nabla(\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u})dx\\
&=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\div(\mu(\rho)\mathbf{u})|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx\\
&\quad-\frac{\alpha-1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)\div\mathbf{u}|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx-\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)\nabla\mathbf{u}:\nabla(\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u})dx\\
&\leq-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx+C(M)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\rho||\mathbf{u}||\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx+C(M)\int_{\Omega}|\mathbf{u}||\nabla\mathbf{u}||\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}|dx\\
&\leq-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx+C(M)\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2l}}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2l}{l-1}}}^{2}+C(M)\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2l}}\left\|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2l}{l-1}}}\\
&\leq-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx+C(M)\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2l}{l-1}}}^{2}+C(M)\left\|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2l}{l-1}}}\\
&\leq-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx+C(M)\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2l-N}{l}}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{\frac{N}{l}}+C(M)\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2l-N}{2l}}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{\frac{2l+N}{2l}}\\
&\leq-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx+\frac{1}{6}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(M)\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(M)\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.\end{split}

(94)

Similarly, estimating
I_{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&I_{3}=-\int_{\Omega}\lambda(\rho)\div\mathbf{u}\div\mathbf{u}_{t}dx-\int_{\Omega}\lambda(\rho)\div\mathbf{u}\div(\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u})dx\\
&=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\lambda(\rho)(\div\mathbf{u})^{2}dx+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\lambda(\rho)(\div\mathbf{u})^{2}dx-\int_{\Omega}\lambda(\rho)\div\mathbf{u}\div(\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u})dx\\
&=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\lambda(\rho)(\div\mathbf{u})^{2}dx-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\div(\lambda(\rho)\mathbf{u})(\div\mathbf{u})^{2}dx\\
&\quad-\frac{(\alpha-1)}{2}\int_{\Omega}\lambda(\rho)(\div\mathbf{u})^{3}dx-\int_{\Omega}\lambda(\rho)\div\mathbf{u}\div(\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u})dx\\
&\leq-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\lambda(\rho)(\div\mathbf{u})^{2}dx+C(M)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\rho||\mathbf{u}||\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx+C(M)\int_{\Omega}|\mathbf{u}||\nabla\mathbf{u}||\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}|dx\\
&\leq-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\lambda(\rho)(\div\mathbf{u})^{2}dx+\frac{1}{6}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(M)\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(M)\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.\end{split}

(95)

Inserting (
90
), (
94
) and (
95
) into (
89
) gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}(\mu(\rho)|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}+\lambda(\rho)(\div\mathbf{u})^{2})dx+\int_{\Omega}\rho|\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx\leq C(M)(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).\end{split}

(96)

Integrating the above equality over
(0,t)
and using (
86
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\mu(\rho)|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}+\lambda(\rho)(\div\mathbf{u})^{2})dx+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\rho|\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dxdt\leq C(M).

(97)

Note that
\frac{N-1}{N}<\alpha<1
gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\int_{\Omega}(\mu(\rho)|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}+\lambda(\rho)(\div\mathbf{u})^{2})dx\\
&=\int_{\Omega}\Big(\rho^{\alpha}|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}+(\alpha-1)\rho^{\alpha}(\div\mathbf{u})^{2}\Big)dx\\
&\geq\int_{\Omega}(1+(\alpha-1)N)\rho^{\alpha}|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx\\
&\geq\frac{1+(\alpha-1)N}{M^{\alpha}}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx,\end{split}

which, combined with (
97
), shows (
87
). This completes the proof of Proposition
3.15
.
∎

Proposition 3.16
.

Assume that (
86
) holds. Then there exists a constant
C(M)>0
, independent of
T
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{2}}\right)+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dxdt\leq C(M).

(98)

Proof.

We use the method of Hoff in
[
22
]
to prove (
98
). Operating
\dot{u}^{j}[\partial/\partial t+\div(\mathbf{u}\cdot)]
to
\eqref{hh3}^{j}
, summing over
j
, integrating the resulting equation over
\Omega
and using integration by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(\dfrac{1}{2}\int\rho|\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx\right)_{t}\\
&=-\int_{\Omega}\dot{u}^{j}[\partial_{j}P_{t}+\div(\mathbf{u}\partial_{j}P)]dx+\int_{\Omega}\dot{u}_{j}[\partial_{it}(\mu(\rho)\partial_{i}u_{j})+\div(\mathbf{u}\partial_{i}(\mu(\rho)\partial_{i}u_{j}))]dx\\
&\quad+\int_{\Omega}\dot{u}^{j}[\partial_{jt}(\lambda(\rho)\div\mathbf{u})+\div(\mathbf{u}\partial_{j}(\lambda(\rho)\div\mathbf{u}))]dx:=\sum_{i=1}^{3}N_{i}.\end{split}

(99)

Using equation
\eqref{0}_{1}
, we obtain by integration by parts that

\displaystyle\begin{split}N_{1}&=-\int_{\Omega}\dot{u}^{j}[\partial_{j}P_{t}+\div(\partial_{j}P\mathbf{u})]dx\\
&=\int_{\Omega}\left[-P^{\prime}\rho\div\mathbf{u}\partial_{j}\dot{u}^{j}+\partial_{k}(\partial_{j}\dot{u}^{j}u^{k})P-P\partial_{j}(\partial_{k}\dot{u}^{j}u^{k})\right]dx\\
&\leq C(M)\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\leq\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon,M)\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2},\end{split}

(100)

where
\varepsilon>0
is a sufficiently small positive constant to be determined. From (
86
) and equation
\eqref{0}_{1}
, we obtain by integration by parts that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&N_{2}=\int_{\Omega}\dot{u}_{j}[\partial_{it}(\mu(\rho)\partial_{i}u_{j})+\div(\mathbf{u}\partial_{i}(\mu(\rho)\partial_{i}u_{j}))]dx\\
&=-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{i}\dot{u}_{j}\partial_{t}(\mu(\rho)\partial_{i}u_{j})dx-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{k}\dot{u}_{j}u_{k}\partial_{i}(\mu(\rho)\partial_{i}u_{j})dx\\
&=-\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx+\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)\partial_{i}\dot{u}_{j}\partial_{i}(u_{k}\partial_{k}u_{j})dx-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{i}\dot{u}_{j}\partial_{t}\mu(\rho)\partial_{i}u_{j}dx\\
&\quad-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{k}\dot{u}_{j}u_{k}\partial_{i}\mu(\rho)\partial_{i}u_{j}dx-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{k}\dot{u}_{j}u_{k}\mu(\rho)\partial_{ii}u_{j}dx\\
&\leq-\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx+C(M)\int_{\Omega}\left(|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}||\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}+|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}||\mathbf{u}||\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}|+|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}||\mathbf{u}||\nabla\rho||\nabla\mathbf{u}|\right)dx\\
&\leq-\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx+\varepsilon\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx+C(\varepsilon,M)\int_{\Omega}\left(|\mathbf{u}|^{2}|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}|^{2}+|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{4}+|\nabla\rho|^{2}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}\right)dx.\end{split}

(101)

Using standard
L^{p}
estimates for elliptic systems, we obtain from
(
3.6
)
and (
86
) that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{W^{2,{2l}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(M)(\left\|\rho\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2l}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}+\left\||\nabla\rho||\nabla\mathbf{u}|\right\|_{L^{2l}})

\displaystyle\leq C(M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2l}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}})

\displaystyle\leq C(M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2l}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2l-N}{Nl+2l-N}}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{W^{1,{2l}}}^{\frac{Nl}{Nl+2l-N}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{W^{2,{2l}}}+C(M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2l}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}})

which implies that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{W^{2,{2l}}}\leq C(M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2l}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}).

(102)

Observing that
\mathbf{u}|_{\partial\Omega}=0
implies
\|\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\frac{2l}{l-1}}}\leq C\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(M)
, we thus obtain from (
102
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}C(\varepsilon,M)\int_{\Omega}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx&\leq C(\varepsilon,M)\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2l}{l-1}}}^{2}\left\|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2}\leq C(\varepsilon,M)\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2l}{l-1}}}^{2}\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{W^{2,2l}}^{2}\\
&\leq C(\varepsilon,M)\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2l}{l-1}}}^{2}(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\\
&\leq C(\varepsilon,M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{N+2l-Nl}{l}}\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{Nl-N}{l}}+\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2l}{l-1}}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\\
&\leq\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon,M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2l}+\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2l}{l-1}}}^{\frac{2l}{l-1}}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\\
&\leq\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon,M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2l}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}),\end{split}

(103)

where the last inequality follows from
\frac{2l}{l-1}>2
and (
87
). We use (
93
), (
87
) and (
86
) to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}C(\varepsilon,M)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{4}dx&=C(\varepsilon,M)\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\leq C(\varepsilon,M)\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{4}\\
&\leq C(\varepsilon,M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4})\\
&\leq C(\varepsilon,M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).\end{split}

(104)

Arguing as in the estimate for (
103
), one has

\displaystyle\begin{split}C(\varepsilon,M)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\rho|^{2}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx&\leq C(\varepsilon,M)\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2}\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2l}{l-1}}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\\
&\leq C(\varepsilon,M)\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2l}{l-1}}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{W^{1,{2l}}}^{2}\\
&\leq\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\varepsilon,M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2l}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).\end{split}

(105)

Inserting (
103
), (
104
) and (
105
) into (
101
) shows that

\displaystyle\begin{split}N_{2}\leq-\int_{\Omega}\mu(\rho)|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx&+3\varepsilon\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx+C(\varepsilon,M)\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\\
&+C(\varepsilon,M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2l}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).\end{split}

(106)

Similarly, estimating
N_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&N_{3}=\int_{\Omega}\dot{u}^{j}[\partial_{jt}(\lambda(\rho)\div\mathbf{u})+\div(\mathbf{u}\partial_{j}(\lambda(\rho)\div\mathbf{u}))]dx\\
&=-\int_{\Omega}\div\dot{\mathbf{u}}\partial_{t}(\lambda(\rho)\div\mathbf{u})dx-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{i}\dot{u}_{j}u_{i}\partial_{j}(\lambda(\rho)\partial_{k}u_{k})dx\\
&=-\int_{\Omega}\lambda(\rho)(\div\dot{\mathbf{u}})^{2}dx+\int_{\Omega}\lambda(\rho)\div\dot{\mathbf{u}}\div(\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u})dx-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\lambda(\rho)\div\dot{\mathbf{u}}\div\mathbf{u}dx\\
&\quad-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{i}\dot{u}_{j}u_{i}\partial_{j}(\lambda(\rho)\partial_{k}u_{k})dx\\
&\leq-\int_{\Omega}\lambda(\rho)(\div\dot{\mathbf{u}})^{2}dx+C(M)\int_{\Omega}\left(|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}||\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}+|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}||\mathbf{u}||\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}|+|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}||\mathbf{u}||\nabla\rho||\nabla\mathbf{u}|\right)dx\\
&\leq-\int_{\Omega}\lambda(\rho)(\div\dot{\mathbf{u}})^{2}dx+\varepsilon\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx+C(\varepsilon,M)\int_{\Omega}\left(|\mathbf{u}|^{2}|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}|^{2}+|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{4}+|\nabla\rho|^{2}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}\right)dx.\end{split}

(107)

Inserting (
103
), (
104
) and (
105
) into (
107
) gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}N_{3}\leq-\int_{\Omega}\lambda(\rho)(\div\dot{\mathbf{u}})^{2}dx&+3\varepsilon\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx+C(\varepsilon,M)\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\\
&+C(\varepsilon,M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2l}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).\end{split}

(108)

Using (
100
), (
106
) and (
108
), we obtain from (
99
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\rho|\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx+\int_{\Omega}(\mu(\rho)|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}+\lambda(\rho)(\div\dot{\mathbf{u}})^{2})dx-7\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\leq C(\varepsilon,M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2l}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2})+C(\varepsilon,M)\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}.\end{split}

Therefore, one has

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\rho|\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx+\left(\frac{1+(\alpha-1)N}{M^{\alpha}}-7\varepsilon\right)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx\\
&\leq C(\varepsilon,M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2l}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2})+C(\varepsilon,M)\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}.\end{split}

Choose
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small so that
7\varepsilon<\frac{1+(\alpha-1)N}{2M^{\alpha}}
. Then we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\rho|\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx+\frac{1+(\alpha-1)N}{2M^{\alpha}}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx\\
&\leq C(M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2l}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2})+C(M)\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4}.\end{split}

(109)

Using Gronwall’s inequality together with (
87
), (
86
) and (
93
), we obtain (
98
).
∎

Proposition 3.17
.

Assume that (
86
) holds. Then there exists a constant
C(M,T)>0
depending on
M
and
T
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho\right\|_{H^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{3}}^{2}dt\leq C(M,T).

(110)

Proof.

Operating
\partial_{i}\partial_{j}
to equation
\eqref{0}_{1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{t}(\partial_{ij}\rho)&+\partial_{ij}u_{k}\partial_{k}\rho+\partial_{i}u_{k}\partial_{jk}\rho+\partial_{j}u_{k}\partial_{ik}\rho+u_{k}\partial_{ijk}\rho\\
&+\partial_{ij}\rho\partial_{k}u_{k}+\partial_{i}\rho\partial_{jk}u_{k}+\partial_{j}\rho\partial_{ik}u_{k}+\rho\partial_{ijk}u_{k}=0.\end{split}

Multiplying the above equation by
\partial_{ij}\rho
, summing over
i,j
, and integrating the resulting equation over
\Omega
, we use integration by parts to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\rho|^{2}dx\leq C\left(\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\rho||\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}||\nabla^{2}\rho|dx+\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\mathbf{u}||\nabla^{2}\rho|^{2}dx+\int_{\Omega}\rho|\nabla^{3}\mathbf{u}||\nabla^{2}\rho|dx\right)\\
&\leq C(M)(1+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}})\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\rho|^{2}dx+C(M)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\rho|^{2}|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx+C(M)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{3}\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx\\
&\leq C(M)(1+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}})\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\rho|^{2}dx+C(M)\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2}\left\|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2l}{l-1}}}^{2}+C(M)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{3}\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx\\
&\leq C(M)(1+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}})\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\rho|^{2}dx+C(M)+C(M)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{3}\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx,\end{split}

(111)

where the last inequality follows from (
86
) and (
98
). We obtain from (
102
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}&\leq C\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{W^{1,{2l}}}\leq C(M)(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2l}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}})\\
&\leq C(M)+C(M)\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

(112)

Using the elliptic system (
3.6
) once more, we obtain from (
86
), (
98
) and (
112
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{3}}^{2}&\leq C\left\|\frac{1}{\rho^{\alpha}}(\rho\dot{\mathbf{u}}+\nabla P-\nabla\mu(\rho)\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u}-\nabla\lambda(\rho)\div\mathbf{u})\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\\
&\leq C(M)\left(1+\left\||\nabla\rho||\dot{\mathbf{u}}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla^{2}\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\left\||\nabla\rho|^{2}|\nabla\mathbf{u}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right.\\
&\left.\quad+\left\||\nabla^{2}\rho||\nabla\mathbf{u}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\nabla\rho||\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\\
&\leq C(M)\left(1+\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\left(1+\left\|\nabla^{2}\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+C(M)\left\|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2l}{l-1}}}^{2}\\
&\leq C(M)\left(1+\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\left(1+\left\|\nabla^{2}\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+C(M)\left\|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2l-N}{l}}\left\|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{\frac{N}{l}}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{3}}^{2}+C(M)\left(1+\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\left(1+\left\|\nabla^{2}\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right),\end{split}

which implies

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{3}}^{2}\leq C(M)\left(1+\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\left(1+\left\|\nabla^{2}\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

(113)

Using (
112
) and (
113
), we obtain from (
111
) that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\rho|^{2}dx\leq C(M)\left(1+\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\left(1+\left\|\nabla^{2}\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

This, combined with Gronwall’s inequality, (
113
) and (
98
), gives (
110
).
∎

Proposition 3.18
.

Assume that (
86
) holds. Then there exists a constant
C(M,T)>0
depending on
M
and
T
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}(\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{3}})+\int_{0}^{T}(\left\|\mathbf{u}_{tt}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2})dt\leq C(M,T).

(114)

Proof.

Differentiating (
88
) with respect to
t
gives

\displaystyle-\Delta\mathbf{u}_{t}-(\alpha-1)\nabla\div\mathbf{u}_{t}=-\left[\frac{1}{\rho^{\alpha}}\left(\rho\dot{\mathbf{u}}+\nabla P-\nabla\mu(\rho)\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u}-\nabla\lambda(\rho)\div\mathbf{u}\right)\right]_{t}.

(115)

Multiplying by
\mathbf{u}_{tt}
and integrating the resulting equation over
\Omega
by parts gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\Big(|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}|^{2}+(\alpha-1)(\div\mathbf{u}_{t})^{2}\Big)dx+\int_{\Omega}\rho^{1-\alpha}|\mathbf{u}_{tt}|^{2}dx\\
&\leq C(M)\int_{\Omega}|\mathbf{u}_{tt}|\left(|\rho_{t}||\mathbf{u}_{t}|+|\rho_{t}||\mathbf{u}||\nabla\mathbf{u}|+|\mathbf{u}_{t}||\nabla\mathbf{u}|+|\mathbf{u}||\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}|+|\rho_{t}||\nabla\rho|+|\nabla\rho_{t}|\right.\\
&\quad\left.+|\rho_{t}||\nabla\rho||\nabla\mathbf{u}|+|\nabla\rho_{t}||\nabla\mathbf{u}|+|\nabla\rho||\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}|\right)dx\\
&\leq\varepsilon\int_{\Omega}|\mathbf{u}_{tt}|^{2}dx+C(\varepsilon,M)\int_{\Omega}\left(|\rho_{t}|^{2}|\mathbf{u}_{t}|^{2}+|\rho_{t}|^{2}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}+|\mathbf{u}_{t}|^{2}|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}+|\mathbf{u}|^{2}|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}|^{2}\right.\\
&\quad\left.+|\rho_{t}|^{2}|\nabla\rho|^{2}+|\nabla\rho_{t}|^{2}+|\rho_{t}|^{2}|\nabla\rho|^{2}|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}+|\nabla\rho_{t}|^{2}|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}+|\nabla\rho|^{2}|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}|^{2}\right)dx\\
&\leq\varepsilon\int_{\Omega}|\mathbf{u}_{tt}|^{2}dx+C(\varepsilon,M)\left(\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\right.\\
&\quad+\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\\
&\quad+\left.\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{3}}^{2}\right),\end{split}

(116)

where
\varepsilon>0
is a sufficiently small constant to be determined. Note that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|\nabla(\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(M);\\
\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|\nabla(\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(M).\end{split}

(117)

Using (
115
) and the boundary condition
\mathbf{u}_{t}|_{\partial\Omega}=0
, we obtain from the
L^{p}
estimate for the elliptic system that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\left[\frac{1}{\rho^{\alpha}}\left(\rho\dot{\mathbf{u}}+\nabla P-\nabla\mu\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u}-\nabla\lambda\div\mathbf{u}\right)\right]_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(M)\left(\left\|\rho_{t}\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{u}_{tt}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}|\mathbf{u}||\nabla\mathbf{u}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\mathbf{u}_{t}||\nabla\mathbf{u}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\mathbf{u}||\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\left\|\rho_{t}|\nabla\rho|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}|\nabla\rho||\nabla\mathbf{u}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\nabla\rho_{t}||\nabla\mathbf{u}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\nabla\rho||\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(M)(\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{u}_{tt}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\nabla\rho_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{3}}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq C(M,T)(1+\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{u}_{tt}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{3}}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq C(M,T)(1+\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{u}_{tt}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2})+C(M,T)\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{6-N}{3}}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{\frac{N}{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+C(M,T)(1+\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{u}_{tt}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}),

which shows that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\leq C(M,T)(1+\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{u}_{tt}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).

(118)

Therefore, we obtain from (
116
)–(
118
) that

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\Big(|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}|^{2}+(\alpha-1)(\div\mathbf{u}_{t})^{2}\Big)dx+\int_{\Omega}\rho^{1-\alpha}|\mathbf{u}_{tt}|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{\Omega}|\mathbf{u}_{tt}|^{2}dx+C(\varepsilon,M,T)(1+\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{3}}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{\Omega}|\mathbf{u}_{tt}|^{2}dx+C(\varepsilon,M,T)(1+\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{6-N}{3}}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{\frac{N}{3}})

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon\int_{\Omega}|\mathbf{u}_{tt}|^{2}dx+C(\varepsilon,M,T)+C(\varepsilon,M,T)\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Choosing
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small so that
2\varepsilon<\frac{M^{\alpha-1}}{2}
holds, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\Big(|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}|^{2}+(\alpha-1)(\div\mathbf{u}_{t})^{2}\Big)dx+\frac{M^{\alpha-1}}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\mathbf{u}_{tt}|^{2}dx\leq C(M,T)+C(M,T)\left\|\nabla\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Integrating with respect to time, we obtain (
114
) from (
98
), (
113
), (
117
) and (
118
).
∎

Proposition 3.19
.

Assume that (
86
) holds. Then there exists a constant
C(M,T)>0
depending on
M
and
T
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho\right\|_{H^{3}}+\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{4}}^{2}dt\leq C(M,T).

(119)

Proof.

Applying
\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\partial_{k}
to equation
\eqref{0}_{1}
, multiplying the resulting equation by
\partial_{ijk}\rho
, summing over
i,j,k
, and integrating the resulting equation over
\Omega
, we use integration by parts, (
110
), (
114
) and (
112
) to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{3}\rho|^{2}dx\leq C\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{3}\rho|\left(\rho|\nabla^{4}\mathbf{u}|+|\nabla\rho||\nabla^{3}\mathbf{u}|+|\nabla^{2}\rho||\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}|+|\nabla^{3}\rho||\nabla\mathbf{u}|\right)dx\\
&\leq C(M)(\left\|\nabla^{3}\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla^{4}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|\nabla^{3}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla^{2}\rho\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}\left\|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{6}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla^{3}\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2})\\
&\leq C(M,T)+C(M,T)\left\|\nabla^{3}\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(M,T)\left\|\nabla^{4}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.\end{split}

(120)

From the elliptic system (
3.6
), (
110
) and (
114
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{4}}^{2}\leq C\left\|\frac{1}{\rho^{\alpha}}(\rho\mathbf{u}_{t}+\rho\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u}+\nabla P-\nabla\mu\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u}-\nabla\lambda\div\mathbf{u})\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\\
&\leq C(M,T)+C(M)(\left\||\nabla^{2}\rho||\mathbf{u}_{t}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\nabla\rho||\nabla\mathbf{u}_{t}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\nabla\rho|^{2}|\mathbf{u}_{t}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\\
&\quad+C(M)(\left\||\nabla^{2}\rho||\mathbf{u}||\nabla\mathbf{u}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\nabla\rho||\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\mathbf{u}||\nabla^{3}\mathbf{u}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\nabla\rho|^{2}|\mathbf{u}||\nabla\mathbf{u}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\quad+\left\||\nabla\rho||\mathbf{u}||\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}||\nabla\mathbf{u}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2})+C(M)(\left\|\nabla^{3}\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\nabla^{2}\rho||\nabla\rho|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\nabla\rho|^{3}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\\
&\quad+C(M)(\left\||\nabla^{3}\mathbf{u}||\nabla\rho|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}||\nabla^{2}\rho|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}||\nabla\rho|^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\nabla\rho||\nabla^{2}\rho||\nabla\mathbf{u}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\quad+\left\||\nabla^{3}\rho||\nabla\mathbf{u}|\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\||\nabla\mathbf{u}||\nabla\rho|^{3}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\\
&\leq C(M,T)(1+\left\|\nabla^{3}\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2})\\
&\leq C(M,T)(1+\left\|\nabla^{3}\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{u}_{tt}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}),\end{split}

(121)

where the last inequality follows from (
118
). Inserting (
121
) into (
120
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{3}\rho|^{2}dx\leq C(M,T)(1+\left\|\nabla^{3}\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{u}_{tt}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}),

(122)

By Gronwall’s inequality, (
114
) and (
121
) imply (
119
).
∎

3.7
The proof of Theorem
2.1

Proposition 3.20
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.1
, there exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\left\|\rho\right\|_{H^{3}}+\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|\rho_{tt}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)+\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\rho_{tt}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}dt\leq C(T);\\
&\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{3}}+\left\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{H^{1}}\right)+\int_{0}^{T}(\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{4}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{u}_{tt}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2})dt\leq C(T).\end{split}

(123)

Proof.

By Proposition
3.6
,
3.9
,
3.7
and
3.10
, we obtain that there exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(T).

(124)

For
N=2
, set
l=k
, where
k
is as defined in (
59
). Then, by Proposition
3.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho^{\frac{1}{2l}}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2l}}+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2l}}\right\|_{L^{2l}}+\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2l}}\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2l}dt\leq C(T).

(125)

Therefore, (
124
), (
45
), (
52
), (
125
) together imply

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\Big(\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2l}}&+\left\|\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}\Big)\\
&+\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2l}}^{2l}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)dt\leq C(T).\end{split}

(126)

For
N=3
, choose any
l
satisfying
1.5<l<\min\{3,k\}
, where
k
is as defined in (
68
). Therefore, (
124
), (
45
), (
52
), (
72
), (
73
) together imply (
126
).

Therefore, by Proposition
3.15
-Proposition
3.19
, we obtain (
123
) except for the estimates of
\rho_{t}
and
\rho_{tt}
. Indeed, from equation
\eqref{0}_{1}
, one has
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{H^{2}}\leq C(T).
Differentiating equation
\eqref{0}_{1}
with respect to
t
, we obtain

\displaystyle\rho_{tt}+\rho_{t}\div\mathbf{u}+\rho\div\mathbf{u}_{t}+\nabla\rho_{t}\cdot\mathbf{u}+\nabla\rho\cdot\mathbf{u}_{t}=0,

which shows that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{tt}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\rho_{tt}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}dt\leq C(T).

We have completed the proof.
∎

The pair
(\rho,\mathbf{u})
in Proposition
3.20
can be shown to belong to
C([0,T];H^{3})
. Indeed,
\rho\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{3})
together with
\rho_{t}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2})
implies
\rho\in C([0,T];H^{2})
. The
L^{2}
-continuity of the third-order derivatives of
\rho
follows from standard arguments on weak convergence and norm convergence. Moreover, since
\mathbf{u}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{4})
and
\mathbf{u}_{t}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{2})
, standard interpolation gives
\mathbf{u}\in C([0,T];H^{3})
.

We now prove that
T^{*}=\infty
. If not, then
T^{*}<\infty
, and the constant
C(T)
in Proposition
3.20
can be replaced by
C(T^{*})
, where
C(T^{*})
depends on
T^{*}
and not on
T<T^{*}
. Therefore, we can define

\displaystyle(\rho(T^{*}),\mathbf{u}(T^{*}))=\lim\limits_{t\to T^{*}}(\rho(t),\mathbf{u}(t))\in H^{3}.

The pair
(\rho(T^{*}),\mathbf{u}(T^{*}))
can serve as new initial data, and at this time
\rho(T^{*})
has a positive lower bound. By the local existence Lemma
3.1
, there exists a small time
t_{0}>0
such that the new initial-boundary-value problem has a solution on
[T^{*},T^{*}+t_{0}]
. This contradicts the maximality of
T^{*}
. We have completed the proof of Theorem
2.1
.

3.8
The proof of Theorem
2.2

Note that global existence of classical solutions has been established by Theorem
2.1
for
N=2
with
\alpha>0.54,\gamma>1
and for
N=3
with
(\alpha,\gamma)\in\Big\{(\alpha,\gamma)|0.689<\alpha<1,1<\gamma<3\alpha-1\Big\}\cap\Big\{(\alpha,\gamma)|0.686<\alpha<1,1<\gamma<6\alpha-3+\frac{3-5\alpha}{2n_{3}(\alpha)}\Big\}
. We now sketch the proof for the remaining case
N=3
with
(\alpha,\gamma)\in\Big\{(\alpha,\gamma)|0.689<\alpha<1,1<\gamma<3\alpha-1\Big\}-\Big\{(\alpha,\gamma)|0.686<\alpha<1,1<\gamma<6\alpha-3+\frac{3-5\alpha}{2n_{3}(\alpha)}\Big\}.
In this case, Propositions
3.13
and
3.14
yield positive upper and lower bounds for the density, while Proposition
3.12
provides the
L^{4}
bound on the velocity field and the derivative of the density. Consequently, combining this with (
45
) and (
52
), we know that (
86
) holds for
l=2
with
M
replaced by
C(T)
. Iterating Propositions
3.15
-
3.19
then gives the higher-order estimates, and repeating the arguments of the previous section, we obtain global existence of solutions.

Now, we assume that
(\rho,\mathbf{u})
is the global classical solution constructed in Theorem
2.2
. We obtain from (
45
), (
52
), (
82
) and (
83
) that,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}&\left(\left\|\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\rho^{\alpha-\frac{7}{4}}\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}\right)\\
&+\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{\gamma+\alpha-1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\rho^{\frac{\gamma+3\alpha-7}{4}}\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\rho^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt\leq C.\end{split}

(127)

We now show that the lower bound of the density is in fact independent of time.

Proposition 3.21
.

There exist constants
\varepsilon>0
depending only on the initial data such that

\displaystyle\rho\geq\varepsilon,\quad\forall(x,t)\in\Omega\times[0,\infty).

(128)

Furthermore, it holds that

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{t\to\infty}\left\|\rho(t)-\overline{\rho_{0}}\right\|_{C(\overline{\Omega})}=0.

(129)

Proof.

We choose
b>1
sufficiently large so that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}&\left(\left\|\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{b-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{b}\right\|_{L^{4}}\right)\\
&+\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|\nabla\rho^{b-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{b}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}dt\leq C.\end{split}

(130)

By the embedding inequality we have

\displaystyle\left\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right\|_{C(\overline{\Omega})}\leq C\left\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{\frac{4-N}{4}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{b}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{\frac{N}{4}}\leq C\left\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{\frac{4-N}{4}}.

(131)

Next, we claim that

\displaystyle g(t)=\left\|(\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}})(t)\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\rightarrow 0,\text{ as }t\rightarrow\infty.

(132)

To prove (
132
), we first show that
\int_{0}^{\infty}|g^{\prime}(t)|dt\leq C
. A direct computation gives

\displaystyle g^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=4b\left\langle(\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}})^{3}\rho^{b-1},\rho_{t}\right\rangle-4(\overline{\rho^{b}})_{t}\int_{\Omega}(\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}})^{3}dx

\displaystyle=-4b\left\langle\nabla\left((\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}})^{3}\rho^{b-1}\right),\sqrt{\rho}\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\rangle-4(\overline{\rho^{b}})_{t}\int_{\Omega}(\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}})^{3}dx:=I_{1}+I_{2}.

Using (
130
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\infty}|I_{1}|dt\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Omega}\Big((\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}})^{2}|\nabla\rho^{b}|\rho^{b-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\rho}|\mathbf{u}|+\left|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right|^{3}|\nabla\rho^{b-1}|\sqrt{\rho}\sqrt{\rho}|\mathbf{u}|\Big)dxdt\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{b}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{b-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\right)dt\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho^{b-1}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{b}\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\right)dt\leq C.\end{split}

(133)

Note that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}\left|\frac{d}{dt}\overline{\rho^{b}}\right|

\displaystyle=b\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}\left|\langle\rho^{b-1},\rho_{t}\rangle\right|=b\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}\left|\int_{\Omega}\nabla\rho^{b-1}\sqrt{\rho}\cdot\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}dx\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{b-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}\leq C.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}|I_{2}|dt\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|\nabla\rho^{b}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt\leq C.

(134)

Finally, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}g(t)dt\leq C\left\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|\nabla\rho^{b}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt\leq C.

(135)

The estimates (
133
)–(
135
) imply (
132
). Therefore, we obtain from (
131
) that

\displaystyle\left\|\rho^{b}(t)-\overline{\rho^{b}}(t)\right\|_{C(\overline{\Omega})}\to 0,\text{ as }t\rightarrow\infty.

(136)

Then, by Jensen’s inequality we obtain

\displaystyle\overline{\rho^{b}}\geq\overline{\rho}^{b}=\overline{\rho_{0}}^{b}>0.

Together with (
136
), this shows that there exist
\varepsilon_{0}
and
T^{*}
such that

\displaystyle\rho(x,t)\geq\varepsilon_{0},\quad\forall(x,t)\in\Omega\times[T^{*},\infty).

(137)

We note that
\rho
has a positive lower bound on
\Omega\times[0,T^{*}]
, which together with (
137
) proves (
128
).

Below we prove (
129
). We first prove that
\overline{\rho^{b}}(t)
converges to some positive constant as
t\to\infty
. Indeed, from (
128
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\frac{d}{dt}\overline{\rho^{b}}(t)\right|&=\left|\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\rho^{b}dx\right|\leq C\left|\int_{\Omega}(\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}})\div\mathbf{u}dx\right|\\
&\leq C\int_{\Omega}\Big(|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}+|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}|^{2}\Big)dx\\
&\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{\alpha}}\int_{\Omega}\rho^{\alpha}|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dx+C\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\rho^{b}|^{2}dx.\end{split}

(138)

Integrating with respect to
t
and using (
127
) and (
130
) gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|\frac{d}{dt}\overline{\rho^{b}}(t)\right|dt<\infty.

This shows that
\overline{\rho^{b}}(t)
converges to some positive constant
a_{0}
, which together with (
136
) implies

\displaystyle\left\|\rho^{b}(t)-a_{0}\right\|_{C(\overline{\Omega})}\rightarrow 0,\text{ as }t\rightarrow\infty.

(139)

Using
b>1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\rho(t)-a_{0}^{\frac{1}{b}}\right\|_{C(\overline{\Omega})}\rightarrow 0,\text{ as }t\rightarrow\infty.

(140)

It is easily checked, by applying the dominated convergence theorem, that
a_{0}^{1/b}=\overline{\rho_{0}}
. Thus, we have completed the proof.
∎

Therefore, combining (
81
), (
127
), and (
128
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}\Big(\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{4}}&+\left\|\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}\Big)\\
&+\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)dt\leq C.\end{split}

(141)

Proposition 3.22
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{t\to\infty}\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}=0.

(142)

Proof.

We set
g(t)=\int_{\Omega}(\mu(\rho)|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}+\lambda(\rho)(\div\mathbf{u})^{2})dx
. Using (
141
), one has

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}g(t)dt\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}dxdt\leq C.

(143)

We obtain from (
96
) that

\displaystyle g^{\prime}(t)\leq C(\left\|\nabla\rho(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).

Letting
t
be sufficiently large and taking
s\in[t-1,t]
, we have

\displaystyle g(t)=g(s)+\int_{s}^{t}g^{\prime}(\tau)d\tau\leq g(s)+C\int_{t-1}^{t}(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2})d\tau.

Integrating with respect to
s
over
[t-1,t]
gives

\displaystyle g(t)\leq\int_{t-1}^{t}g(\tau)d\tau+C\int_{t-1}^{t}(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2})d\tau.

(144)

Therefore, we know from (
141
) and (
143
) that

\displaystyle g(t)\to 0,\text{ as }t\to\infty,

which, combined with (
16
), (
17
) and (
128
), implies (
142
).
∎

Proposition 3.23
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{t\to\infty}(\left\|\nabla\rho(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}})=0.

(145)

Proof.

We first prove that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\rho|\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx(t)\to 0,\text{ as }t\to\infty.

(146)

Similarly to (
144
), we obtain from (
109
) that, for sufficiently large
t
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\Omega}\rho|\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dx(t)\leq&\int_{t-1}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\rho|\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}dxd\tau\\
&+C\int_{t-1}^{t}(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{4})d\tau.\end{split}

(147)

Letting
t\to\infty
, we get (
146
) from (
87
), (
98
) and (
141
). Next, we prove

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\rho|^{2}dx(t)\to 0,\text{ as }t\to\infty.

(148)

Using (
93
), one has

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\rho|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\Omega}(|\nabla\mathbf{u}||\nabla\rho|^{2}+\rho|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}||\nabla\rho|)dx

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\Omega}(|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}+|\nabla\rho|^{4}+|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}|^{2}+|\nabla\rho|^{2})dx

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\Omega}(|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}+|\nabla\rho|^{4}+|\nabla\rho|^{2}+\rho|\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2})dx.

Therefore, similarly to (
144
), we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\rho|^{2}dx(t)\leq\int_{t-1}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\rho|^{2}dxd\tau+C\int_{t-1}^{t}\int_{\Omega}(|\nabla\mathbf{u}|^{2}+|\nabla\rho|^{4}+|\nabla\rho|^{2}+\rho|\dot{\mathbf{u}}|^{2})dxd\tau.\end{split}

(149)

Letting
t\to\infty
, we get (
148
) from (
87
) and (
141
). Finally, from (
93
) one obtains

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\dot{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\rho(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}})\to 0,\text{ as }t\to\infty.

(150)

The proof is complete.
∎

4
Global classical solutions away from vacuum including viscous shallow water equations
(\alpha=1)

Throughout this section, we always assume
\alpha=1
. Suppose that
(\rho,\mathbf{u})
is the unique local classical solution to the initial‑boundary‑value problem (
1
)–(
3
) with initial data satisfying (
14
), defined on
\Omega\times[0,T]
for some
T>0
. The existence of such a solution is guaranteed by Lemma
3.1
.

4.1
Upper bound for the density

Noting that
n_{2}(1)=+\infty
, we can obtain the following proposition by following exactly the same arguments as in Propositions
3.11
–
3.13
. For brevity, we omit the proof here.

Proposition 4.1
.

Assume that (
20
) holds. Then there exists a constant
C>0
, independent of
T
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}\rho u^{4}rdr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}\Big(\frac{\rho u^{4}}{r}+\rho(\partial_{r}u)^{2}u^{2}r\Big)drdt\leq C

(151)

as well as

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\frac{1}{4}}|^{4}rdr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{4}}|^{4}rdrdt\leq C

(152)

and finally,

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}\leq C.

(153)

4.2
Positive lower bound for the density

In this subsection we establish a positive lower bound for the density, which is the most crucial part in proving the global existence of solutions for the case
\alpha=1
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Assume that (
20
) holds. Then there exists a constant
C(T)>0
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}\leq C(T).

(154)

Proof.

Applying Lemma
7.4
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+C\left\|\nabla\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+C\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{1}{4}}\right\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\left\|\rho^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)},

which, together with (
45
), (
151
), and (
152
), yields (
154
).
∎

From now until the end of this subsection, we return to calculations in Lagrangian coordinates. Define the effective velocity

\displaystyle w=u+r\partial_{y}\rho,\quad\text{on }[0,1]\times[0,T].

(155)

The following proposition provides an
L^{\infty}
estimate for the effective velocity.

Proposition 4.2
.

Assume that (
20
) holds. Then there exists a constant
C(T)>0
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left\|w\right\|_{L^{\infty}((0,1))}\leq C(T).

(156)

Proof.

Let
n\in\mathbb{R}
be sufficiently large. Multiplying both sides of the B-D entropy equation
\partial_{\tau}w+r\partial_{y}\rho^{\gamma}=0
by
w^{2n-1}
and integrating the resulting equation over
[0,1]
yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{0}^{1}w^{2n}dy+\int_{0}^{1}\gamma\rho^{\gamma-1}r\partial_{y}\rho w^{2n-1}dy=0,

which, combined with the definition of the effective velocity (
155
), gives

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{2n}\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{0}^{1}w^{2n}dy+\int_{0}^{1}\gamma\rho^{\gamma-1}w^{2n}dy

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\gamma\rho^{\gamma-1}uw^{2n-1}dy

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\left\|w\right\|_{L^{2n}((0,1))}^{2n-1}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}u\right\|_{L^{2n}((0,1))}\left\|\rho^{\gamma-\frac{3}{2}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((0,1))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{0}^{1}w^{2n}dy+1\right)\left\|\sqrt{\rho}u\right\|_{L^{\infty}((0,1))},

where in the last inequality we have used (
20
) and (
153
). Therefore, applying Gronwall’s inequality we obtain for any
\tau\in(0,T)
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}w^{2n}(\tau)dy\leq\exp\left\{2nC\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}u\right\|_{L^{\infty}((0,1))}d\tau\right\}\left(\int_{0}^{1}w_{0}^{2n}dy+2nC\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}u\right\|_{L^{\infty}((0,1))}d\tau\right).

Using (
154
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|w(\tau)\right\|_{L^{2n}((0,1))}\leq C(T)\left\|w_{0}\right\|_{L^{2n}((0,1))}+C(T)\leq C(T)\left\|w_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((0,1))}+C(T).

Letting
n\to\infty
, we obtain (
156
).
∎

Next, we use the
L^{\infty}
estimate of the effective velocity to derive an
L^{\infty}
estimate for the velocity.

Proposition 4.3
.

Assume that (
20
) holds. Then there exists a constant
C(T)>0
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left\|u\right\|_{L^{\infty}((0,1))}\leq C(T).

(157)

Proof.

Let
n\in\mathbb{R}
be sufficiently large. Multiplying
\eqref{Equ 3}_{2}
by
ru^{2n-1}
, integrating the resulting equation over
(0,1)
, and using integration by parts together with the boundary condition (
44
) and the fact that
\alpha=1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\frac{1}{2n}\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{0}^{1}u^{2n}dy+\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\frac{u^{2n}}{r^{2}}+(2n-1)\rho^{2}(\partial_{y}u)^{2}u^{2n-2}r^{2}\Big)dy

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{y}\rho^{\gamma}|r|u|^{2n-1}dy

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\gamma\rho^{\gamma-1}(|w|+|u|)|u|^{2n-1}dy

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{1}u^{2n}dy+C\int_{0}^{1}w^{2n}dy

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{1}u^{2n}dy+CC(T)^{2n},

where we have used (
153
), (
156
), and (
155
). Applying Gronwall’s inequality, we obtain for any
\tau\in(0,T)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}u^{2n}(\tau)dy\leq\exp\left\{2nCT\right\}\left(\int_{0}^{1}u_{0}^{2n}dy+2nTCC(T)^{2n}\right),

which implies that

\displaystyle\left\|u(\tau)\right\|_{L^{2n}((0,1))}\leq C(T)\left\|u_{0}\right\|_{L^{2n}((0,1))}+C(T)\leq C(T)\left\|u_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((0,1))}+C(T).

Letting
n\to\infty
, we obtain (
157
).
∎

Finally, we prove that the density admits a positive lower bound, which is essential to ensure the global existence of the solution.

Proposition 4.4
.

Assume that (
20
) holds. Then there exists a constant
C(T)>0
, such that

\displaystyle V_{T}\leq C(T).

(158)

Proof.

Using (
156
) and (
157
), we directly obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left\|r\partial_{y}\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}((0,1))}\leq C(T).

(159)

Let
v=\rho^{-1}
. Then
v
satisfies the equation
v_{\tau}=(ru)_{y}
. Integrating this equation over
(0,1)\times(0,\tau)
and using (
44
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}v(y,\tau)dy=\int_{0}^{1}v_{0}(y)dy\leq C.

(160)

Let
\beta\in(0,1)
be a parameter to be determined later. For any
(y,\tau)\in(0,1)\times(0,T)
, using the one‑dimensional Sobolev embedding, Hölder’s inequality, (
159
), and (
160
), we have

\displaystyle v^{\beta}(y,\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}v^{\beta}dy+\beta\int_{0}^{1}v^{\beta-1}|\partial_{y}v|dy

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{0}^{1}vdy\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta}}+\beta\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{y}\rho|v^{\beta+1}dy

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta)+\beta\left\|r\partial_{y}\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}((0,1))}\left(\int_{0}^{1}vr^{-\frac{3}{2}}dy\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}v^{3\beta+1}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta)+C(T)\beta V_{T}^{\beta}.

Taking the supremum over
(y,\tau)
of the above inequality and setting
\beta=\min\{\frac{1}{2C(T)},\frac{1}{2}\}
, we obtain (
158
). This completes the proof of Proposition
4.4
.
∎

4.3
The proof of Theorem
2.3

Combining (
151
), (
153
), (
158
), (
52
), (
152
), and (
45
), we obtain the following bound:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\Big(\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}&+\left\|\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+\left\|\rho^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\Big)\\
&+\int_{0}^{T}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}^{4}+\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\right)dt\leq C(T).\end{split}

(161)

Using Propositions
3.15
-
3.19
we obtain estimates for higher‑order derivatives. Hence, by a continuity argument, we have proved that the local classical solution
(\rho,\mathbf{u})
exists globally in time.

Regarding the large‑time behavior of the solution, we recall the uniform estimates (
153
), (
52
), (
152
), and (
45
) already obtained:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}&\left(\left\|\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{1}{4}}\right\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}\right)\\
&+\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{4}}\right\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}^{4}+\left\|\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}dt\leq C.\end{split}

This, together with Propositions
3.21
-
3.23
, implies that the density possesses a uniform positive lower bound, and consequently, the large‑time behavior of the solution follows.

We have completed the proof of Theorem
2.3
.

5
Global weak solutions allowing vacuum
(\alpha\geq 1)

Throughout this section we always assume
\gamma>1
and
\alpha\geq 1
if
N=2
or
1\leq\alpha<11.7
if
N=3
; the radially symmetric initial data
(\rho_{0},\mathbf{m}_{0})
are taken to satisfy (
21
) with
p,q
fulfilling

\displaystyle\frac{N}{2}<q<p<n_{N}(\alpha),\quad p\in\mathbb{R},\quad q\in M_{set}.

5.1
Construction of the approximate system I

We employ the approximate system from
[
14
]
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\rho_{\eta}+\div(\rho_{\eta}\mathbf{u}_{\eta})=0,\\
\partial_{t}(\rho_{\eta}\mathbf{u}_{\eta})+\div(\rho_{\eta}\mathbf{u}_{\eta}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{\eta})+\nabla(\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma})=\div(\tilde{\mu}(\rho_{\eta})\nabla\mathbf{u}_{\eta})+\nabla(\tilde{\lambda}(\rho_{\eta})\div\mathbf{u}_{\eta}),\\
(\rho_{\eta},\mathbf{u}_{\eta})|_{t=0}=(\rho_{\eta,0},\mathbf{u}_{\eta,0}),\\
\mathbf{u}_{\eta}|_{\partial\Omega_{\eta}}=0\end{array}\right.

where
t\geq 0
and
x\in\Omega_{\eta}=\Omega-B_{\eta}
. The viscosity coefficients satisfy
