\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}(\rho_{\eta})=\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}+\eta\rho^{\delta}_{\eta},\quad\tilde{\lambda}(\rho_{\eta})=(\alpha-1)\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}+\eta(\delta-1)\rho_{\eta}^{\delta},

(166)

where
\delta
is chosen as follows:

\displaystyle 1-\frac{p\sqrt{2p-1}-2p+1}{p^{2}-2p+1}<\delta<1-\frac{1}{2p},\ N=2;

(167)

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\sqrt{4p(4p^{2}-p-1)+1}-6p+3}{4p^{2}-8p+4}<\delta<1-\frac{1}{2p},\ N=3\text{ and }p\geq 1.55;

(168)

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\sqrt{4n(4n^{2}-n-1)+1}-6n+3}{4n^{2}-8n+4}|_{n=1.55}<\delta=0.677<1-\frac{1}{2n}|_{n=1.55},\ N=3\text{ and }p<1.55.

(169)

Remark 5.1
.

The artificial viscosity term is added to obtain an
\eta
-dependent positive lower bound for
\rho_{\eta}
, which is crucial for the global existence of the approximate solution. It is readily verified that the
\delta
appearing in (
167
) and (
168
) is well-defined.

Remark 5.2
.

For
N=2
, or for
N=3
and
p\geq 1.55
, the definition of
\delta
immediately gives
p<n_{N}(\delta)
.
When
N=3
and
p<1.55
, we likewise have
p<1.55<n_{3}(\delta)
.

We now specify the initial data for the approximate system. Without loss of generality, we assume the total initial mass
\int_{\Omega}\rho_{0}dx=1
. First, extend
\rho_{0}
continuously to
\mathbb{R}^{N}
and set

\displaystyle\rho_{\eta,0}=C_{\eta}\left(j_{\varepsilon(\eta)}*\rho_{0}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}+\eta^{\frac{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}{\alpha-\delta}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}},\text{ in }\Omega,

(170)

where the constant
C_{\eta}
is chosen so that
\int_{\Omega_{\eta}}\rho_{\eta,0}\,dx=1
, and
j_{\varepsilon(\eta)}
is the standard mollifier. We then define

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{\eta,0}=\left(\frac{1}{\rho_{\eta,0}}\left(\frac{|\mathbf{m}_{0}|^{2p}}{\rho_{0}^{2p-1}}\alpha_{\eta}\right)*j_{\varepsilon(\eta)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2p}}\frac{\mathbf{x}}{r},\text{ in }\Omega,

(171)

where
\alpha_{\eta}(\cdot)
is a smooth cut-off function such that
\alpha_{\eta}(x)=0
whenever
0\leq|x|\leq 2\eta
or
|x|\geq R-\eta
, and
\alpha(x)=1
when
3\eta\leq|x|\leq R-2\eta
. One verifies that the initial data of the approximate system possess the following properties:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho_{\eta,0}\rightarrow\rho_{0}\text{ in }L^{\gamma}(\Omega),\quad\nabla\rho_{\eta,0}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\rightarrow\nabla\rho_{0}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\text{ in }L^{2}(\Omega),\quad\rho_{\eta,0}\geq C\eta^{\frac{1}{\alpha-\delta}},\\
&\nabla\rho_{\eta,0}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}\rightarrow\nabla\rho_{0}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}\text{ in }L^{2q}(\Omega),\quad|\mathbf{m}_{\eta,0}|^{2p}/\rho_{\eta,0}^{2p-1}\rightarrow|\mathbf{m}_{0}|^{2p}/\rho_{0}^{2p-1}\text{ in }L^{1}(\Omega),\\
&|\mathbf{m}_{\eta,0}|^{2}/\rho_{\eta,0}\rightarrow|\mathbf{m}_{0}|^{2}/\rho_{0}\text{ in }L^{1}(\Omega),\quad\mathbf{u}_{\eta,0}|_{\partial\Omega_{\eta}}=0,\end{split}

(172)

where
C>0
is a generic constant and
\mathbf{m}_{\eta,0}=\rho_{\eta,0}\mathbf{u}_{\eta,0}
. Restricting the obtained pair
(\rho_{\eta,0},\mathbf{u}_{\eta,0})
to
\Omega_{\eta}
, we still denote it by
(\rho_{\eta,0},\mathbf{u}_{\eta,0})
.

In spherical coordinates, the approximate system (
5.1
) takes the following form

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\rho_{\eta})_{t}+(\rho_{\eta}u_{\eta})_{r}+\frac{N-1}{r}\rho_{\eta}u_{\eta}=0,\\
\rho_{\eta}(u_{\eta})_{t}+\rho_{\eta}u_{\eta}(u_{\eta})_{r}+(\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma})_{r}-\left(\frac{\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}+\eta\delta\rho_{\eta}^{\delta}}{r^{N-1}}(r^{N-1}u_{\eta})_{r}\right)_{r}+\frac{N-1}{r}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}+\eta\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{r}u_{\eta}=0,\\
(\rho_{\eta},u_{\eta})|_{t=0}=(\rho_{\eta,0},u_{\eta,0}),\\
u_{\eta}(\eta,t)=u_{\eta}(R,t)=0,\end{array}\right.

where
t\geq 0
and
r\in(\eta,R)
.

Define
y(r,t)=\int_{\eta}^{r}\rho_{\eta}(s,t)s^{N-1}ds
and
\tau(r,t)=t
. Then, in Lagrangian coordinates, the approximate system (
5.1
) becomes

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\rho_{\eta})_{\tau}+\rho^{2}_{\eta}(r^{N-1}u_{\eta})_{y}=0,\\
r^{-(N-1)}(u_{\eta})_{\tau}+(\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma})_{y}-((\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha+1}+\eta\delta\rho^{1+\delta}_{\eta})(r^{N-1}u_{\eta})_{y})_{y}+\frac{N-1}{r}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}+\eta\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{y}u_{\eta}=0,\\
(\rho_{\eta},u_{\eta})|_{\tau=0}=(\rho_{\eta,0},u_{\eta,0}),\\
u_{\eta}(0,\tau)=u_{\eta}(1,\tau)=0,\end{array}\right.

where
\tau\geq 0
and
y\in(0,1)
.

5.2
Local solvability of the approximate system

We study the global solvability of system (
5.1
) in Lagrangian coordinates. Fix
\eta>0
. It is easily seen that
\rho_{0}^{\eta}\in C^{1+\beta}[0,1]
and
u_{0}^{\eta}\in C^{2+\beta}[0,1]
for any
0<\beta<1
. As remarked in
[
14
]
, the system is now essentially one-dimensional and contains no singularities. For such problems a standard argument yields a unique local classical solution
(\rho_{\eta},u_{\eta})
with
\rho_{\eta},\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta},\partial_{\tau y}\rho_{\eta},u_{\eta},\partial_{y}u_{\eta},\partial_{\tau}u_{\eta},\partial_{yy}u_{\eta}\in C^{\beta,\frac{\beta}{2}}([0,1]\times[0,T_{0}])
for some
T_{0}>0
.

5.3
Some useful estimates

Let
T^{*}
be the maximal existence time of the local classical solution
(\rho_{\eta},u_{\eta})
to the approximate system (
5.1
), and fix
0<T<T^{*}
.

Proposition 5.1
.

There exists a constant
C>0
independent of
\tau
and
\eta
such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}(u_{\eta}^{2}+\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-1})dy+\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2}}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle+\rho_{\eta}^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}{u_{\eta}})^{2}r^{2(N-1)}\Big)dyd\tau

\displaystyle+\eta\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}

\displaystyle\Big(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2}}{r^{2}}+\rho_{\eta}^{1+\delta}(\partial_{y}{u_{\eta}})^{2}r^{2(N-1)}\Big)dyd\tau\leq C.

(181)

For
1<n\leq p
, there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
\tau
and
\eta
such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}u_{\eta}^{2n}dy+\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2n}}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle+\rho_{\eta}^{1+\alpha}{(\partial_{y}{u_{\eta}})}^{2}u_{\eta}^{2n-2}r^{2(N-1)}\Big)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\leq C+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{2n(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha-1}r^{2(n-1)}dyd\tau.

(182)

For
p<n<\min\{n_{N}(\alpha),n_{N}(\delta)\}
, there exists a constant
C(\eta)>0
independent of
\tau
but depending on
\eta
such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}u_{\eta}^{2n}dy+\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2n}}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle+\rho^{1+\alpha}{(\partial_{y}{u_{\eta}})}^{2}u_{\eta}^{2n-2}r^{2(N-1)}\Big)dyd\tau

\displaystyle\leq C(\eta)+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{2n(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha-1}r^{2(n-1)}dyd\tau.

(183)

Proof.

Multiplying
\eqref{app system lag}_{2}
by
r^{N-1}u_{\eta}
and integrating the resulting equation over
(0,1)\times(0,\tau)
, we obtain (
181
) by using the fact
1<\min\{n_{N}(\alpha),n_{N}(\delta)\}
and the boundary condition
\eqref{app system lag}_{4}
.

Fix
1<n<\min\{n_{N}(\alpha),n_{N}(\delta)\}
. Multiplying
\eqref{app system lag}_{2}
by
r^{N-1}u_{\eta}^{2n-1}
and integrating the resulting equation over
(0,1)
gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{0}^{1}u_{\eta}^{2n}dy+\int_{0}^{1}\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{1+\alpha}(r^{N-1}u_{\eta})_{y}(r^{N-1}u_{\eta}^{2n-1})_{y}-(N-1)\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}(r^{N-2}u_{\eta}^{2n})_{y}dy

\displaystyle+\eta\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\delta\rho_{\eta}^{1+\delta}(r^{N-1}u_{\eta})_{y}(r^{N-1}u_{\eta}^{2n-1})_{y}-(N-1)\rho_{\eta}^{\delta}(r^{N-2}u_{\eta}^{2n})_{y}\Big)dy=\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma}(r^{N-1}u_{\eta}^{2n-1})_{y}dy.

A direct computation shows

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2n}\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{0}^{1}u_{\eta}^{2n}dy+\int_{0}^{1}\Big((\alpha(N-1)^{2}-(N-1)(N-2))\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2n}}{r^{2}}\\
&\quad+(2n-1)\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2n-2}r^{2(N-1)}+2n(N-1)(\alpha-1)\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}u_{\eta}^{2n-1}\partial_{y}u_{\eta}r^{N-2}\Big)dy\\
&\quad+\eta\int_{0}^{1}\Big((\delta(N-1)^{2}-(N-1)(N-2))\rho_{\eta}^{\delta-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2n}}{r^{2}}\\
&\quad+(2n-1)\delta\rho_{\eta}^{1+\delta}(\partial_{y}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2n-2}r^{2(N-1)}+2n(N-1)(\delta-1)\rho_{\eta}^{\delta}u_{\eta}^{2n-1}\partial_{y}u_{\eta}r^{N-2}\Big)dy\\
&=\int_{0}^{1}\Big((2n-1)\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma}u_{\eta}^{2n-2}\partial_{y}u_{\eta}r^{N-1}+(N-1)\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2n-1}}{r}\Big)dy.\end{split}

(184)

Since
1<n<\min\{n_{N}(\alpha),n_{N}(\delta)\}
, we can choose
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small so that

\displaystyle\frac{(2n(N-1)(\alpha-1))^{2}}{4(1-2\varepsilon)(2n-1)\alpha}<(1-2\varepsilon)(\alpha(N-1)^{2}-(N-1)(N-2))

and

\displaystyle\frac{(2n(N-1)(\delta-1))^{2}}{4(1-2\varepsilon)(2n-1)\delta}<(1-2\varepsilon)(\delta(N-1)^{2}-(N-1)(N-2)).

Therefore, Young’s inequality yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad 2n(N-1)(\alpha-1)\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}u_{\eta}^{2n-1}\partial_{y}u_{\eta}r^{N-2}\\
&\geq-(1-2\varepsilon)(2n-1)\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}{u_{\eta}})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2n-2}r^{2(N-1)}-\frac{(2n(N-1)(\alpha-1))^{2}}{4(1-2\varepsilon)(2n-1)\alpha}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2n}}{r^{2}}\\
&\geq-(1-2\varepsilon)(2n-1)\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2n-2}r^{2(N-1)}\\
&\quad-(1-2\varepsilon)(\alpha(N-1)^{2}-(N-1)(N-2))\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2n}}{r^{2}}\end{split}

(185)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad 2n(N-1)(\delta-1)\rho_{\eta}^{\delta}u_{\eta}^{2n-1}\partial_{y}u_{\eta}r^{N-2}\\
&\geq-(1-2\varepsilon)(2n-1)\delta\rho_{\eta}^{1+\delta}(\partial_{y}{u_{\eta}})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2n-2}r^{2(N-1)}-\frac{(2n(N-1)(\delta-1))^{2}}{4(1-2\varepsilon)(2n-1)\delta}\rho_{\eta}^{\delta-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2n}}{r^{2}}\\
&\geq-(1-2\varepsilon)(2n-1)\delta\rho_{\eta}^{1+\delta}(\partial_{y}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2n-2}r^{2(N-1)}\\
&\quad-(1-2\varepsilon)(\delta(N-1)^{2}-(N-1)(N-2))\rho_{\eta}^{\delta-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2n}}{r^{2}}.\end{split}

(186)

Using (
185
) and (
186
), we obtain from (
184
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2n}\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{0}^{1}u_{\eta}^{2n}dy+2\varepsilon\int_{0}^{1}\Big((\alpha(N-1)^{2}-(N-1)(N-2))\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2n}}{r^{2}}\\
&\quad+(2n-1)\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2n-2}r^{2(N-1)}\Big)dy\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}\Big((2n-1)\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma}u_{\eta}^{2n-2}\partial_{y}u_{\eta}r^{N-1}+(N-1)\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2n-1}}{r}\Big)dy.\end{split}

(187)

Applying Young’s inequality once more, we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2n}\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{0}^{1}u_{\eta}^{2n}dy+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{1}\Big((\alpha(N-1)^{2}-(N-1)(N-2))\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2n}}{r^{2}}\\
&\quad+(2n-1)\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2n-2}r^{2(N-1)}\Big)dy\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{2n(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha-1}r^{2n-2}dy.\end{split}

(188)

Integrate the above expression over
(0,\tau)
. If
1<n\leq p
, we obtain from (
172
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2n}\int_{0}^{1}u_{\eta}^{2n}dy+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\Big((\alpha(N-1)^{2}-(N-1)(N-2))\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2n}}{r^{2}}\\
&\quad+(2n-1)\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2n-2}r^{2(N-1)}\Big)dyd\tau\\
&\leq\frac{1}{2n}\int_{0}^{1}u_{\eta,0}^{2n}dy+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{2n(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha-1}r^{2n-2}dyd\tau\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{1}u_{\eta,0}^{2p}dy+C\int_{0}^{1}u_{\eta,0}^{2}dy+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{2n(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha-1}r^{2n-2}dyd\tau\\
&\leq C+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{2n(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha-1}r^{2n-2}dyd\tau.\end{split}

(189)

If
p<n<\min\{n_{N}(\alpha),n_{N}(\delta)\}
, the definition of
u_{\eta,0}
implies (
183
). This completes the proof of Proposition
5.1
.
∎

Proposition 5.2
.

Assume that
1<m\leq q
and
m\in M_{set}
. It holds that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}(u_{\eta}+r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha})_{y}+\eta r^{N-1}(\rho^{\delta}_{\eta})_{y})^{2}dy

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\alpha}(r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha})_{y})^{2}dyd\tau\leq C;

(190)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}(u_{\eta}+r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha})_{y}+\eta r^{N-1}(\rho^{\delta}_{\eta})_{y})^{2m}dy

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\alpha}(r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha})_{y})^{2m}dyd\tau

\displaystyle\leq C+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\alpha}u_{\eta}^{2m}dyd\tau,

(191)

where
C
is a positive constant independent of
\tau
and
\eta
.

Proof.

From equation (
5.1
) we derive the following B–D entropy equation:

\displaystyle(u_{\eta}+r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha})_{y}+\eta r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{y})_{\tau}+(\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma})_{y}r^{N-1}=0.

(192)

Multiplying (
192
) by (
u_{\eta}+r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha})_{y}+\eta r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{y}
) and integrating the resulting equation over
(0,1)\times(0,\tau)
gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{2}(u_{\eta}+r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha})_{y}+\eta r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{y})^{2}+\frac{\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-1}}{\gamma-1}dy+\alpha\gamma\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma+\alpha-2}(\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta})^{2}r^{2(N-1)}dyd\tau

\displaystyle+\eta\delta\gamma\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma+\delta-2}(\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta})^{2}r^{2(N-1)}dyd\tau=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{2}(u_{\eta,0}+r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta,0}^{\alpha})_{y}+\eta r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta,0}^{\delta})_{y})^{2}+\frac{\rho_{\eta,0}^{\gamma-1}}{\gamma-1}dy,

which together with (
172
) yields (
190
).

Multiplying (
192
) by
\bigl(u_{\eta}+r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha})_{y}+\eta r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{y}\bigr)^{2m-1}
and integrating the resulting equation over
y\in(0,1)
gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m}\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{0}^{1}

\displaystyle(u_{\eta}+r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha})_{y}+\eta r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{y})^{2m}dy

\displaystyle+\gamma\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-1}\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta}(u_{\eta}+r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha})_{y}+\eta r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{y})^{2m-1}r^{N-1}dy=0.

Using Lemma
7.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-1}\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta}(u_{\eta}+r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha})_{y}+\eta r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{y})^{2m-1}r^{N-1}

\displaystyle=(\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}+\eta\delta\rho_{\eta}^{\delta-1})r^{N-1}\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta}(u_{\eta}+(\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}+\eta\delta\rho_{\eta}^{\delta-1})r^{N-1}\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta})^{2m-1}\frac{\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-1}}{\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}+\eta\delta\rho_{\eta}^{\delta-1}}

\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon((\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}+\eta\delta\rho_{\eta}^{\delta-1})r^{N-1}\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta})^{2m}\frac{\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-1}}{\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}+\eta\delta\rho_{\eta}^{\delta-1}}-\frac{\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-1}}{\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}+\eta\delta\rho_{\eta}^{\delta-1}}u_{\eta}^{2m}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}+\eta\delta\rho_{\eta}^{\delta-1})^{2m-1}(r^{N-1}\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta})^{2m}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-1}-\frac{\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-1}}{\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}+\eta\delta\rho_{\eta}^{\delta-1}}u_{\eta}^{2m}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{\alpha}(r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha})_{y})^{2m}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\alpha}-\frac{1}{\alpha}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\alpha}u_{\eta}^{2m},

where
\varepsilon>0
is a sufficiently small generic constant. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m}\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{0}^{1}(u_{\eta}+r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha})_{y}

\displaystyle+\eta r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{y})^{2m}dy+\frac{\varepsilon}{\alpha}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\alpha}(r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha})_{y})^{2m}dy

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\alpha}u_{\eta}^{2m}dy.

Integrating the inequatity with respect to time and using (
172
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{1}{2m}\int_{0}^{1}(u_{\eta}+r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha})_{y}+\eta r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{y})^{2m}dy+\frac{\varepsilon}{\alpha}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\alpha}(r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha})_{y})^{2m}dyd\tau

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2m}\int_{0}^{1}(u_{\eta,0}+r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta,0}^{\alpha})_{y}+\eta r^{N-1}(\rho_{\eta,0}^{\delta})_{y})^{2m}dy+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\alpha}u_{\eta}^{2m}dyd\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{\Omega_{\eta}}\rho_{\eta,0}|\mathbf{u}_{\eta,0}|^{2m}dx+\left\|\nabla\rho_{\eta,0}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2m}}\right\|_{L^{2m}(\Omega_{\eta})}^{2m}+\left\|\eta\rho_{\eta,0}^{\delta-2+\frac{1}{2m}}\nabla\rho_{\eta,0}\right\|_{L^{2m}(\Omega_{\eta})}^{2m}\right)+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\alpha}u_{\eta}^{2m}dyd\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|\nabla\rho_{\eta,0}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2m}}\right\|_{L^{2m}(\Omega_{\eta})}^{2m}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{\eta,0}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2m}}\right\|_{L^{2m}(\Omega_{\eta})}^{2m}\left\|\rho^{-1}_{\eta,0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{\eta})}^{2m(\alpha-\delta)}\eta^{2m}\right)+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\alpha}u_{\eta}^{2m}dyd\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|\nabla\rho_{\eta,0}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}\right\|_{L^{2m}(\Omega_{\eta})}^{2m}\left\|\rho_{\eta,0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{\eta})}^{1-\frac{m}{q}}\right)+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\alpha}u_{\eta}^{2m}dyd\tau

\displaystyle\leq C+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\alpha}u_{\eta}^{2m}dyd\tau.

Therefore, we complete the proof.
∎

We extend
(\rho_{\eta},\mathbf{u}_{\eta})
continuously from
\Omega_{\eta}\times[0,T]
to
\Omega\times[0,T]
. Specifically,

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{\eta}(r,t)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\rho_{\eta}(r,t),&\text{ if }\eta\leq r\leq R,\\
\rho_{\eta}(\eta,t),&\text{ if }0\leq r\leq\eta;\\
\end{array}\right.\quad\tilde{u}_{\eta}(r,t)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}u_{\eta}(r,t),&\text{ if }\eta\leq r\leq R,\\
0,&\text{ if }0\leq r\leq\eta.\\
\end{array}\right.

For brevity we omit the tilde notation. From now on,
(\rho_{\eta},\mathbf{u}_{\eta})
denotes the functions defined on
\Omega\times[0,T]
.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
N=2
. Assume that

\displaystyle\gamma>\alpha-\frac{1}{2}.

For any
1\leq s<\infty
, there exists a constant
C(s)>0
, independent of
\eta
and
T
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\rho_{\eta}\|_{L^{s}(\Omega)}\leq C(s).

(197)

Moreover, for any
0<\xi\ll 1
, there exists a constant
C(\xi)>0
, independent of
\eta
and
T
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}\leq C(\xi).

(198)

Proof.

We choose
2>\zeta>\frac{2\alpha-1}{\gamma}
. Using (
181
) and (
190
), the one-dimensional Sobolev embedding yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\zeta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}\leq C\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\zeta}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\zeta-1}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}|r^{\zeta}dr\\
&\leq C\int_{\eta}^{R}(\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma}r)^{\frac{2\alpha-1}{2\gamma}}r^{\zeta-\frac{2\alpha-1}{2\gamma}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}(\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma}r)^{\frac{2\alpha-1}{2\gamma}}r^{\zeta-1-\frac{2\alpha-1}{2\gamma}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}(|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}|^{2}r)^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\zeta-\frac{1}{2}}dr\\
&\leq C+C\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{\frac{2\gamma}{2\gamma-2\alpha+1}\zeta+\frac{-2\alpha+1}{2\gamma-2\alpha+1}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{\frac{2\gamma}{2\gamma-2\alpha+1}\zeta+\frac{-2\gamma-2\alpha+1}{2\gamma-2\alpha+1}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{2\zeta-1}dr\leq C,\end{split}

(199)

where we have used

\displaystyle\frac{2\gamma}{2\gamma-2\alpha+1}\zeta+\frac{-2\gamma-2\alpha+1}{2\gamma-2\alpha+1}>-1\Leftrightarrow\zeta>\frac{2\alpha-1}{\gamma}.

This implies

\displaystyle\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}(\eta,t)\leq C\eta^{-\zeta}.

(200)

Therefore, by the definition of
\rho_{\eta}
and (
181
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}&=\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{1}(\Omega_{\eta})}+\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{1}(\Omega-\Omega_{\eta})}\leq C+C\eta^{-\zeta+2}\leq C.\end{split}

(201)

Noting also that
\|\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}=\|\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\eta})}\leq C
, we apply the standard Sobolev embedding on
\Omega
to obtain a bound on
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\|_{L^{s}(\Omega)}
for any finite
s
, from which (
197
) follows.

We next prove (
198
). For sufficiently small
\xi>0
, using (
190
), (
197
) and Young’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\xi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}\leq C\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\xi}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\xi-1}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}|r^{\xi}dr\\
&\leq C\int_{\eta}^{R}(\rho_{\eta}^{(\alpha-\frac{1}{2})\frac{3}{\xi}}r)^{\frac{\xi}{3}}r^{\frac{2\xi}{3}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}(\rho_{\eta}^{(\alpha-\frac{1}{2})\frac{3}{\xi}}r)^{\frac{\xi}{3}}r^{\frac{2\xi-3}{3}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}(|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}|^{2}r)^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\xi-\frac{1}{2}}dr\\
&\leq C(\xi)+C\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{\frac{2\xi}{3-\xi}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{\frac{2\xi-3}{3-\xi}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{2\xi-1}dr\leq C(\xi).\end{split}

(202)

This completes the proof of Proposition
5.3
.
∎

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
N=3
. Assume that

\displaystyle\gamma>\alpha-\frac{1}{2}.

Then there exists a constant
C>0
, independent of
\eta
and
T
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\eta}\right\|_{L^{6\alpha-3}(\Omega)}\leq C.

(203)

For any
0<\xi\ll 1
, there exists a constant
C(\xi)>0
, independent of
\eta
and
T
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}\leq C(\xi).

(204)

Proof.

We choose
3>\zeta>\max\{\frac{6\alpha-3}{2\gamma},\frac{1}{2}\}
. Using (
181
) and (
190
), the one-dimensional Sobolev embedding yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\zeta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}\leq C\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\zeta}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\zeta-1}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}|r^{\zeta}dr\\
&\leq C\int_{\eta}^{R}(\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma}r^{2})^{\frac{2\alpha-1}{2\gamma}}r^{\zeta-\frac{2\alpha-1}{\gamma}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}(\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma}r^{2})^{\frac{2\alpha-1}{2\gamma}}r^{\zeta-1-\frac{2\alpha-1}{\gamma}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}(|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}|^{2}r^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\zeta-1}dr\\
&\leq C+C\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{\frac{2\gamma}{2\gamma-2\alpha+1}\zeta+\frac{-4\alpha+2}{2\gamma-2\alpha+1}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{\frac{2\gamma}{2\gamma-2\alpha+1}\zeta+\frac{-2\gamma-4\alpha+2}{2\gamma-2\alpha+1}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{2\zeta-2}dr\leq C,\end{split}

(205)

where we have used

\displaystyle\frac{2\gamma}{2\gamma-2\alpha+1}\zeta+\frac{-2\gamma-4\alpha+2}{2\gamma-2\alpha+1}>-1\Leftrightarrow\zeta>\frac{6\alpha-3}{2\gamma}.

This implies

\displaystyle\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}(\eta,t)\leq C\eta^{-\zeta}.

(206)

Therefore, by the definition of
\rho_{\eta}
and (
181
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}&=\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{1}(\Omega_{\eta})}+\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{1}(\Omega-\Omega_{\eta})}\leq C+C\eta^{-\zeta+3}\leq C.\end{split}

(207)

Note also that
\|\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}=\|\nabla\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\eta})}\leq C
. Applying the standard Sobolev embedding on
\Omega
, we obtain a bound on
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\|_{L^{6}(\Omega)}
, from which (
203
) follows.

We next prove (
204
). For sufficiently small
\xi>0
, using (
190
), (
203
) and Young’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\xi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}\leq C\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\xi}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\xi-\frac{1}{2}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}|r^{\frac{1}{2}+\xi}dr\\
&\leq C\int_{\eta}^{R}(\rho_{\eta}^{6\alpha-3}r^{2})^{\frac{1}{6}}r^{\frac{1}{6}+\xi}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}(\rho_{\eta}^{6\alpha-3}r^{2})^{\frac{1}{6}}r^{\xi-\frac{5}{6}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}(|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}|^{2}r^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\xi-\frac{1}{2}}dr\\
&\leq C+C\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{\frac{6}{5}\xi+\frac{1}{5}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{\frac{6}{5}\xi-1}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{2\xi-1}dr\leq C(\xi).\end{split}

This completes the proof of Proposition
5.4
.
∎

5.4
Upper bound and positive lower bound of
\rho_{\eta}

(N=2)

We first derive a Mellet–Vasseur-type estimate together with bounds on the derivative of the density.

Proposition 5.5
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.4
, there exists a constant
C(T)>0
independent of
\eta
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}u_{\eta}^{2p}rdr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\Big(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2p}}{r}+\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}(\partial_{r}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2p-2}r\Big)drdt\leq C(T)

(208)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}|^{2q}rdr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2q}}|^{2q}rdrdt\leq C(T).

(209)

Proof.

We first prove (
208
). Using (
198
), we obtain from (
182
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}u_{\eta}^{2p}rdr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\Big(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2p}}{r}+\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}(\partial_{r}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2p-2}r\Big)drdt\\
&\leq C+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{2p(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha}r^{2p-1}drdt\\
&\leq C+C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\xi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}^{\frac{2p(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha}{\alpha-1/2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{2p-1-\frac{2p(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha}{\alpha-1/2}\xi}drdt\\
&\leq C(T),\end{split}

where we have used

\displaystyle 2p(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha\geq 0\Longleftrightarrow\gamma\geq\left(1-\frac{1}{2p}\right)\alpha

and
\xi>0
is chosen sufficiently small so that

\displaystyle 2p-1-\frac{2p(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha}{\alpha-1/2}\xi>-1.

Next, we prove (
209
). Since
q\in M_{\text{set}}
, we apply (
191
) to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}|^{2q}rdr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2q}}|^{2q}rdrdt\\
&\leq\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}u_{\eta}^{2q}rdr+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}(u_{\eta}+\rho_{\eta}^{-1}\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}+\eta\rho^{-1}_{\eta}\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})^{2q}rdr\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2q}}|^{2q}rdrdt\\
&\leq C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}(u_{\eta}^{2p}+u_{\eta}^{2})rdr+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\alpha+1}u_{\eta}^{2q}rdrdt\\
&\leq C(T)+C\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}u_{\eta}^{2p}rdrdt\right)^{\frac{q}{p}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{(\gamma-\alpha)\frac{p}{p-q}+1}rdrdt\right)^{\frac{p-q}{p}}\\
&\leq C(T),\end{split}

where the last inequality follows from (
208
), (
197
) and the fact

\displaystyle(\gamma-\alpha)\frac{p}{p-q}+1\geq 0\Longleftrightarrow\gamma\geq\alpha-1+\frac{q}{p}.

This completes the proof of Proposition
5.5
.
∎

Finally, we establish the upper and positive lower bounds for the density in two dimensions. Denote that

\displaystyle\begin{split}R_{\eta,T}:=\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\eta}(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+1,\quad V_{\eta,T}:=\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{-1}(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+1.\end{split}

Proposition 5.6
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.4
, there exists a constant
C(T)>0
independent of
\eta
such that

\displaystyle R_{\eta,T}\leq C(T).

(210)

Proof.

Using (
197
) and (
209
), we obtain from the one-dimensional Sobolev embedding that

\displaystyle\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}\leq C\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}|dr

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}r^{\frac{1}{3}}r^{-\frac{1}{3}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}|\rho_{\eta}^{1-\frac{1}{2q}}dr

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{3\alpha}rdr\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{-\frac{1}{2}}dr\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}+C\left(\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}|^{2q}rdr\right)^{\frac{1}{2q}}\left(\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}r^{-\frac{1}{2q-1}}dr\right)^{\frac{2q-1}{2q}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)+C(T)\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}r^{-\frac{1}{2q-1}}dr

\displaystyle\leq C(T)+C(T)\left(\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\frac{2q}{q-1}}rdr\right)^{\frac{q-1}{2q}}\left(\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{-\frac{2q^{2}-q+1}{2q^{2}+q-1}}dr\right)^{\frac{q+1}{2q}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T).

This completes the proof of Proposition
5.6
.
∎

We then use the method in
[
14
]
to obtain a positive lower bound for
\rho_{\eta}
that depends on
\eta
.

Proposition 5.7
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.4
, there exists a constant
C(\eta,T)>0
such that

\displaystyle V_{\eta,T}\leq C(\eta,T).

(211)

Proof.

We claim that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta}^{\delta}|^{2p}dy\leq C(\eta,T).

(212)

Indeed, fixing
y\in[0,1]
and integrating
(
192
)
with respect to
\tau
from
0
to
\tau
gives

\displaystyle[r(\rho^{\alpha}_{\eta})_{y}+\eta r(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{y}](y,\tau)=[u_{\eta,0}+r(\rho^{\alpha}_{\eta,0})_{y}+\eta r(\rho_{\eta,0}^{\delta})_{y}](y)-u_{\eta}(y,\tau)-\int_{0}^{\tau}\partial_{y}(\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma})r(y,s)ds.

(213)

Multiplying the above equation by
[r(\rho^{\alpha}_{\eta})_{y}+\eta r(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{y}]^{2p-1}
and integrating over
[0,1]
, we obtain from (
208
) and Young’s inequality that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{1}[r(\rho^{\alpha}_{\eta})_{y}+\eta r(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{y}]^{2p}dy\\
&\leq C\left(\int_{0}^{1}[r(\rho^{\alpha}_{\eta})_{y}+\eta r(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{y}]^{2p}dy\right)^{\frac{2p-1}{2p}}\Big(\left\|u_{\eta,0}+r(\rho^{\alpha}_{\eta,0})_{y}+\eta r(\rho_{\eta,0}^{\delta})_{y}\right\|_{L^{2p}([0,1])}\\
&\quad+\left\|u_{\eta}\right\|_{L^{2p}([0,1])}+\Big(\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\|\partial_{y}(\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma})r\right\|_{L^{2p}([0,1])}^{2p}ds\Big)^{\frac{1}{2p}}\Big)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}[r(\rho^{\alpha}_{\eta})_{y}+\eta r(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{y}]^{2p}dy+C(\eta,T)+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\|\partial_{y}(\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma})r\right\|_{L^{2p}([0,1])}^{2p}ds.\end{split}

(214)

Therefore, we obtain from
\delta<1
,
r\geq\eta
and
(
210
)
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{1}[\eta r(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})_{y}]^{2p}dy&\leq C(\eta,T)+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\|\partial_{y}(\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma})r\right\|_{L^{2p}([0,1])}^{2p}ds\\
&\leq C(\eta,T)+C(\eta)\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([0,1])}^{2p}\left\|\partial_{y}(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})\right\|_{L^{2p}([0,1])}^{2p}ds\\
&\leq C(\eta,T)+C(\eta,T)\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\|\partial_{y}(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})\right\|_{L^{2p}([0,1])}^{2p}ds,\end{split}

(215)

which implies that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{y}(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})|^{2p}dy\leq C(\eta,T)+C(\eta,T)\int_{0}^{\tau}\left\|\partial_{y}(\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})\right\|_{L^{2p}([0,1])}^{2p}ds.

Using Gronwall’s inequality, we obtain (
212
).

We next establish the lower bound for the density. Set
v_{\eta}(y,\tau)=\frac{1}{\rho_{\eta}(y,\tau)}
. The continuity equation
\eqref{app system lag}_{1}
gives
(v_{\eta})_{\tau}=(ru_{\eta})_{y}
, so we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}v_{\eta}(y,\tau)dy=\int_{0}^{1}v_{\eta}(y,0)dy\leq C(\eta).

(216)

Then, by the one-dimensional Sobolev embedding,
(
212
)
and (
216
), one has

\displaystyle\begin{split}v_{\eta}(y,\tau)&\leq\int_{0}^{1}v_{\eta}(y,\tau)dy+\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{y}v_{\eta}(y,\tau)|dy\\
&\leq C(\eta)+C\int_{0}^{1}v_{\eta}^{1+\delta}|\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta}^{\delta}|dy\\
&\leq C(\eta)+C\left(\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta}^{\delta}|^{2p}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2p}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}v_{\eta}^{(1+\delta)\frac{2p}{2p-1}}dy\right)^{\frac{2p-1}{2p}}\\
&\leq C(\eta)+C(\eta,T)\left(\int_{0}^{1}v_{\eta}^{(1+\delta)\frac{2p}{2p-1}-1+1}dy\right)^{\frac{2p-1}{2p}}\\
&\leq C(\eta)+C(\eta,T)(V_{\eta,T})^{\delta+\frac{1}{2p}}.\end{split}

(217)

Taking the supremum of the above expression over
(y,\tau)\in[0,1]\times[0,T]
and applying Young’s inequality together with (
167
), we obtain (
211
).
∎

5.5
Upper bound and positive lower bound of
\rho_{\eta}

(N=3)

Proposition 5.8
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.5
, there exists a constant
0\leq\sigma<\min\{2\alpha-1,(3\alpha-2)(1-\frac{1}{q})\}
such that

\displaystyle\alpha-\frac{\alpha}{2p}+\sigma<\gamma<3\alpha-1+\frac{\alpha-1}{2p}+\sigma.

(218)

Furthermore, there exists a constant
C(T)>0
independent of
\eta
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}u_{\eta}^{2p}r^{2}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\Big(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}u_{\eta}^{2p}+\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}(\partial_{r}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2p-2}r^{2}\Big)drdt\leq C(T)R_{\eta,T}^{2p\sigma}

(219)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}|^{2q}r^{2}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2q}}|^{2q}r^{2}drdt\leq C(T)R_{\eta,T}^{2q\sigma}.

(220)

Proof.

It is readily verified that a constant
\sigma
satisfying the required condition exists, thanks to (
34
).

Next, we establish (
219
). We use (
182
), (
204
) and (
218
) to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}u_{\eta}^{2p}r^{2}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\Big(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}u_{\eta}^{2p}+\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}(\partial_{r}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2p-2}r^{2}\Big)drdt\\
&\leq C+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{2p(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha}r^{2p}drdt\\
&\leq C+C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\xi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}^{\frac{2p(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha-2p\sigma}{\alpha-1/2}}R_{\eta,T}^{2p\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{2p-\frac{2p(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha-2p\sigma}{\alpha-1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\xi\right)}drdt\\
&\leq C(T)R_{\eta,T}^{2p\sigma},\end{split}

where we have used

\displaystyle 2p(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha-2p\sigma>0\Longleftrightarrow\gamma>\alpha-\frac{\alpha}{2p}+\sigma

and
\xi>0
is a sufficiently small constant satisfying

\displaystyle 2p-\frac{2p(\gamma-\alpha)+\alpha-2p\sigma}{\alpha-1/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\xi\right)>-1.

Such a
\xi
exists because (
218
). We thus obtain (
219
).

Finally, we proof (
220
). Invoking (
204
), (
181
) and (
219
), one derives from (
191
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}|^{2q}r^{2}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2q}}|^{2q}r^{2}drdt\\
&\leq\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}u_{\eta}^{2q}r^{2}dr+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}(u_{\eta}+\rho_{\eta}^{-1}\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}+\eta\rho^{-1}_{\eta}\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})^{2q}r^{2}dr\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2q}}|^{2q}r^{2}drdt\\
&\leq\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}u_{\eta}^{2q}r^{2}dr+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\alpha+1}u_{\eta}^{2q}r^{2}drdt\\
&\leq\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}u_{\eta}^{2p}r^{2}dr\right)^{\frac{q}{p}}\left(\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}r^{2}dr\right)^{\frac{p-q}{p}}\\
&\quad+C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-2\alpha+1}r^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}u_{\eta}^{2p}drdt\right)^{\frac{q-1}{p-1}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}u_{\eta}^{2}drdt\right)^{\frac{p-q}{p-1}}\\
&\leq C(T)R_{\eta,T}^{2q\sigma}+C(T)\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-2\alpha+1}r^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}R_{\eta,T}^{2p\sigma\frac{q-1}{p-1}}\\
&\leq C(T)R_{\eta,T}^{2q\sigma}+C(T)\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\xi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}^{\frac{\gamma-2\alpha+1}{\alpha-1/2}}R_{\eta,T}^{2q\sigma}\leq C(T)R_{\eta,T}^{2q\sigma},\end{split}

where we have used

\displaystyle\gamma-2\alpha+1\geq 0,

and
\xi
is taken sufficiently small so that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\gamma-2\alpha+1}{\alpha-1/2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}+\xi\right)<2.

Such a
\xi
exists because
\gamma<6\alpha-3
. This completes the proof of Proposition
5.8
.
∎

Next, we derive an
\eta
-independent upper bound and an
\eta
-dependent positive lower bound for
\rho_{\eta}
in three dimensions.

Proposition 5.9
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.5
, there exists a constant
C(T)>0
independent of
\eta
such that

\displaystyle R_{\eta,T}\leq C(T).

(221)

Proof.

Choose
\beta
such that

\displaystyle\max\Bigl\{\sigma,\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}\Bigr\}<\beta<\min\Bigl\{2\alpha-1,(3\alpha-2)\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{q}\Bigr)\Bigr\},

(222)

where
\sigma
is given in Proposition
5.8
. Therefore, combining the one-dimensional Sobolev embedding with (
203
), (
204
), (
220
) and (
222
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\beta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}\leq C\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\beta}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\beta}|dr\\
&\leq C\int_{\eta}^{R}(\rho_{\eta}^{6\alpha-3}r^{2})^{\frac{\beta}{6\alpha-3}}r^{-\frac{2\beta}{6\alpha-3}}dr+C\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}|\rho_{\eta}^{\beta-\alpha+1-\frac{1}{2q}}dr\\
&\leq C\left(\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{6\alpha-3}r^{2}dr\right)^{\frac{\beta}{6\alpha-3}}\left(\int_{\eta}^{R}r^{-\frac{2\beta}{6\alpha-\beta-3}}dr\right)^{\frac{6\alpha-\beta-3}{6\alpha-3}}\\
&\quad+C\left(\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}|^{2q}r^{2}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{2q}}\left(\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\frac{2q}{2q-1}(\beta-\alpha)+1}r^{-\frac{2}{2q-1}}dr\right)^{\frac{2q-1}{2q}}\\
&\leq C+C(T)R_{\eta,T}^{\sigma}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\xi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}^{\frac{\beta-\alpha+\frac{2q-1}{2q}}{\alpha-1/2}}\left(\int_{0}^{R}r^{-\frac{2}{2q-1}-\left(\frac{1}{2}+\xi\right)\frac{\frac{2q}{2q-1}(\beta-\alpha)+1}{\alpha-1/2}}dr\right)^{\frac{2q-1}{2q}}\\
&\leq C(T)R_{\eta,T}^{\sigma},\end{split}

where
\xi
is taken sufficiently small so that

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{2q-1}-\left(\frac{1}{2}+\xi\right)\frac{\frac{2q}{2q-1}(\beta-\alpha)+1}{\alpha-1/2}>-1.

Such a
\xi
exists by virtue of (
222
). Applying Young’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle R_{\eta,T}^{\beta}\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\eta,T}^{\beta}+C(T),

which implies
(
221
)
.

This completes the proof of Proposition
5.9
.
∎

The approach to obtaining the
\eta
-dependent positive lower bound on the density is identical to the two-dimensional case, because away from the sphere center the system is essentially one-dimensional. Hence we only outline the proof.

Proposition 5.10
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.5
, there exists a constant
C(\eta,T)>0
such that

\displaystyle V_{\eta,T}\leq C(\eta,T).

(223)

Proof.

We consider two cases. In the first case
p\geq 1.55
, we have obtain from (
219
) and (
221
) that
\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\int_{0}^{1}u^{2p}_{\eta}dy\leq C(T)
, which yields
\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta}^{\delta}|^{2p}dy\leq C(\eta,T)
. We then obtain
V_{\eta,T}\leq C(\eta,T)
provided

\displaystyle\delta<1-\frac{1}{2p},

(224)

which is guaranteed by the definition of
\delta
in (
168
).

The second case is
p<1.55
. Using (
221
) and the simple fact that
1.55<\min\{n_{3}(\alpha),n_{3}(\delta)\}
, we obtain from (
183
) that

\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\int_{0}^{1}|u_{\eta}|^{3.1}dy\leq C(\eta,T)
, which yields

\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\int_{0}^{1}|\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta}^{\delta}|^{3.1}dy\leq C(\eta,T)
. Therefore, we obtain
V_{\eta,T}\leq C(\eta,T)
provided that

\displaystyle\delta<1-\frac{1}{3.1},

(225)

which is guaranteed by the definition of
\delta
in (
169
).

This completes the proof of Proposition
5.10
.
∎

5.6
Uniform upper bound of
\rho_{\eta}

(N=2,3)

In this subsection we establish a uniform upper bound for the density. We first show that the
L^{4}
-integrability of the velocity field can be established independently of
T
and
\eta
.

Proposition 5.11
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.6
, there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
T
and
\eta
such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}u_{\eta}^{4}dy+\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{4}}{r^{2}}+\rho^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2}r^{2(N-1)}\Big)dyd\tau\leq C.

(226)

Proof.

Multiplying
\eqref{app system lag}_{2}
by
r^{N-1}u_{\eta}^{3}
and integrating the resulting equation over
(0,1)
gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{0}^{1}u_{\eta}^{4}dy+\int_{0}^{1}(\alpha(N-1)^{2}-(N-1)(N-2))\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{4}}{r^{2}}+3\alpha\rho_{\eta}^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2}r^{2(N-1)}dy

\displaystyle\quad+\eta\left(\int_{0}^{1}(\delta(N-1)^{2}-(N-1)(N-2))\rho_{\eta}^{\delta-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{4}}{r^{2}}+3\delta\rho_{\eta}^{1+\delta}(\partial_{y}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2}r^{2(N-1)}dy\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\Big(3\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma}u_{\eta}^{2}\partial_{y}u_{\eta}r^{N-1}+(N-1)\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{3}}{r}\Big)dy+\int_{0}^{1}4(N-1)(1-\alpha)\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}u_{\eta}^{3}\partial_{y}u_{\eta}r^{N-2}dy

\displaystyle\quad+\eta\int_{0}^{1}4(N-1)(1-\delta)\rho_{\eta}^{\delta}u_{\eta}^{3}\partial_{y}u_{\eta}r^{N-2}dy.

From (
35
), (
36
), (
167
) and (
168
) we
see that
\min\{n_{N}(\alpha),n_{N}(\delta)\}>2
. Therefore, by Young’s inequality, we obtain a sufficiently small generic constant
\varepsilon>0
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\frac{1}{4}\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{0}^{1}u_{\eta}^{4}dy+2\varepsilon\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{4}}{r^{2}}+\rho_{\eta}^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2}r^{2(N-1)}dy\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma}u_{\eta}^{2}|\partial_{y}u_{\eta}|r^{N-1}dy+C\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-1}\frac{|u_{\eta}|^{3}}{r}dy\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\left(\rho_{\eta}^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2}r^{2(N-1)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\rho_{\eta}^{2\gamma-\alpha-1}u_{\eta}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}dy+C\int_{0}^{1}\left(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{4}}{r^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\rho_{\eta}^{2\gamma-\alpha-1}u_{\eta}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}dy\\
&\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2}r^{2(N-1)}dy+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{4}}{r^{2}}dy+C\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{2\gamma-\alpha-1}u_{\eta}^{2}dy.\end{split}

(227)

When
N=2
, using (
198
), we obtain, for sufficiently small
\xi
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad C\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{2\gamma-\alpha-1}u_{\eta}^{2}dy=C\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2}}{r^{2}}\rho_{\eta}^{2\gamma-2\alpha}r^{2}dy\\
&\leq C\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\xi}\right\|^{\frac{2(\gamma-\alpha)}{\alpha-1/2}}_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2}}{r^{2}}dy\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2}}{r^{2}}dy,\end{split}

where we used

\displaystyle\frac{2(\gamma-\alpha)}{\alpha-1/2}\geq 0\Longleftrightarrow\gamma\geq\alpha.

When
N=3
, we use (
204
) to get that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad C\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{2\gamma-\alpha-1}u_{\eta}^{2}dy=C\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2}}{r^{2}}\rho_{\eta}^{2\gamma-2\alpha}r^{2}dy\\
&\leq C\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\xi}\right\|^{\frac{2(\gamma-\alpha)}{\alpha-1/2}}_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2}}{r^{2}}dy\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2}}{r^{2}}dy,\end{split}

where we have used
\alpha\leq\gamma<3\alpha-1
to choose a sufficiently small
\xi
such that

\displaystyle 0\leq\left(\frac{1}{2}+\xi\right)\frac{2(\gamma-\alpha)}{\alpha-1/2}\leq 2.

Therefore, one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{0}^{1}u_{\eta}^{4}dy+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{4}}{r^{2}}+\rho_{\eta}^{1+\alpha}(\partial_{y}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2}r^{2(N-1)}\Big)dy\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1}\frac{u_{\eta}^{2}}{r^{2}}dy.

Integrating with respect to
\tau
and using (
181
) and (
172
) complete the proof.
∎

Proposition 5.12
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.6
, there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
T
and
\eta
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}u_{\eta}^{4}r^{N-1}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\Big(\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}u_{\eta}^{4}r^{N-3}+\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}(\partial_{r}u_{\eta})^{2}u_{\eta}^{2}r^{N-1}\Big)drdt\leq C

(228)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}|^{2q}r^{N-1}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2q}}|^{2q}r^{N-1}drdt\leq C.

(229)

Proof.

Rewriting (
226
) in Eulerian coordinates gives (
228
).

We next prove (
229
). Noting that
q\in M_{\mathrm{set}}
, so we can apply Proposition
5.2
to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}|^{2q}r^{N-1}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2q}}|^{2q}r^{N-1}drdt\\
&\leq\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}u_{\eta}^{2q}r^{N-1}dr+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}(u_{\eta}+\rho_{\eta}^{-1}\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}+\eta\rho^{-1}_{\eta}\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\delta})^{2q}r^{N-1}dr\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2q}}|^{2q}r^{N-1}drdt\\
&\leq\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}u_{\eta}^{2q}r^{N-1}dr+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-\alpha+1}u_{\eta}^{2q}r^{N-1}drdt\\
&\leq\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}u_{\eta}^{4}r^{N-1}dr\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\left(\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}r^{N-1}dr\right)^{\frac{2-q}{2}}\\
&\quad+C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-2\alpha+1}r^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}u_{\eta}^{4}r^{N-3}drdt\right)^{q-1}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\eta}^{R}\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha}u_{\eta}^{2}r^{N-3}drdt\right)^{2-q}\\
&\leq C+C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-2\alpha+1}r^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)},\end{split}

where we used (
181
), (
228
) and

\displaystyle\gamma\geq 2\alpha-1.

Therefore, it suffices to obtain (
229
) by estimating
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-2\alpha+1}r^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}
.

When
N=2
, using (
198
), we obtain,

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-2\alpha+1}r^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}\leq C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\xi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}^{\frac{\gamma-2\alpha+1}{\alpha-1/2}}\leq C,

where
\xi
is taken sufficiently small so that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\gamma-2\alpha+1}{\alpha-1/2}\right)\xi\leq 2.

When
N=3
, we use (
204
) to obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\gamma-2\alpha+1}r^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}\leq C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\xi}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\eta,R)}^{\frac{\gamma-2\alpha+1}{\alpha-1/2}}\leq C,

where we have used
\gamma<6\alpha-3
to choose a sufficiently small
\xi
such that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}+\xi\right)\frac{\gamma-2\alpha+1}{\alpha-1/2}\leq 2.

This completes the proof of Proposition
5.12
.
∎

Finally, we establish a uniform upper bound for the density.

Proposition 5.13
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.6
, there exist a constant
C>0
independent of
T
and
\eta
such that

\displaystyle R_{\eta,T}\leq C.

(230)

Proof.

We obtain from (
197
) and (
203
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}\right\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\leq C,

(231)

where
C
is independent of
T
and
\eta
. Using the standard Sobolev embedding, we obtain from (
229
) and (
231
) that

\displaystyle R_{\eta,T}\leq C.

We have completed the proof.
∎

5.7
Global solvability of the approximate system

Under the hypotheses of Theorem
2.4
or Theorem
2.5
, for any
0<T<T^{*}
, from (
210
), (
211
), (
221
) and (
223
) we conclude that
\rho_{\eta}(y,\tau)
is bounded from above and below on
[0,1]\times[0,T]
. From (
208
) and (
219
) we know that
u_{\eta}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2p}([0,1]))
, and from (
209
) and (
220
) we know that
\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2q}([0,1]))
. Furthermore, differentiating equation (
5.1
) and applying standard energy methods yields higher-order estimates for
(\rho_{\eta},u_{\eta})
. We then invoke Schauder theory for linear parabolic equations to conclude that the
C^{\beta,\beta/2}([0,1]\times[0,T])
-norms of
\rho_{\eta},\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta},\partial_{\tau y}\rho_{\eta},u_{\eta},\partial_{y}u_{\eta},\partial_{\tau}u_{\eta}
and
\partial_{yy}u_{\eta}
are bounded. Therefore, we can continue the local solution globally in time and obtain the unique global solution
(\rho_{\eta},u_{\eta})
of the initial-boundary-value problem (
5.1
) satisfying, for any
T>0
,

\displaystyle\rho_{\eta},\partial_{y}\rho_{\eta},\partial_{\tau y}\rho_{\eta},u_{\eta},\partial_{y}u_{\eta},\partial_{\tau}u_{\eta},\partial_{yy}u_{\eta}\in C^{\beta,\beta/2}([0,1]\times[0,T])

for some
0<\beta<1
, and
\rho_{\eta}>0
on
[0,1]\times[0,T]
. This can be done in a similar way as in
[
jiang-2001
]
.

5.8
Taking the limit and stability of the approximate solution sequence

With the uniform estimates for the approximate solutions in hand, we can study the stability of the approximate solution sequence. Indeed, the stability of approximate solution sequences in two and three dimensions for periodic domains and the whole space has already been investigated in
[
53
]
. The stability of the weak sequence for the approximate system (
5.1
) has also been proved in
[
14
]
. Our
\eta
-independent estimates for the approximate solutions are stronger than theirs, so the stability result for the approximate solution sequence follows easily. The proof in this subsection follows
[
53
]
and
[
14
]
. In this subsection we do not distinguish between two and three dimensions except where specifically stated.

Under the hypotheses of Theorems
2.4
and
2.5
, for any
T>0
, we obtained solutions
(\rho_{\eta},u_{\eta})
on
\Omega_{\eta}\times[0,T]
and extended them continuously to
\Omega\times[0,T]
. Consider a sequence
\eta_{j}\to 0
as
j\to\infty
. Write
\rho_{j}=\rho_{\eta_{j}}
,
\mathbf{u}_{j}=\mathbf{u}_{\eta_{j}}
, and set
\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}=\Omega-B_{\frac{1}{n}}(0)
for
n=1,2,\dots
. Let us recall the
\eta
-independent estimates (
181
), (
190
), (
208
)-(
210
) and (
219
)-(
221
) we have already obtained for the approximate solutions:

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\Big(\left\|\rho_{j}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+\left\|\nabla\rho_{j}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{j}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}\right\|_{L^{2q}(\Omega)}\Big)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\left\|\nabla\rho_{j}^{\frac{\gamma+\alpha-1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho_{j}^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2q}}\right\|_{L^{2q}(\Omega)}^{2q}\Big)dt\leq C(T),

(232)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\Omega}\Big(\rho_{j}|\mathbf{u}_{j}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\rho_{j}|\mathbf{u}_{j}|^{2p}\Big)dx+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\Big(\rho_{j}^{\alpha}|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j}|^{2}+\eta\rho_{j}^{\delta}|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j}|^{2}\Big)dxdt\leq C(T).

(233)

Proposition 5.14
.

There exists a subsequence of
\rho_{j}
, still denoted by itself, such that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and any
1\leq s<\infty
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\rho_{j}\rightarrow\rho,\text{ in }C(\overline{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{n}}\times[0,T]),\quad\rho_{j}\rightarrow\rho\text{ in }L^{s}(\Omega\times(0,T)),\end{split}

(234)

where
\rho
is a radially symmetric function and
\rho\in L^{\infty}(\Omega\times(0,T))\cap C((\overline{\Omega}\setminus\{0\})_{\text{loc}}\times[0,T])
.

Proof.

Fix
n
and choose
b\geq\alpha
. When
j
is sufficiently large, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{j}^{b}+\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\nabla\rho_{j}^{b}+b\rho_{j}^{b}\div\mathbf{u}_{j}=0,\text{ in }\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}.

Therefore, one has

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{j}^{b}=-\frac{2b}{2b-1}\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\nabla\rho_{j}^{b-\frac{1}{2}}-b\rho_{j}^{b-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\rho_{j}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\div\mathbf{u}_{j},

which implies that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{j}^{b}\text{ is bounded in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}})).

(235)

Moreover, since

\displaystyle|\nabla\rho_{j}^{b}|\leq C\rho_{j}^{b-\alpha+1-\frac{1}{2q}}|\nabla\rho_{j}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}|,

we have

\displaystyle|\nabla\rho_{j}^{b}|\text{ is bounded in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2q}(\Omega)).

(236)

The Aubin–Lions lemma, together with (
235
) and (
236
), shows that, up to a subsequence still denoted by itself,

\displaystyle\rho_{j}^{b}\rightarrow\rho^{b},\text{ in }C(\overline{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{n}}\times[0,T]).

For
n=1,2,\dots
, a standard diagonal argument allows us to extract a subsequence of
\rho_{j}
, still denoted by itself, such that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
it holds that

\displaystyle\rho^{b}_{j}\rightarrow\rho^{b},\text{ in }C(\overline{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{n}}\times[0,T]).

This shows that
\rho\in C((\overline{\Omega}\setminus\{0\})_{\text{loc}}\times[0,T])
and

\displaystyle\rho_{j}\rightarrow\rho,\text{ in }C(\overline{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{n}}\times[0,T]),\forall n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}.

Since the
\rho_{j}
are uniformly bounded and converge to
\rho
almost everywhere,
\rho
is also in
L^{\infty}(\Omega\times(0,T))
and its bound does not exceed the uniform bound of the
\rho_{j}
. Moreover, each
\rho_{j}
is radially symmetric, so the limit
\rho
is radially symmetric as well. Finally, it follows from the dominated convergence theorem that, for every
1\leq s<\infty
,

\displaystyle\rho_{j}\rightarrow\rho\text{ in }L^{s}(\Omega\times(0,T)).

We have completed the proof.
∎

Proposition 5.15
.

There exists a radially symmetric function
\mathbf{u}
such that, up to a subsequence,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\rightarrow\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u},\text{ in }L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,T)).

(237)

Proof.

We take
b
sufficiently large. Since

\displaystyle\nabla(\rho_{j}^{b}\mathbf{u}_{j})=\frac{2b}{2b-1}\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\nabla\rho_{j}^{b-\frac{1}{2}}+\rho_{j}^{b-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\rho_{j}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j},

it follows that

\displaystyle\nabla(\rho_{j}^{b}\mathbf{u}_{j})\text{ is bounded in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega)).

(238)

Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
. We claim that for sufficiently large
j
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\rho_{j}^{b}\mathbf{u}_{j})\text{ is bounded in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{-1,s^{\prime}}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}})),

(239)

where
s=\max\{p^{\prime},2N\}
. Indeed, a direct computation shows that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\rho_{j}^{b}\mathbf{u}_{j})=\partial_{t}(\rho_{j}\mathbf{u}_{j})\rho_{j}^{b-1}+\rho_{j}\mathbf{u}_{j}\partial_{t}(\rho_{j}^{b-1}),\text{ in }\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}.

(240)

We first consider the second term on the right-hand side of (
240
). For every
\bm{\psi}\in L^{2}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,s}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))
, one has

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\langle\rho_{j}\mathbf{u}_{j}\partial_{t}(\rho_{j}^{b-1}),\bm{\psi}\rangle|=\frac{b-1}{b}|\langle\partial_{t}(\rho_{j}^{b})\mathbf{u}_{j},\bm{\psi}\rangle|\\
&\leq\frac{b-1}{b}\left(|\langle\div(\rho_{j}^{b}\mathbf{u}_{j}),\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\bm{\psi}\rangle|+|\langle(b-1)\rho_{j}^{b}\div\mathbf{u}_{j},\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\bm{\psi}\rangle|\right)\\
&=\frac{b-1}{b}\left(|\langle\rho_{j}^{b}\mathbf{u}_{j},\nabla(\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\bm{\psi})\rangle|+|\langle(b-1)\rho_{j}^{b}\div\mathbf{u}_{j},\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\bm{\psi}\rangle|\right)\\
&\leq C(T)\left\|\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}\left\|\rho_{j}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}\left\|\bm{\psi}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}\\
&\quad+C(T)\left\|\rho_{j}|\mathbf{u}_{j}|^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{p}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}\left\|\nabla\bm{\psi}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{s}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}\\
&\leq C(T)\left\|\bm{\psi}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,s}_{0}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}.\end{split}

This shows that

\displaystyle\rho_{j}\mathbf{u}_{j}\partial_{t}(\rho_{j}^{b-1})\text{ is bounded in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{-1,s^{\prime}}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}})).

(241)

Next, we consider the second term on the right-hand side of (
240
). A direct calculation shows that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{t}(\rho_{j}\mathbf{u}_{j})\rho_{j}^{b-1}=&\Big(-\div(\rho_{j}\mathbf{u}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{j})-\nabla(\rho_{j}^{\gamma})+\div((\rho^{\alpha}_{j}+\eta_{j}\rho^{\delta}_{j})\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j})\\
&+\nabla(((\alpha-1)\rho_{j}^{\alpha}+\eta_{j}(\delta-1)\rho_{j}^{\delta})\div\mathbf{u}_{j})\Big)\times\rho_{j}^{b-1},\text{ in }\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}.\end{split}

(242)

For every
\bm{\psi}\in L^{2}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,s}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\langle\div(\rho_{j}\mathbf{u}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{j})\rho_{j}^{b-1},\bm{\psi}\rangle|=|\langle\rho_{j}\mathbf{u}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{j},\nabla(\rho_{j}^{b-1}\bm{\psi})\rangle|\\
&\leq\frac{b-1}{b}|\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{j},\nabla(\rho_{j}^{b})\otimes\bm{\psi}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{j},\rho_{j}^{b}\nabla\bm{\psi}\rangle|\\
&\leq\frac{b-1}{b}|\langle\div((\mathbf{u}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{j})\cdot\bm{\psi}),\rho_{j}^{b}\rangle|+|\langle\mathbf{u}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{j},\rho_{j}^{b}\nabla\bm{\psi}\rangle|\\
&\leq C(T)\left\|\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}\left\|\rho_{j}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}\left\|\bm{\psi}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}\\
&\quad+C(T)\left\|\rho_{j}|\mathbf{u}_{j}|^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{p}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}\left\|\nabla\bm{\psi}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{s}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}\\
&\leq C(T)\left\|\bm{\psi}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,s}_{0}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}.\end{split}

This implies that

\displaystyle-\div(\rho_{j}\mathbf{u}_{j}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{j})\rho_{j}^{b-1}\text{ is bounded in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{-1,s^{\prime}}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}})).

(243)

It is easily verified that

\displaystyle\nabla(\rho_{j}^{\gamma})\rho_{j}^{b-1}\text{ is bounded in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)).

(244)

For every
\bm{\psi}\in L^{2}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,s}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))
, it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\langle\div((\rho^{\alpha}_{j}+\eta_{j}\rho^{\delta}_{j})\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j})\rho_{j}^{b-1},\bm{\psi}\rangle|=|\langle((\rho^{\alpha}_{j}+\eta_{j}\rho^{\delta}_{j})\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j}),\nabla(\rho_{j}^{b-1}\bm{\psi})\rangle|\\
&\leq C(T)\left\|\nabla\rho_{j}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}\left\|\rho_{j}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}\left\|\bm{\psi}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}\\
&\quad+C(T)\left\|\rho_{j}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}\left\|\nabla\bm{\psi}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{s}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}\\
&\leq C(T)\left\|\bm{\psi}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,s}_{0}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}}))}.\end{split}

This implies that

\displaystyle\div((\rho^{\alpha}_{j}+\eta_{j}\rho^{\delta}_{j})\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j})\rho_{j}^{b-1}\text{ is bounded in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{-1,s^{\prime}}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}})).

(245)

Similarly,

\displaystyle\nabla(((\alpha-1)\rho_{j}^{\alpha}+\eta_{j}(\delta-1)\rho_{j}^{\delta})\div\mathbf{u}_{j})\rho_{j}^{b-1}\text{ is bounded in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{-1,s^{\prime}}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}})).

(246)

Therefore, substituting (
243
)–(
246
) into (
242
) gives

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\rho_{j}\mathbf{u}_{j})\rho_{j}^{b-1}\text{ is bounded in }L^{2}(0,T;W^{-1,s^{\prime}}(\Omega_{\frac{1}{n}})),

(247)

which together with (
241
), shows that (
239
).

Therefore, by the Aubin–Lions lemma together with (
238
) and (
239
), there exists a subsequence of
\rho_{j}^{b}\mathbf{u}_{j}
, still denoted by itself, such that for any
1\leq s<\tfrac{3}{2}
,

\displaystyle\rho_{j}^{b}\mathbf{u}_{j}\to\overline{\rho^{b}\mathbf{u}}\quad\text{in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{s}(\Omega_{\tfrac{1}{n}})).

For
n=1,2,\dots
we apply the standard diagonal argument to extract a subsequence
\rho_{j}^{b}\mathbf{u}_{j}
, still denoted by itself, such that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and
1\leq s<\frac{3}{2}

\displaystyle\rho_{j}^{b}\mathbf{u}_{j}\to\overline{\rho^{b}\mathbf{u}}\quad\text{in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{s}(\Omega_{\tfrac{1}{n}})).

(248)

We define

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{\overline{\rho^{b}\mathbf{u}}}{\rho^{b}},&\text{ in }\{\rho>0\};\\
0,&\text{ in }\{\rho=0\}.\end{array}\right.

We obtain from (
248
) that
\rho_{j}^{b}\mathbf{u}_{j}\to\overline{\rho^{b}\mathbf{u}}
a.e. in
\Omega\times(0,T)
, which implies
\mathbf{u}_{j}\to\mathbf{u}
a.e. in
\{\rho>0\}
. Noting that each
\mathbf{u}_{j}
is radially symmetric, we conclude that
\mathbf{u}
is also radially symmetric.

Finally, we prove (
237
). By Fatou’s lemma, one has

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{2p}dxdt=\iint_{\{\rho>0\}}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{2p}dxdt\leq\liminf_{j}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho_{j}|\mathbf{u}_{j}|^{2p}dxdt\leq C(T).

(251)

Given a sufficiently large
M
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}-\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}|^{2}dxdt

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\chi_{\{|\mathbf{u}_{j}|>M\}}|^{2}dxdt

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\chi_{\{|\mathbf{u}_{j}|\leq M\}}-\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\chi_{\{|\mathbf{u}|\leq M\}}|^{2}dxdt

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\chi_{\{|\mathbf{u}|>M\}}|^{2}dxdt:=\sum_{i=1}^{3}I_{i}.

From (
233
) and (
251
) we obtain

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{3}\leq\frac{C}{M^{2p-2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho_{j}|\mathbf{u}_{j}|^{2p}dxdt+\frac{C}{M^{2p-2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{2p}dxdt\leq\frac{C(T)}{M^{2p-2}}.

Therefore, for any given
\varepsilon>0
, we can choose
M
sufficiently large such that
I_{1}+I_{3}\leq 2\varepsilon
, and then, by the dominated convergence theorem, pick
j
sufficiently large so that
I_{2}\leq\varepsilon
. Therefore, we finish the proof.
∎

We now show that the radially symmetric pair
(\rho,\mathbf{u})
is a weak solution in the sense of Definition
2.7
.

Proposition 5.16
.

The pair
(\rho,\mathbf{u})
possesses the regularity stated in (
22
).

Proof.

We obtain from (
232
) that

\displaystyle\nabla\rho_{j}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\rightharpoonup\nabla\rho^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}},\text{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)),

(252)

\displaystyle\nabla\rho_{j}^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}\rightharpoonup\nabla\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}},\text{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T,L^{2q}(\Omega)).

(253)

From (
233
) and (
237
) we obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\rightharpoonup\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u},\text{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T,L^{2}(\Omega)).

Proposition
5.14
implies
\rho\in L^{\infty}(\Omega\times(0,T))
.
Verifying that
\rho^{\alpha}\nabla\mathbf{u}\in L^{2}(0,T;W^{-1,1}_{\text{loc}}(\Omega))
is straightforward from its definition (
27
). Therefore, we have completed the proof.
∎

Proposition 5.17
.

The weak form (
23
) of the continuity equation holds.

Proof.

We know that equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{j}+\div(\rho_{j}\mathbf{u}_{j})=0

(254)

holds on
\Omega_{j}
. For arbitrary
t_{2}\geq t_{1}\geq 0
and
\zeta\in C^{1}(\overline{\Omega}\times[t_{1},t_{2}])
, multiplying the equation by
\zeta
and integrating by parts gives

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{j}}\rho_{j}\zeta dx|_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\rho_{j}\partial_{t}\zeta+\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\nabla\zeta dxdt.

(255)

Letting
j\to\infty
and using (
234
) and (
237
), we obtain (
23
).
∎

Proposition 5.18
.

The weak form (
24
) of the renormalized continuity equation holds.

Proof.

Let
b\geq\alpha
. On
\Omega_{j}
,
(\rho_{j},\mathbf{u}_{j})
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{j}^{b}+\div(\rho_{j}^{b}\mathbf{u}_{j})+(b-1)\rho_{j}^{b}\div\mathbf{u}_{j}=0.

(256)

For arbitrary
t_{2}\geq t_{1}\geq 0
and
\phi\in C^{1}(\overline{\Omega}\times[t_{1},t_{2}])
, multiplying the equation by
\phi
and integrating by parts gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\rho_{j}^{b}\phi dx\left|{}_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\right.=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\left(\rho_{j}^{b}\partial_{t}\phi+\frac{2b(b-1)}{2b-1}\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\nabla\rho_{j}^{b-\frac{1}{2}}\phi+b\rho_{j}^{b-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\nabla\phi\right)dxdt.\end{split}

Letting
j\to\infty
and using (
234
) and (
237
), we obtain (
24
).
∎

Proposition 5.19
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\rho\in C(\overline{\Omega}\times[0,T]).

Proof.

Equation
(
24
)
shows that for any
\phi\in C^{1}(\overline{\Omega})
we have for any
b\geq\alpha
,

\displaystyle\langle\partial_{t}\rho^{b},\phi\rangle=\int_{\Omega}\frac{2b(b-1)}{2b-1}\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\rho^{b-\frac{1}{2}}\phi+b\rho^{b-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\phi dx,\text{ in }\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(0,T).

(257)

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\langle\partial_{t}\rho^{b},\phi\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq C(b)\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|\nabla\rho^{b-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2q}(\Omega)}\left\|\phi\right\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\quad+C(b)\left\|\rho^{b-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|\phi\right\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\leq C(b,T)\left\|\phi\right\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)},

which shows that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho^{b}\text{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;(H^{1}(\Omega))^{*}).

Combined with the spatial-derivative estimate
\rho^{b}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{1,2q}(\Omega))
, the Aubin–Lions lemma yields
\rho^{b}\in C(\overline{\Omega}\times[0,T])
, which implies
\rho\in C(\overline{\Omega}\times[0,T])
.
∎

Proposition 5.20
.

The weak form (
26
) of the momentum equation holds.

Proof.

The approximate solutions satisfy the momentum equation: for any
\bm{\psi}\in C^{2}(\overline{\Omega}\times[0,T])
such that
\bm{\psi}(x,t)=0
on
\partial\Omega
and
\bm{\psi}(x,T)=0
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}0&=\int_{\Omega_{j}}\rho_{j,0}\mathbf{u}_{j,0}\cdot\bm{\psi}(x,0)dx+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\partial_{t}\bm{\psi}+\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\otimes\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}:\nabla\bm{\psi}+\rho_{j}^{\gamma}\div\bm{\psi}dxdt\\
&\quad-(\langle\rho_{j}^{\alpha}\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j},\nabla\bm{\psi}\rangle+(\alpha-1)\langle\rho_{j}^{\alpha}\div\mathbf{u}_{j},\div\bm{\psi}\rangle)-\eta_{j}(\langle\rho_{j}^{\delta}\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j},\nabla\bm{\psi}\rangle+(\delta-1)\langle\rho_{j}^{\delta}\div\mathbf{u}_{j},\div\bm{\psi}\rangle)\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\partial\Omega_{j}}-\bm{\psi}\cdot\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\otimes\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\mathbf{n}-\rho_{j}^{\gamma}\bm{\psi}\cdot\mathbf{n}dSdt\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\partial\Omega_{j}}(\rho_{j}^{\alpha}+\eta_{j}\rho_{j}^{\delta})\bm{\psi}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\mathbf{n}+((\alpha-1)\rho_{j}^{\alpha}+\eta_{j}(\delta-1)\rho_{j}^{\delta})\div\mathbf{u}_{j}\bm{\psi}\cdot\mathbf{n}dSdt=\sum_{k=1}^{6}K^{j}_{k}.\end{split}

(258)

Using (
172
), (
170
) and (
171
), we obtain

\displaystyle K_{1}^{j}\to\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{m}(0)\cdot\bm{\psi}(x,0)dx,\text{ as }j\rightarrow\infty.

(259)

From (
234
) and (
237
) we obtain

\displaystyle K_{2}^{j}\to\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\sqrt{\rho}\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\cdot\partial_{t}\bm{\psi}+\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\otimes\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}:\nabla\bm{\psi}+\rho^{\gamma}\div\bm{\psi}dxdt,\text{ as }j\to\infty.

(260)

The diffusion terms of the approximate system can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle\rho_{j}^{\alpha}\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j},\nabla\bm{\psi}\rangle&=-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\rho_{j}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\Delta\bm{\psi}dxdt\\
&\quad-\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha-1}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\nabla\rho_{j}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\nabla\bm{\psi}\cdot\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}dxdt;\\
\langle\rho_{j}^{\alpha}\div\mathbf{u}_{j},\div\bm{\psi}\rangle&=-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{j}}\rho_{j}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\nabla\div\bm{\psi}dxdt\\
&\quad-\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha-1}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\nabla\rho_{j}^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\rho_{j}}\mathbf{u}_{j}\div\bm{\psi}dxdt.\end{split}

Therefore, using (
234
), (
237
) and (
252
), we obtain

\displaystyle K_{3}^{j}\to-\langle\rho^{\alpha}\nabla\mathbf{u},\nabla\bm{\psi}\rangle-(\alpha-1)\langle\rho^{\alpha}\div\mathbf{u},\div\bm{\psi}\rangle,\text{ as }j\to\infty,

(261)

where the diffusion terms are defined as in (
27
). For the diffusion terms arising from the artificial viscosity, we get from (
233
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}|K_{4}^{j}|\leq C\sqrt{\eta_{j}}\sqrt{\eta_{j}}\left\|\rho_{\eta}^{\frac{\delta}{2}}\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{j}))}\left\|\nabla\bm{\psi}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega_{j}))}\leq C\sqrt{\eta_{j}}\to 0,\text{ as }j\to\infty.\end{split}

(262)

Finally, we only need to handle the boundary terms. Noting that
\mathbf{u}_{j}|_{\partial\Omega_{j}}=0
, we have

\displaystyle|K_{5}^{j}|\leq\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\partial\Omega_{j}}\rho_{j}^{\gamma}\bm{\psi}\cdot\mathbf{n}dSdt\right|\leq C(T)\eta_{j}^{N-1}\rightarrow 0,\text{ as }j\to\infty.

(263)

Noting that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{j}^{\alpha}+\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\nabla\rho_{j}^{\alpha}+\alpha\rho_{j}^{\alpha}\div\mathbf{u}_{j}=0,

(264)

we therefore have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\partial\Omega_{j}}(\alpha-1)\rho_{j}^{\alpha}\div\mathbf{u}_{j}\bm{\psi}\cdot\mathbf{n}dSdt\right|=\left|\frac{\alpha-1}{\alpha}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\partial\Omega_{j}}\partial_{t}\rho_{j}^{\alpha}\bm{\psi}\cdot\mathbf{n}dSdt\right|\\
&=\left|\frac{\alpha-1}{\alpha}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega_{j}}\rho_{j}^{\alpha}\bm{\psi}\cdot\mathbf{n}dS(T)-\int_{\partial\Omega_{j}}\rho_{j}^{\alpha}\bm{\psi}\cdot\mathbf{n}dS(0)-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\partial\Omega_{j}}\rho_{j}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\bm{\psi}\cdot\mathbf{n}dSdt\right)\right|\\
&\leq C(T)\eta_{j}^{N-1}\rightarrow 0.\end{split}

(265)

Similarly,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\partial\Omega_{j}}\eta_{j}(\delta-1)\rho_{j}^{\delta}\div\mathbf{u}_{j}\bm{\psi}\cdot\mathbf{n}dSdt\right|\rightarrow 0.

(266)

Noting the following simple fact:

\displaystyle\bm{\psi}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\mathbf{n}|_{\partial\Omega_{j}}=\bm{\psi}\cdot\nabla(\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\mathbf{n})|_{\partial\Omega_{j}}=\partial_{r}u_{j}\bm{\psi}\cdot\mathbf{n}|_{\partial\Omega_{j}}=\div\mathbf{u}_{j}\bm{\psi}\cdot\mathbf{n}|_{\partial\Omega_{j}}

This implies that, similarly to (
265
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\partial\Omega_{j}}(\rho_{j}^{\alpha}+\eta_{j}\rho_{j}^{\delta})\bm{\psi}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u}_{j}\cdot\mathbf{n}dSdt\right|\to 0,\text{ as }j\rightarrow\infty.

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle|K_{6}^{j}|\rightarrow 0,\text{ as }j\to\infty.

(267)

Letting
j\to\infty
and using (
259
)-(
263
) and (
267
), we obtain (
26
) from (
258
). We have completed the proof.
∎

5.9
Finite-time vacuum vanishing

In this subsection we investigate the finite-time vanishing of the vacuum region. Assuming the conditions of Theorem
2.6
hold, let
(\rho,\mathbf{u})
be the weak solution on
\Omega\times[0,\infty)
obtained in Theorem
2.5
. From (
181
), (
230
), (
190
) and (
229
) we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}&\left(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{1}{2q}}\right\|_{L^{2q}(\Omega)}\right)\\
&+\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{\gamma+\alpha-1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{\alpha-1+\frac{\gamma-\alpha+1}{2q}}\right\|_{L^{2q}(\Omega)}^{2q}\Big)dt\leq C.\end{split}

(268)

Proposition 5.21
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.6
, there exist constants
T_{0}>0
and
\rho^{-}>0
, such that

\displaystyle\rho\geq\rho^{-},\quad\forall(x,t)\in\Omega\times[T_{0},\infty).

(269)

Proof.

We choose
b>1
sufficiently large so that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq t\leq\infty}&\left(\left\|\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{b-1}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{b}\right\|_{L^{2q}(\Omega)}\right)\\
&+\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|\nabla\rho^{b-1}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{b}\right\|_{L^{2q}(\Omega)}^{2q}dt\leq C.\end{split}

(270)

By the embedding inequality we have

\displaystyle\left\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right\|_{C(\overline{\Omega})}\leq C\left\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}^{\frac{4q-2N}{4q-2N+Nq}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{b}\right\|_{L^{2q}(\Omega)}^{\frac{Nq}{4q-2N+Nq}}\leq C\left\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}^{\frac{4q-2N}{4q-2N+Nq}}\rightarrow 0,

(271)

if

\displaystyle g(t)=\left\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right\|_{L^{4}(\Omega)}^{4}\rightarrow 0,\text{ as }t\rightarrow\infty.

(272)

Then, by Jensen’s inequality we obtain

\displaystyle\overline{\rho^{b}}\geq\overline{\rho}^{b}=\overline{\rho_{0}}^{b}>0.

Together with (
271
), this shows that there exist
\rho^{-}
and
T_{0}
for which (
269
) holds. Thus it remains only to verify that (
272
) holds.

We first show that
\int_{0}^{\infty}|g^{\prime}(t)|dt\leq C
. A direct computation gives

\displaystyle g^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=4b\left\langle(\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}})^{3}\rho^{b-1},\rho_{t}\right\rangle-4(\overline{\rho^{b}})_{t}\int_{\Omega}(\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}})^{3}dx

\displaystyle=-4b\left\langle\nabla\left((\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}})^{3}\rho^{b-1}\right),\sqrt{\rho}\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\rangle-4(\overline{\rho^{b}})_{t}\int_{\Omega}(\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}})^{3}dx:=I_{1}+I_{2}.

Using (
270
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\infty}|I_{1}|dt\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Omega}|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}|^{2}|\nabla\rho^{b}|\rho^{b-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\rho}|\mathbf{u}|+\left|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right|^{3}|\nabla\rho^{b-1}|\sqrt{\rho}\sqrt{\rho}|\mathbf{u}|dxdt\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|\nabla\rho^{b}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}^{q}+\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|\nabla\rho^{b-1}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}^{q}\right)dt\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\left\|\nabla\rho^{b-1}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{b}\right\|_{L^{2q}(\Omega)}^{2q}\right)dt\leq C.\end{split}

(273)

Note that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}\left|\frac{d}{dt}\overline{\rho^{b}}\right|

\displaystyle=b\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}\left|\langle\rho^{b-1},\rho_{t}\rangle\right|=b\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}\left|\int_{\Omega}\nabla\rho^{b-1}\sqrt{\rho}\cdot\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}dx\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}\left\|\nabla\rho^{b-1}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}|I_{2}|dt\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}dt\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|\nabla\rho^{b}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}dt\leq C.

(274)

Finally, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}g(t)dt\leq C\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}\left\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\rho^{b}-\overline{\rho^{b}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}dt\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|\nabla\rho^{b}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}dt\leq C.

(275)

The estimates (
273
)–(
275
) imply (
272
), and the proof of Proposition
5.21
is complete.
∎

6
Global weak solutions with possible vacuum at the origin
(\alpha=1)

6.1
Construction of the approximate system II

We begin by introducing the approximate system for (
1
)–(
3
) with sufficiently small
\iota>0
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\rho_{\iota}+\div(\rho_{\iota}\mathbf{u}_{\iota})=0,\\
\partial_{t}(\rho_{\iota}\mathbf{u}_{\iota})+\div(\rho_{\iota}\mathbf{u}_{\iota}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{\iota})+\nabla(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})=\div(\rho_{\iota}\nabla\mathbf{u}_{\iota}),\\
(\rho_{\iota},\mathbf{u}_{\iota})|_{t=0}=(\rho_{\iota,0},\mathbf{u}_{\iota,0}),\\
\mathbf{u}_{\iota}|_{\partial\Omega_{\iota}}=0,\end{array}\right.

where
t\geq 0
and
x\in\Omega_{\iota}=\Omega-B_{\iota}
. Next, we specify the initial data
(\rho_{\iota,0},\mathbf{u}_{\iota,0})
of the approximate system. Without loss of generality, assume
\int_{\Omega}\rho_{0}\,dx=1
. Extend
\rho_{\iota,0}
constantly to
\mathbb{R}^{N}
and define

\displaystyle\rho_{\iota,0}=C_{\iota}\rho_{0}*j_{\varepsilon(\iota)},\text{ in }\Omega,

where
C_{\iota}
is a constant chosen so that
\int_{\Omega_{\iota}}\rho_{\iota,0}\,dx=1
and
j_{\varepsilon(\iota)}
is the standard mollifier. We then define

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{\iota,0}=(\mathbf{u}_{0}\alpha_{\iota})*j_{\varepsilon(\iota)},\text{ in }\Omega,

where
\alpha_{\iota}(\cdot)
is a smooth cut-off function such that
\alpha_{\iota}(x)=0
whenever
0\leq|x|\leq 2\iota
or
|x|\geq R-\iota
, and
\alpha(x)=1
when
3\iota\leq|x|\leq R-2\iota
, and
|\nabla\alpha_{\iota}|\leq\frac{C}{\iota}
. One verifies that the initial data of the approximate system possess the following properties:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho_{\iota,0}\rightarrow\rho_{0}\text{ in }W^{1,{2q}}(\Omega),\quad\nabla\rho_{\iota,0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\rightarrow\nabla\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\text{ in }L^{2}(\Omega),\quad\rho_{\iota,0}\geq C(\underline{\rho_{0}})>0,\\
&\nabla\rho_{\iota,0}^{\frac{1}{2q}}\rightarrow\nabla\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2q}}\text{ in }L^{2q}(\Omega),\quad\rho_{\iota,0}|\mathbf{u}_{\iota,0}|^{2p}\rightarrow\rho_{0}|\mathbf{u}_{0}|^{2p}\text{ in }L^{1}(\Omega),\\
&\rho_{\iota,0}|\mathbf{u}_{\iota,0}|^{2}\rightarrow\rho_{0}|\mathbf{u}_{0}|^{2}\text{ in }L^{1}(\Omega),\quad\mathbf{u}_{\iota,0}|_{\partial\Omega_{\iota}}=0,\end{split}

(280)

where
C(\underline{\rho_{0}})
is a constant depending only on
\underline{\rho_{0}}
. Moreover, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
, when
\iota
is sufficiently small, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\rho_{\iota,0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega\backslash B_{1/n})}\leq C,

(281)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{u}_{\iota,0}\right\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\backslash B_{1/n})}+\left\|\mathbf{u}_{\iota,0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega\backslash B_{1/n})}\leq C,

(282)

where
C
is independent of
\iota
due to (
39
).

In spherical coordinates, the approximate system (
6.1
) takes the following form
