\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\rho_{\iota})_{t}+(\rho_{\iota}u_{\iota})_{r}+\frac{N-1}{r}\rho_{\iota}u_{\iota}=0,\\
\rho_{\iota}(u_{\iota})_{t}+\rho_{\iota}u_{\iota}(u_{\iota})_{r}+(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})_{r}-\left(\frac{\rho_{\iota}}{r^{N-1}}(r^{N-1}u_{\iota})_{r}\right)_{r}+\frac{N-1}{r}(\rho_{\iota})_{r}u_{\iota}=0,\\
(\rho_{\iota},u_{\iota})|_{t=0}=(\rho_{\iota,0},u_{\iota,0}),\\
u_{\iota}(\iota,t)=u_{\iota}(R,t)=0,\end{array}\right.

where
t\geq 0
and
r\in(\iota,R)
.

Define
y(r,t)=\int_{\iota}^{r}\rho_{\iota}(s,t)s^{N-1}ds
and
\tau(r,t)=t
. Then, in Lagrangian coordinates, the approximate system (
6.1
) becomes

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\rho_{\iota})_{\tau}+\rho^{2}_{\iota}(r^{N-1}u_{\iota})_{y}=0,\\
r^{-(N-1)}(u_{\iota})_{\tau}+(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})_{y}-(\rho_{\iota}^{2}(r^{N-1}u_{\iota})_{y})_{y}+\frac{N-1}{r}(\rho_{\iota})_{y}u_{\iota}=0,\\
(\rho_{\iota},u_{\iota})|_{\tau=0}=(\rho_{\iota,0},u_{\iota,0}),\\
u_{\iota}(0,\tau)=u_{\iota}(1,\tau)=0,\end{array}\right.

where
\tau\geq 0
and
y\in(0,1)
.

6.2
Global existence of weak solutions

As in Section
5
, we investigate the global solvability of the approximate system (
6.1
) in Lagrangian coordinates. Let
(\rho_{\iota},u_{\iota})
be a uniqule local classical solution of system (
6.1
) defined on
[0,1]\times[0,T]
for some fixed
T>0
. To show that the approximate solution exists globally in time, it suffices to obtain an upper bound and a positive lower bound for
\rho_{\iota}
.

We first establish the standard energy estimate and the B-D entropy estimate. Indeed, it suffices to note that
n_{N}(1)=+\infty
and to use the properties of the initial data
(\rho_{0,\iota},\mathbf{u}_{0,\iota})
stated in (
280
). Repeating the proofs of Propositions
5.1
and
5.2
, we obtain the following Proposition.

Proposition 6.1
.

Assume that (
38
) holds. Then there exists a constant
C>0
, independent of
\iota
and
\tau
, such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}(u_{\iota}^{2}+\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma-1})dy+\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\frac{u_{\iota}^{2}}{r^{2}}+\rho_{\iota}^{2}(\partial_{y}{u_{\iota}})^{2}r^{2(N-1)}\Big)dyd\tau\leq C,

(291)

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}(r^{N-1}(\rho_{\iota})_{y})^{2}dy+\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma-1}(r^{N-1}(\rho_{\iota})_{y})^{2}dyd\tau\leq C.

(292)

Next, using the facts that
n_{N}(1)=+\infty
,
q\in M_{\mathrm{set}}
, and that (
280
) holds, and following the proofs of Propositions
5.5
–
5.9
, we obtain higher integrability of the velocity field and of the density gradient, together with an upper bound on the density.

Proposition 6.2
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.7
, there exists a constant
C(T)>0
, independent of
\iota
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\int_{0}^{1}u_{\iota}^{2p}dy+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\frac{u_{\iota}^{2p}}{r^{2}}+\rho_{\iota}^{2}(\partial_{y}u_{\iota})^{2}u_{\iota}^{2p-2}r^{2(N-1)}\Big)dyd\tau\leq C(T)

(293)

as well as

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\int_{0}^{1}(r^{N-1}(\rho_{\iota})_{y})^{2q}dy+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma-1}(r^{N-1}(\rho_{\iota})_{y})^{2q}dyd\tau\leq C(T)

(294)

and finally,

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\iota}(\tau)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}\leq C(T).

(295)

Finally, we derive a positive lower bound for
\rho_{\iota}
that depends on
\iota
. It should be noted that Proposition
6.3
below can be obtained by a slight modification of the proof of Proposition
6.4
, or the reader may refer to
[
15
]
. To avoid repetition, we omit the proof of Proposition
6.3
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.7
, there exists a constant
C(\iota,T)>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left\|\rho^{-1}_{\iota}(\tau)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}\leq C(\iota,T).

(296)

With (
295
) and (
296
) at hand, we can argue in a standard way that
(\rho_{\iota},u_{\iota})
is a global classical solution on
[0,1]\times[0,\infty)
; see the previous section or
[
14
]
for details. Using the
\iota
-independent estimates (
293
)–(
295
) and standard compactness arguments, we establish the global existence of the weak solution stated in Theorem
2.7
; see Section
5
or
[
14
]
for details.

6.3
Some
\iota
-independent estimates

In this section we follow the ideas of
[
24
]
to derive several
\iota
-independent estimates, which play a key role in characterizing the vacuum states and the regularity of the solution in the flow region. To this end, we fix a sufficiently small constant
h>0
. Then, for any
\iota>0
, we define the particle path
r_{h}^{\iota}(t)
by

h=\int_{\iota}^{r_{h}^{\iota}(t)}\rho_{\iota}(r,t)r^{N-1}dr,

(297)

where
\rho_{\iota}(r,t)
is the global classical solution to the approximate system (
6.1
) defined on
[\iota,R]\times[0,\infty)
. From (
291
), one can easily check that there exists a constant
C(h)>0
, independent of
\iota
and
t
, satisfying

\displaystyle r_{h}^{\iota}(t)\geq(C(h))^{-1}.

(298)

Next, the following Proposition
6.4
shows that
\rho_{\iota}
admits a
\iota
-independent positive lower bound to the right of the particle path determined by
h
.

Proposition 6.4
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.7
, there exists a constant
C(h/2,T)>0
, independent of
\iota
, such that for all
(y,\tau)\in[h,1]\times[0,T]
,

\rho_{\iota}(y,\tau)\geq(C(h/2,T))^{-1}.

(299)

Proof.

Fix
\beta>2
. We define the following cut-off function
\phi_{h}(y)\in C([0,1])
:

\displaystyle\phi_{h}(y)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&y\in[0,\frac{h}{2}],\\
(y-\frac{h}{2})^{\beta},&y\in[\frac{h}{2},\frac{3h}{4}],\\
\text{linear connection},&y\in[\frac{3h}{4},h],\\
1,&[h,1].\end{array}\right.

Next, we set

\displaystyle v_{\iota}(y,\tau)=\frac{1}{({\rho_{\iota}}r_{\iota}^{N-1})(y,\tau)},

then
\eqref{app system lag'}_{1}
gives

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}v_{\iota}^{\beta}=\beta v_{\iota}^{\beta-1}\partial_{y}(r_{\iota}^{N-1}{u_{\iota}}){r_{\iota}^{1-N}}-(N-1)\beta v_{\iota}^{\beta}{u_{\iota}}r_{\iota}^{-1}.

(304)

Multiplying the above equation by the cut-off function
\phi_{h}(y)
, integrating the resulting equation over
[\frac{h}{2},1]\times[0,\tau]
, and using integration by parts together with boundary condition
\eqref{app system lag'}_{4}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\phi_{h}dy(\tau)&=\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\phi_{h}dy(0)+\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}(\beta v^{\beta-1}_{\iota}\partial_{y}(r^{N-1}_{\iota}u_{\iota})r^{1-N}_{\iota}-(N-1)\beta v_{\iota}^{\beta}u_{\iota}r^{-1}_{\iota})\phi_{h}dy\\
&=\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\phi_{h}dy(0)+\beta(\beta-1)\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}r_{\iota}^{N-1}u_{\iota}\phi_{h}dyds\\
&\quad-\beta\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta-1}u_{\iota}\partial_{y}\phi_{h}dyds+(N-1)(\beta^{2}-\beta)\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}u_{\iota}r_{\iota}^{-1}\phi_{h}dyds.\end{split}

(305)

From
\eqref{app system lag'}_{1}
and
\eqref{app system lag'}_{2}
we derive the B-D entropy equation

\displaystyle(u_{\iota}+r^{N-1}\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota})_{\tau}+r^{N-1}\partial_{y}(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})=0,

which, upon integration over
(0,s)
, gives

\displaystyle r_{\iota}^{N-1}\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}(y,s)=r_{\iota}^{N-1}\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}(y,0)+u_{\iota}(y,0)-u_{\iota}(y,\tau)-\int_{0}^{s}\partial_{y}(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})r_{\iota}^{N-1}(y,\xi)d\xi.

(306)

Substituting (
306
) into (
305
) yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\phi_{h}dy(\tau)\\
&=\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\phi_{h}dy(0)+\beta(\beta-1)\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\Big(r_{\iota}^{N-1}\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}(y,0)+u_{0,\iota}(y)-u_{\iota}(y,s)\Big)u_{\iota}\phi_{h}dyds\\
&\quad-\beta(\beta-1)\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\left(\int_{0}^{s}\partial_{y}(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})r_{\iota}^{N-1}(y,\xi)d\xi\right)u_{\iota}\phi_{h}dyds-\beta\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta-1}u_{\iota}\partial_{y}\phi_{h}dyds\\
&\quad+(N-1)(\beta^{2}-\beta)\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}u_{\iota}r_{\iota}^{-1}\phi_{h}dyds,\end{split}

(307)

which, together with Young’s inequality, implies

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\phi_{h}dy(\tau)+\frac{\beta(\beta-1)}{2}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}u_{\iota}^{2}\phi_{h}dyds\\
&\leq\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\phi_{h}dy(0)+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}|r_{\iota}^{N-1}\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}(y,0)+u_{0,\iota}(y)|^{2}\phi_{h}dyds\\
&\quad+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\left(\int_{0}^{s}\partial_{y}(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})r_{\iota}^{N-1}(y,\xi)d\xi\right)^{2}\phi_{h}dyds+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta-2}|\partial_{y}\phi_{h}|^{2}\phi_{h}^{-1}dyds\\
&\quad+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}r_{\iota}^{-2}\phi_{h}dyds\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{5}W_{i}.\end{split}

(308)

Using (
280
) and (
298
), we obtain

\displaystyle|W_{1}|\leq C\left\|\rho_{\iota}^{-\beta}r_{\iota}^{\beta(1-N)}(0)\right\|_{L^{\infty}((h/2,1))}\leq C(h/2).

(309)

Similarly, from (
281
), (
282
) and (
298
), one gets

\displaystyle\begin{split}|W_{2}|&\leq C\left(\left\|r_{\iota}^{N-1}\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}(0)\right\|_{L^{\infty}((h/2,1))}^{2}+\left\|u_{\iota,0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((h/2,1))}^{2}\right)\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\phi_{h}dyds\\
&\leq C(h/2)\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\phi_{h}dyds.\end{split}

(310)

We now estimate the complex term
W_{3}
. We claim that for all
(y,s)\in[h/2,1]\times[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}|\partial_{y}(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})r_{\iota}^{N-1}(y,\xi)|^{p}d\xi\leq C(h/2,T).

(311)

In fact, it follows from (
306
), (
295
), (
281
), (
282
) and (
298
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{s}|\partial_{y}(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})r_{\iota}^{N-1}(y,\xi)|^{p}d\xi=\gamma^{p}\int_{0}^{s}\rho_{\iota}^{p(\gamma-1)}|\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}r_{\iota}^{N-1}|^{p}d\xi\\
&=\gamma^{p}\int_{0}^{s}\rho_{\iota}^{p(\gamma-1)}|r_{\iota,0}^{N-1}\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota,0}+u_{0,\iota}-u_{\iota}-\int_{0}^{\xi}\partial_{y}(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})r_{\iota}^{N-1}d\zeta|^{p}d\xi\\
&\leq C\left(h/2,T\right)+C\int_{0}^{s}\left\|\rho_{\iota}^{p(\gamma-1)}u_{\iota}^{p}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((h/2,1))}d\xi+C(T)\int_{0}^{s}\left(\int_{0}^{\xi}|\partial_{y}(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})r_{\iota}^{N-1}(y,\zeta)|^{p}d\zeta\right)d\xi\\
&\leq C\left(h/2,T\right)+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}\Big(\rho_{\iota}^{p(\gamma-1)}|u_{\iota}|^{p}+|\partial_{y}(\rho_{\iota}^{p(\gamma-1)})||u_{\iota}|^{p}+\rho_{\iota}^{p(\gamma-1)}|u_{\iota}|^{p-1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|\Big)dyd\xi\\
&\quad+C(T)\int_{0}^{s}\left(\int_{0}^{\xi}|\partial_{y}(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})r_{\iota}^{N-1}(y,\zeta)|^{p}d\zeta\right)d\xi\\
&\leq C\left(h/2,T\right)+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}\Big(1+|u_{\iota}|^{2p}+|\partial_{y}(\rho_{\iota}^{p(\gamma-1)})|^{2}\Big)dyd\xi\\
&\quad+C\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}\Big(\rho_{\iota}^{2}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}u_{\iota}^{2p-2}r_{\iota}^{2(N-1)}+\rho_{\iota}^{2p(\gamma-1)-2}r_{\iota}^{-2(N-1)}\Big)dyd\xi\\
&\quad+C(T)\int_{0}^{s}\left(\int_{0}^{\xi}|\partial_{y}(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})r_{\iota}^{N-1}(y,\zeta)|^{p}d\zeta\right)d\xi\\
&\leq C\left(h/2,T\right)+C(T)\int_{0}^{s}\left(\int_{0}^{\xi}|(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})_{y}r_{\iota}^{N-1}(y,\zeta)|^{p}d\zeta\right)d\xi,\end{split}

(312)

where in the last inequality we have used (
293
), (
292
), (
40
) and (
295
). Therefore, an application of Gronwall’s inequality yields (
311
). Using (
311
), we obtain

\displaystyle|W_{3}|\leq C(h/2,T)\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\phi_{h}dyds.

(313)

From the definition (
6.3
) of the cut-off function, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}|W_{4}|&\leq C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\phi_{h}dyds+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}\phi_{h}|^{\beta}\phi_{h}^{1-\beta}dyds\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\phi_{h}dyds+C(h/2,T).\end{split}

(314)

By (
298
), we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}|W_{5}|&\leq C(h/2)\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\phi_{h}dyds.\end{split}

(315)

Substituting (
309
)–(
315
) into (
308
) gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\phi_{h}dy(\tau)\leq C(h/2,T)+C(h/2,T)\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\phi_{h}dyds.\end{split}

Using Young’s inequality, one has

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}\phi_{h}dy(\tau)\leq C(h/2,T),

which implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\int_{h}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}dy(\tau)\leq C(h/2,T).

(316)

Denote
V_{\iota,T,h}=\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left\|\rho_{\iota}^{-1}(\tau)\right\|_{L^{\infty}((h,1))}+1
. We then obtain from the one-dimensional Sobolev embedding, (
298
), (
292
) and (
316
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\|v_{\iota}^{\beta}(\tau)\right\|_{L^{\infty}((h,1))}&\leq\int_{h}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}dy+\int_{h}^{1}|\partial_{y}(v_{\iota}^{\beta})|dy\\
&\leq\int_{h}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta}dy+C(h/2)\left(\int_{h}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta+1}|\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}|dy+\int_{h}^{1}v_{\iota}^{\beta+1}dy\right)\\
&\leq C(h/2,T)V_{\iota,T,h}+C(h/2)\left(\int_{h}^{1}|\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}|^{2}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{h}^{1}v_{\iota}^{2\beta+2}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\leq C(h/2,T)V_{\iota,T,h}^{\frac{\beta}{2}+1}.\end{split}

(317)

Taking the supremum over
\tau\in[0,T]
and using Young’s inequality together with the fact that
\beta>2
, we obtain (
299
).

This completes the proof of Proposition
6.4
.
∎

Then we show that on the right side of the particle path determined by
h
, the velocity field satisfies a higher-order estimate whose bound is independent of
\iota
.

Proposition 6.5
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.7
, there exists a constant
C(h/4,T)>0
, independent of
\iota
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\int_{h}^{1}(\partial_{y}u_{\iota})^{2}dy+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{h}^{1}((\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota})^{2}+(\partial_{yy}u_{\iota})^{2})dyd\tau\leq C(h/4,T).

(318)

Proof.

It follows from
\eqref{app system lag'}_{2}
that

\displaystyle(u_{\iota})_{\tau}+(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})_{y}r_{\iota}^{N-1}-(\rho_{\iota}^{2}(r_{\iota}^{N-1}u_{\iota})_{y})_{y}r_{\iota}^{N-1}+(N-1)r_{\iota}^{N-2}(\rho_{\iota})_{y}u_{\iota}=0.

(319)

Multiplying the above equation by
(u_{\iota})_{\tau}\phi_{h}
, integrating over
[\frac{h}{2},1]
, and using the boundary condition
(
6.1
)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}(\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota})^{2}\phi_{h}dy&+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}\partial_{y}(\rho^{\gamma}_{\iota})r_{\iota}^{N-1}\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}\phi_{h}dy\\
&+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}\rho_{\iota}^{2}(r_{\iota}^{N-1}u_{\iota})_{y}(r_{\iota}^{N-1}\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}\phi_{h})_{y}dy+(N-1)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}r_{\iota}^{N-2}\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}u_{\iota}\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}\phi_{h}dy=0.\end{split}

(320)

Then a direct calculation gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}\rho_{\iota}^{2}(r_{\iota}^{N-1}u_{\iota})_{y}(r_{\iota}^{N-1}\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}\phi_{h})_{y}dy\\
&=\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}\left(\rho_{\iota}^{2}r_{\iota}^{N-1}\partial_{y}u_{\iota}+\frac{(N-1)\rho_{\iota}u_{\iota}}{r_{\iota}}\right)(r_{\iota}^{N-1}\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}\phi_{h})_{y}dy\\
&=(N-1)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}r_{\iota}^{N-2}\rho_{\iota}\partial_{y}u_{\iota}\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}\phi_{h}dy+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}\partial_{y}u_{\iota}\partial_{y\tau}u_{\iota}\rho_{\iota}^{2}r_{\iota}^{2(N-1)}\phi_{h}dy\\
&\quad+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}r_{\iota}^{2(N-1)}\partial_{y}u_{\iota}\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}\rho_{\iota}^{2}\partial_{y}\phi_{h}dy-(N-1)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}\left(\frac{\rho_{\iota}u_{\iota}}{r_{\iota}}\right)_{y}r_{\iota}^{N-1}\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}\phi_{h}dy\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}(\partial_{y}u_{\iota})^{2}\rho_{\iota}^{2}r_{\iota}^{2(N-1)}\phi_{h}dy-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}(\partial_{y}u_{\iota})^{2}\partial_{\tau}(\rho_{\iota}^{2}r_{\iota}^{2(N-1)})\phi_{h}dy\\
&\quad+(N-1)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}r_{\iota}^{N-2}\rho_{\iota}\partial_{y}u_{\iota}\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}\phi_{h}dy+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}r_{\iota}^{2(N-1)}\partial_{y}u_{\iota}\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}\rho_{\iota}^{2}\partial_{y}\phi_{h}dy\\
&\quad-(N-1)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}\left(\frac{\rho_{\iota}u_{\iota}}{r_{\iota}}\right)_{y}r_{\iota}^{N-1}\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}\phi_{h}dy.\end{split}

(321)

Substituting (
321
) into (
320
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}(\partial_{y}u_{\iota})^{2}\rho_{\iota}^{2}r_{\iota}^{2(N-1)}\phi_{h}dy+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}(\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota})^{2}\phi_{h}dy\\
&=-\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}\partial_{y}(\rho^{\gamma}_{\iota})r_{\iota}^{N-1}\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}\phi_{h}dy+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}(\partial_{y}u_{\iota})^{2}\partial_{\tau}(\rho_{\iota}^{2}r_{\iota}^{2(N-1)})\phi_{h}dy\\
&\quad-(N-1)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}r_{\iota}^{N-2}\rho_{\iota}\partial_{y}u_{\iota}\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}\phi_{h}dy-\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}r_{\iota}^{2(N-1)}\partial_{y}u_{\iota}\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}\rho_{\iota}^{2}\partial_{y}\phi_{h}dy\\
&\quad+(N-1)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}\left(\frac{\rho_{\iota}u_{\iota}}{r_{\iota}}\right)_{y}r_{\iota}^{N-1}\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}\phi_{h}dy-(N-1)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}r_{\iota}^{N-2}\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}u_{\iota}\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}\phi_{\iota}dy\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{6}Z_{i}.\end{split}

(322)

Next, we estimate
Z_{1}
–
Z_{6}
. It follows from (
298
), (
295
), and (
299
) that for any
(y,\tau)\in[h/2,1]\times[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&C(h/2)\leq r_{\iota}(y,\tau)\leq R,\\
&(C(h/4,T))^{-1}\leq\rho_{\iota}(y,\tau)\leq C(T).\end{split}

(323)

Therefore, Young’s inequality together with (
291
), (
292
), (
6.3
) and (
323
) implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad|Z_{1}|+|Z_{3}|+|Z_{4}|+|Z_{5}|+|Z_{6}|\\
&\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}(\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota})^{2}\phi_{h}dy+C(h/4,T)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}\Big(|\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}|^{2}+|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}+|\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}|^{2}|u_{\iota}|^{2}+|u_{\iota}|^{2}\Big)\phi_{h}dy\\
&\quad+C(h/4,T)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}|\partial_{y}\phi_{h}|^{2}\phi_{h}^{-1}dy\\
&\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}(\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota})^{2}\phi_{h}dy+C(h/4,T)\left(1+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}dy\right),\end{split}

(324)

where in the last inequality we have used the fact that

\displaystyle\left\|u_{\iota}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((h/2,1))}^{2}\leq C\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|u_{\iota}|^{2}dy+C\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}dy.

(325)

Finally, we estimate the complex term
Z_{2}
. A direct calculation, together with (
323
), shows that

\displaystyle|Z_{2}|\leq C(h/4,T)\left(\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{3}\phi_{h}dy+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}|u_{\iota}|\phi_{h}dy\right).

(326)

From (
319
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left(\rho_{\iota}^{2}\left(r_{\iota}^{N-1}\partial_{y}u_{\iota}+\frac{(N-1)u_{\iota}}{\rho_{\iota}r_{\iota}}\right)\right)_{y}r_{\iota}^{N-1}=(u_{\iota})_{\tau}+r^{N-1}_{\iota}(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})_{y}+(N-1)r_{\iota}^{N-2}(\rho_{\iota})_{y}u_{\iota},

which implies that

\displaystyle\rho_{\iota}^{2}r_{\iota}^{2(N-1)}\partial_{yy}u_{\iota}

\displaystyle=-\left[(\rho_{\iota}^{2}r_{\iota}^{N-1})_{y}\partial_{y}u_{\iota}+(N-1)\left(\frac{u_{\iota}\rho_{\iota}}{r_{\iota}}\right)_{y}\right]r_{\iota}^{N-1}

(327)

\displaystyle\quad+(u_{\iota})_{\tau}+r_{\iota}^{N-1}(\rho^{\gamma}_{\iota})_{y}+(N-1)r_{\iota}^{N-2}(\rho_{\iota})_{y}u_{\iota}.

Thus, it deduces from (
323
) that, for any
(y,\tau)\in[h/2,1]\times[0,T]
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{yy}u_{\iota}(y,\tau)|\leq C(h/4,T)(|\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}|+1)(|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|+|u_{\iota}|+1)+C(h/4,T)|\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}|,

and using (
291
), (
292
), (
6.3
), and (
323
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{yy}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy\\
&\leq C(h/4,T)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy+C(h/4,T)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}(|\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}|^{2}+1)(|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}+|u_{\iota}|^{2}+1)\phi_{h}dy\\
&\leq C(h/4,T)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy+C(h/4,T)\left(1+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}dy+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}|^{2}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy\right)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{yy}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy+C(h/4,T)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy+C(h/4,T)\left(1+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}dy\right),\end{split}

(328)

where in the last inequality we have used (
294
), (
323
), and (
6.3
) to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&C(h/4,T)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}|^{2}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy\\
&\leq C(h/4,T)\left(\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}|^{2q}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\left(\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{\frac{2q}{q-1}}\phi_{h}^{\frac{q}{q-1}}dy\right)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}\\
&\leq C(h/4,T)\left\|(\partial_{y}u_{\iota})^{2}\phi_{h}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{q}}\left(\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy\right)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}\\
&\leq C(h/4,T)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}\Big((\partial_{y}u_{\iota})^{2}\phi_{h}+|\partial_{yy}u_{\iota}||\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|\phi_{h}+(\partial_{y}u_{\iota})^{2}|\partial_{y}\phi_{h}|\Big)dy+C(h/4,T)\int_{h/2}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}dy\\
&\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{yy}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy+C(h/4,T)\int_{h/2}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}dy.\end{split}

We obtain from (
328
) that

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{yy}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy\leq C(h/4,T)\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy+C(h/4,T)\left(1+\int_{h/2}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}dy\right).

(329)

Now, based on (
329
), (
325
), (
6.3
), and (
326
), it is sufficient to estimate
Z_{2}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}|Z_{2}|&\leq C(h/4,T)\left\|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}\phi_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((h/2,1))}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}^{\frac{1}{2}}dy+C(h/2,T)\left\|u_{\iota}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((h/2,1))}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy\\
&\leq\varepsilon\left\|(\partial_{y}u_{\iota})^{2}\phi_{h}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((h/2,1))}+C(h/4,T)\left(1+\int_{h/2}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}dy\right)\left(1+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy\right)\\
&\leq\varepsilon\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{yy}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy+C(h/4,T)\left(1+\int_{h/2}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}dy\right)\left(1+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy\right)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy+C(h/4,T)\left(1+\int_{h/2}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}dy\right)\left(1+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy\right),\end{split}

(330)

where
\varepsilon
is a sufficiently small positive constant depending on
h/4
and
T
. Substituting (
324
), (
330
) and (
329
) into (
322
) gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\frac{d}{d\tau}\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}(\partial_{y}u_{\iota})^{2}\rho_{\iota}^{2}r_{\iota}^{2(N-1)}\phi_{h}dy+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{yy}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy\\
&\leq C(h/4,T)\left(1+\int_{h/2}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}dy\right)\left(1+\int_{\frac{h}{2}}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}\phi_{h}dy\right),\end{split}

which, together with Gronwall’s inequality, (
282
), (
323
), and (
291
), yields (
318
).
∎

Finally, we establish an
\iota
-independent bound for the density gradient on the right-hand side of the particle path determined by
h
.

Proposition 6.6
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.7
, there exists a constant
C(h/4,T)>0
, independent of
\iota
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left\|u_{\iota}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((h,1))}+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left\|\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((h,1))}\leq C(h/4,T).

(331)

Proof.

Using the one-dimensional Sobolev embedding, together with (
291
) and (
318
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left\|u_{\iota}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((h,1))}\leq C\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left(\int_{h}^{1}u_{\iota}^{2}dy+\int_{h}^{1}|\partial_{y}u_{\iota}|^{2}dy\right)\leq C(h/4,T).\end{split}

(332)

From (
306
), (
298
), (
281
), (
282
), (
332
), and (
311
), it follows that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left\|\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((h,1))}\\
&\leq C(h)\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\left\|\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota}r_{\iota}^{N-1}(y,0)+u_{\iota}(y,0)-u_{\iota}(y,\tau)+\int_{0}^{\tau}\partial_{y}(\rho_{\iota}^{\gamma})r_{\iota}^{N-1}(y,s)ds\right\|_{L^{\infty}((h,1))}\\
&\leq C(h/4,T).\end{split}

(333)

This completes the proof of Proposition
6.6
.
∎

Now, combining the estimates of Proposition
6.2
,
6.4
–
6.6
, the we change all the estimates back to the Eulerian coordinates. That is, it holds that

\displaystyle r_{h}^{\iota}(t)\geq(C(h))^{-1},\ t\in[0,T];

(334)

\displaystyle\ 0<(C(h/2,T))^{-1}\leq\rho_{\iota}(r,t)\leq C(T),\ (r,t)\in\big[r_{h}^{\iota}(t),R\big]\times[0,T];

(335)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\iota}^{R}\rho_{\iota}u_{\iota}^{2p}r^{N-1}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\iota}^{R}\big(\rho_{\iota}u_{\iota}^{2p}r^{N-3}+{\rho_{\iota}u_{\iota}^{2p-2}(\partial_{r}u_{\iota})^{2}}{r^{N-1}}\big)drdt\leq C(T);

(336)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\iota}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\iota}^{\frac{1}{2q}}|^{2q}r^{N-1}dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\iota}^{R}|\partial_{r}\rho_{\iota}^{\frac{\gamma}{2q}}|^{2q}r^{N-1}drdt\leq C(T);

(337)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{r_{h}^{\iota}(t)}^{R}\Big((\partial_{r}u_{\iota})^{2}+(\partial_{t}\rho_{\iota})^{2}\Big)dr+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{r_{h}^{\iota}(t)}^{R}{\Big((\partial_{t}u_{\iota})^{2}+(\partial_{rr}u_{\iota})^{2}\Big)}drdt\leq C(h/4,T);

(338)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|u_{\iota}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((r_{h}^{\iota}(t),R))}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\partial_{r}\rho_{\iota}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((r_{h}^{\iota}(t),R))}\leq C(h/4,T),

(339)

where we have used

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{r^{\iota}_{h}(t)}^{R}(\partial_{r}u_{\iota})^{2}dr&\leq C(h/4,T)\sup_{\tau\in[0,T]}\int_{h}^{1}(\partial_{y}u_{\iota})^{2}dy\leq C(h/4,T);\\
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{r^{\iota}_{h}(t)}^{R}(\partial_{t}\rho_{\iota})^{2}dr&\leq C(h/4,T)\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{r_{h}^{\iota}(t)}^{R}\Big(|\partial_{r}\rho_{\iota}|^{2}|u_{\iota}|^{2}+|\partial_{r}u_{\iota}|^{2}+|u_{\iota}|^{2}\Big)dr\leq C(h/4,T);\\
\int_{0}^{T}\int_{r^{\iota}_{h}(t)}^{R}(\partial_{t}u_{\iota})^{2}drdt&\leq C(h/4,T)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{h}^{1}\Big((\partial_{\tau}u_{\iota})^{2}+u_{\iota}^{2}(\partial_{y}u_{\iota})^{2}\Big)dyd\tau\leq C(h/4,T);\\
\int_{0}^{T}\int_{r^{\iota}_{h}(t)}^{R}(\partial_{rr}u_{\iota})^{2}drdt&\leq C(h/4,T)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{h}^{1}\Big((\partial_{yy}u_{\iota})^{2}+(\partial_{y}u_{\iota})^{2}(\partial_{y}\rho_{\iota})^{2}+(\partial_{y}u_{\iota})^{2}\Big)dyd\tau\\
&\leq C(h/4,T).\end{split}

(340)

6.4
The proof of Theorem
2.7

The following Proposition implies that the particle paths
r_{h}^{\iota}(t)
are convergent.

Proposition 6.7
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.7
, let
(\rho_{\iota},u_{\iota})
and
r_{h}^{\iota}(t)
be as described above. Then there exists a subsequence
\iota_{i}
such that
r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(t)
converges uniformly for
(h,t)
in compact subset of
(0,1]\times[0,\infty)
,

\lim_{\iota_{i}\rightarrow 0^{+}}r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(t)=r_{h}(t);

(341)

the limit
r_{h}(t)
is
H\ddot{o}lder
continuous on these compact sets, and if

\underline{r}(t)=\lim_{h\rightarrow 0^{+}}r_{h}(t),

(342)

then
\underline{r}(t)
is a upper semi-continuous curve and
\lim_{t\rightarrow 0}{\underline{r}(t)}=0.

Proof.

Fix
h_{0}\in(0,1)
and
T>0
. For any
h_{0}\leq h_{1}<h_{2}\leq 1
and
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
, it follows from (
334
), (
335
), and (
339
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}|r^{\iota_{i}}_{h_{1}}(t_{1})-r_{h_{2}}^{\iota_{i}}(t_{2})|&\leq|r^{\iota_{i}}_{h_{1}}(t_{1})-r_{h_{2}}^{\iota_{i}}(t_{1})|+|r^{\iota_{i}}_{h_{2}}(t_{1})-r_{h_{2}}^{\iota_{i}}(t_{2})|\\
&\leq\int_{h_{1}}^{h_{2}}|\partial_{h}r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(t_{1})|dh+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}|\partial_{t}r_{h_{2}}^{\iota_{i}}(t)|dt\\
&\leq\int_{h_{1}}^{h_{2}}(\rho_{\iota_{i}}(r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(t_{1}),t_{1})(r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(t_{1}))^{N-1})^{-1}dh+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}|u_{\iota_{i}}(r_{h_{2}}^{\iota_{i}}(t),t)|dt\\
&\leq C(h_{0}/4,T)(|h_{2}-h_{1}|+|t_{2}-t_{1}|),\end{split}

where the third inequality follows from the definition (
297
) of
r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(t)
. Therefore, by the Ascoli–Arzelà theorem,
r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(t)
converges uniformly on compact sets, and the limit function
r_{h}(t)
is Hölder continuous on compact sets.

Next, the monotonicity of
r_{h}(t)
with respect to
h\in[0,1]
ensures that
\underline{r}(t)
is well-defined. Fix
t>0
. For any
s>0
and sufficiently small
h>0
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\underline{r}(t)}&={\underline{r}(s)}+(r_{h}(s)-{\underline{r}(s)})+(r_{h}(t)-r_{h}(s))+({\underline{r}(t)}-r_{h}(t))\\
&\geq{\underline{r}(s)}+(r_{h}(t)-r_{h}(s))+({\underline{r}(t)}-r_{h}(t)),\end{split}

(343)

which, together with the definition of
\underline{r}(t)
and the continuity of
r_{h}(\cdot)
, implies that

\displaystyle\underline{r}(t)\geq\limsup_{s\to t}\underline{r}(s).

(344)

This shows that
\underline{r}(\cdot)
is a upper semi-continuous curve.

Finally, for sufficiently small
h>0
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\underline{r}(t)&=\underline{r}(t)-r_{h}(t)+(r_{h}(t)-r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(t))+r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(t)\\
&\leq(r_{h}(t)-r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(t))+r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(t),\end{split}

(345)

which, together with the uniform convergence of
r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(\cdot)
on
[0,T]
, implies that

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{t\to 0}\underline{r}(t)=0.

(346)

∎

Extend
(\rho_{\iota_{i}},u_{\iota_{i}})
constantly from
[\iota_{i},R]\times[0,T]
to
[0,R]\times[0,T]
and still denote the extended functions by
(\rho_{\iota_{i}},u_{\iota_{i}})
. The following proposition shows that the region to the left of
\underline{r}(\cdot)
is actually the vacuum region.

Proposition 6.8
.

Let the hypotheses and notations of Proposition
6.7
be in force. Then there exists a further subsequence, still denoted by
\iota_{i}
, such that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
,

\rho_{\iota_{i}}(r,t)r^{N-1}\to\rho(r,t)r^{N-1}\quad\text{as }\iota_{i}\to 0^{+},

(347)

uniformly in
C([1/n,R]\times[0,T])
. Furthermore, for any
t\in[0,T]
, it holds that

\displaystyle\rho(r,t)r^{N-1}=0\text{ on }[0,\underline{r}(t)],

(348)

\displaystyle\rho(r,t)>0\text{ on }(\underline{r}(t),R].

(349)

Proof.

Using (
337
), we can prove (
347
) by the same method as in Proposition
5.14
.

It follows from (
335
) and (
297
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\underline{r}(t)}{{\rho_{\iota_{i}}(r,t)}r^{N-1}}dr

\displaystyle=\Big(\int_{0}^{\iota_{i}}+\int_{\iota_{i}}^{r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(t)}+\int_{r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(t)}^{r_{h}(t)}+\int_{r_{h}(t)}^{\underline{r}(t)}\Big){{\rho_{\iota_{i}}(r,t)}r^{N-1}}dr

\displaystyle\leq C(T){\iota_{i}}^{N}+\int_{\iota_{i}}^{r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(t)}{{\rho_{\iota_{i}}(r,t)}r^{N-1}}dr

\displaystyle\ \ +C\left(\int_{\iota_{i}}^{R}\rho_{\iota_{i}}^{\gamma}(r,t)r^{N-1}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\Big[\Big({r_{h}(t)}^{N}-{r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(t)}^{N}\Big)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}}+\Big({\underline{r}(t)}^{N}-{r_{h}(t)}^{N}\Big)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C{\iota_{i}}^{N}+h+C\Big({r_{h}(t)}^{N}-{r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(t)}^{N}\Big)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}}+C\Big({\underline{r}(t)}^{N}-{r_{h}(t)}^{N}\Big)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}}.

Letting
\iota_{i}\to 0
and then
h\to 0
gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\underline{r}(t)}{{\rho(r,t)}r^{N-1}}dr=0,

which shows (
348
).

Finally, for any
r>\underline{r}(t)
, there exists
0<h
such that
\underline{r}(t)<r_{h}(t)<r
. By (
335
) and (
341
) we have
\rho_{\iota_{i}}(r,t)\geq(C(h/2,T))^{-1}
for all sufficiently small
\iota_{i}
, which together with (
347
) implies
\rho(r,t)\geq(C(h/2,T))^{-1}
.

This completes the proof of Proposition
6.8
.
∎

Define

\mathcal{F}\triangleq\big\{(r,t)\mid\underline{r}(t)<r\leq R,\ 0\leq t<\infty\big\}.

(350)

The following Proposition
6.9
provides a precise characterization of the flow region.

Proposition 6.9
.

For any
(r,t)\in\mathcal{F}
, there exist two small positive constants
\tilde{r}
and
\tilde{t}
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}[r-\tilde{r},R]\times[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}]\subset(\underline{r}(\cdot)+\tilde{r},R]\times[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}]\subset\mathcal{F}.\end{split}

(351)

Hence,
\mathcal{F}\cap\{t>0\}\cap\{r<R\}
is open.

Proof.

Set
\tilde{r}=\frac{1}{3}(r-\underline{r}(t))
. Since
\underline{r}(\cdot)
is an upper semicontinuous curve, we have

\displaystyle\underline{r}(t)\geq\inf_{\delta>0}\sup_{s\in B_{\delta}(t)}\underline{r}(s),

(352)

which shows that there exists a sufficiently small positive constant
\tilde{t}
such that

\displaystyle\underline{r}(t)+\tilde{r}>\sup_{s\in B_{\tilde{t}}(t)}\underline{r}(s).

(353)

That is, for all
s\in[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\underline{r}(s)+\tilde{r}<\underline{r}(t)+2\tilde{r}\leq r-\tilde{r},

(354)

which implies (
351
). We have thus proved Proposition
6.9
.
∎

Proposition 6.10
.

Under the hypotheses of Proposition
6.9
, there exist a sufficiently small
h>0
and a constant
T>0
such that

\displaystyle[r-\tilde{r},R]\times[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}]\subset(r_{h}(\cdot),R]\times[0,T].

(355)

Furthermore, for any
h^{\prime}<h
, there exists a sufficiently small
\iota_{i_{0}}
such that for all
0<\iota_{i}<\iota_{i_{0}}
we have

\displaystyle r_{h^{\prime}}^{\iota_{i}}(s)<r_{h}(s),\quad\forall s\in[0,T].

(356)

Proof.

We define

\displaystyle g_{h}(s)=(r_{h}(s)-(r-2\tilde{r}))_{+},\quad s\in[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}].

(357)

Since
g_{h}(\cdot)
is monotonically decreasing in
h
, Proposition
6.9
implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{h\rightarrow 0}g_{h}(s)=\lim_{h\rightarrow 0}(r_{h}(s)-(r-2\tilde{r}))_{+}=0,

which, together with the continuity of
g_{h}(\cdot)
and Dini’s theorem, implies that

\displaystyle g_{h}(s)\rightrightarrows 0,\ s\in[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}].

(358)

Thus, there exists small suitably
h>0
such that, for all
s\in[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}]
,

\displaystyle g_{h}(s)<\tilde{r},

(359)

which implies that

\displaystyle r_{h}(s)<r-\tilde{r}.

(360)

This establishes (
355
). Next, we prove (
356
). Hölder’s inequality shows that for any
\iota_{i}>0
and
s\in[0,T]
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}h-h^{\prime}=&\int_{r_{h^{\prime}}^{\iota_{i}}(s)}^{r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(s)}\rho_{\iota_{i}}(r,s)r^{N-1}dr\\
&\leq C\left(\int_{r_{h^{\prime}}^{\iota_{i}}(s)}^{r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(s)}\rho_{\iota_{i}}^{\gamma}(r,s)r^{N-1}dr\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\left((r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(s))^{N}-(r_{h^{\prime}}^{\iota_{i}}(s))^{N}\right)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}}\\
&\leq C\left((r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(s))^{N}-(r_{h^{\prime}}^{\iota_{i}}(s))^{N}\right)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}},\end{split}

(361)

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle(r_{h}^{\iota_{i}}(s))^{N}-(r_{h^{\prime}}^{\iota_{i}}(s))^{N}\geq C(h-h^{\prime})^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}.

(362)

Letting
\iota_{i}\to 0
, we obtain

\displaystyle r_{h}(s)-r_{h^{\prime}}(s)\geq(C(h^{\prime},h))^{-1}>0.

(363)

Therefore, using Proposition
6.7
, we obtain (
356
). We have proved Proposition
6.10
.
∎

Proposition 6.11
.

Let the hypotheses and notations of Propositions
6.7
-
6.10
be in force. Then there is a further subsequence, still denoted by
\iota_{i}
, such that

{u_{\iota_{i}}}(r,t)\rightarrow u(r,t),\ {\rho_{\iota_{i}}}(r,t)\rightarrow\rho(r,t),\ as\ \iota_{i}\rightarrow 0^{+},

(364)

uniformly on
[r-\tilde{r},R]\times[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}]
, and the limiting function
(\rho,u)
is
H\ddot{o}lder
continuous on these sets.

Proof.

This proof can be found in
[
17
, Proposition 4.3]
, we sketch it here for completeness.

Using Proposition
6.10
, we obtain small constants
h>0
and
T>0
such that

\displaystyle[r-\tilde{r},R]\times[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}]\subset(r_{h}(\cdot),R]\times[0,T].

Fix
h^{\prime}<h
. When
\iota_{i}
is sufficiently small, Proposition
6.10
and (
339
) give

\displaystyle|u_{\iota_{i}}(r,t)|\leq C(h^{\prime}/4,T),\quad\forall(r,t)\in(r_{h}(\cdot),R]\times[0,T].

Next, we show that the approximate solutions have a uniform Hölder norm on each rectangular region
[r-\tilde{r},R]\times[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}]
. For any
t\in[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}]
and
r_{1},r_{2}\in[r-\tilde{r},R]
, it follows from (
338
) that

\displaystyle|{u_{\iota_{i}}}(r_{2},t)-{u_{\iota_{i}}}(r_{1},t)|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\partial_{r}{u_{\iota_{i}}}(t)dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}{({\partial_{r}u_{\iota_{i}}})}^{2}dr(t)\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}(r_{2}-r_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(h^{\prime}/4,T)(r_{2}-r_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For any
r\in[r-\tilde{r},R]
and
t_{1},t_{2}\in[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}]
, let
k=\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}}
. Fix
h^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\in(h^{\prime},h)
. By (
363
), there exists a constant
\varepsilon(h,h^{\prime\prime})>0
, depending only on
h
and
h^{\prime\prime}
, such that

\displaystyle r_{h}(s)-r_{h^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}(s)\geq\varepsilon(h,h^{\prime\prime}),\quad\forall s\in[0,T].

We split the discussion into two cases. When
k<\varepsilon(h,h^{\prime\prime})
, we have

\displaystyle r-k\geq r-\tilde{r}-k\geq r_{h}(s)-k\geq r_{h^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}(s),\quad\forall s\in[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}].

Therefore, (
356
) and (
338
) imply that

\displaystyle\quad\big|{u_{\iota_{i}}}(r,t_{2})-{u_{\iota_{i}}}(r,t_{1})\big|

(365)

\displaystyle=k^{-1}\int_{r-k}^{r}\big|{u_{\iota_{i}}}(r,t_{2})-{u_{\iota_{i}}}(r,t_{1})\big|d\xi

\displaystyle\leq k^{-1}\int_{r-k}^{r}\big|{u_{\iota_{i}}}(\xi,t_{2})-{u_{\iota_{i}}}(\xi,t_{1})\big|d\xi+k^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{2}{\int_{r-k}^{r}\big|{u_{\iota_{i}}}(r,t_{i})-{u_{\iota_{i}}}(\xi,t_{i})\big|d\xi}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{k}\Big(\int_{r-k}^{r}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\big({\partial_{t}u_{\iota_{i}}}(r,t)\big)^{2}dtdr\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+k^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{r-k}^{r}\int_{\xi}^{r}|\partial_{r}u_{\iota_{i}}(\zeta,t_{i})|d\zeta d\xi

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{k}\Big(\int_{r-k}^{r}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\big({\partial_{t}u_{\iota_{i}}}(r,t)\big)^{2}dtdr\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+k^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{r-k}^{r}\int_{\xi}^{r}|\partial_{r}u_{\iota_{i}}(\zeta,t_{i})|d\zeta d\xi

\displaystyle\leq C(h^{\prime}/4,T)(t_{2}-t_{1})^{\frac{1}{4}}.

(366)

When
k\geq\varepsilon(h^{\prime\prime},h)
, it follows from (
339
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}|{u_{\iota_{i}}}(r,t_{2})-{u_{\iota_{i}}}(r,t_{1})\big|&\leq 2\sup_{t_{1}\leq t\leq t_{2}}\left\|u_{\iota_{i}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((r_{h}(t),R])}\\
&\leq 2\sup_{t_{1}\leq t\leq t_{2}}\left\|u_{\iota_{i}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}((r^{\iota_{i}}_{h^{\prime}}(t),R])}\\
&\leq C(h^{\prime}/4,T)(\varepsilon(h^{{}^{\prime\prime}},h^{\prime}))^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t_{2}-t_{1})^{\frac{1}{4}}\\
&\leq C(h^{\prime}/4,T)(t_{2}-t_{1})^{\frac{1}{4}}.\end{split}

(367)

Thus we have shown that on each rectangle
u_{\iota_{i}}
possesses a uniform Hölder norm. we conclude that
\{u_{\iota_{i}}(r,t)\}
is uniformly bounded and Hölder continuous, jointly in
r,t
, on
[r-\tilde{r},R]\times[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}]
. Therefore, by the Ascoli–Arzelà theorem,
u_{\iota_{i}}
converges uniformly on the set, and the limit function
u
is Hölder continuous on the set.

Using (
337
) and (
338
), the same conclusion can be shown to hold for
\rho
. This completes the proof of Proposition
6.11
.
∎

Proposition 6.12
.

Let the hypotheses and notations of Proposition
6.7
-
6.10
be in force. For any
(r,t)\in\mathcal{F}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho\in L^{\infty}((t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t};W^{1,\infty}([r-\tilde{r},R]));\\
&\partial_{t}\rho\in L^{\infty}((t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t};L^{2}([r-\tilde{r},R]));\\
&u\in L^{\infty}((t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t};H^{1}([r-\tilde{r},R]))\cap L^{2}((t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t};H^{2}([r-\tilde{r},R]));\\
&\partial_{t}u\in L^{2}((t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t};L^{2}([r-\tilde{r},R]).\end{split}

(368)

Proof.

Let
h
and
h^{\prime}
be as in Proposition
6.10
. Then for sufficiently small
\iota_{i}
we have

\displaystyle[r-\tilde{r},R]\times[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}]\subset(r_{h^{\prime}}^{\iota_{i}}(\cdot),R]\times[0,T].

(369)

We thus deduce from (
338
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}]}\int_{r-\tilde{r}}^{R}\Big((\partial_{r}u_{\iota_{i}})^{2}+(\partial_{t}\rho_{\iota_{i}})^{2}\Big)dr+\int_{(t-\tilde{t})_{+}}^{t+\tilde{t}}\int_{r-\tilde{r}}^{R}{\Big((\partial_{t}u_{\iota_{i}})^{2}+(\partial_{rr}u_{\iota_{i}})^{2}\Big)}drdt\leq C(h^{\prime}/4,T),

and form (
339
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[(t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t}]}\left\|\partial_{r}\rho_{\iota_{i}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([r-\tilde{r},R])}\leq C(h^{\prime}/4,T).

This shows that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho_{\iota_{i}}\rightharpoonup\rho,\text{ weak-$*$ in }L^{\infty}((t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t};W^{1,\infty}([r-\tilde{r},R]));\\
&\partial_{t}\rho_{\iota_{i}}\rightharpoonup\partial_{t}\rho,\text{ weak-$*$ in }L^{\infty}((t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t};L^{2}([r-\tilde{r},R]));\\
&u_{\iota_{i}}\rightharpoonup u,\text{ weak-$*$ in }L^{\infty}((t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t};H^{1}([r-\tilde{r},R]));\\
&\partial_{t}u_{\iota_{i}}\rightharpoonup\partial_{t}u,\text{ weak in }L^{2}((t-\tilde{t})_{+},t+\tilde{t};L^{2}([r-\tilde{r},R])).\end{split}

This completes the proof of Proposition
6.12
.
∎

Proposition 6.13
.

Let the hypotheses and notations of Proposition
6.7
be in force. It holds that

\displaystyle\underline{r}(t)\leq\left(R^{N}-\frac{M_{0}^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}}{\omega_{N}((\gamma-1)E_{0})^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{N}},

(370)

where
\omega_{N}
denotes the measure of the unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{N}
.

Proof.

From
\eqref{App Stm Euler'}_{1}
and (
291
) it follows that for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\Omega_{\iota_{i}}}\rho_{\iota_{i}}(t,x)dx=\int_{\Omega_{\iota_{i}}}\rho_{\iota_{i},0}(x)dx=M_{0};\\
&\frac{1}{\gamma-1}\int_{\Omega_{\iota_{i}}}\rho_{\iota_{i}}^{\gamma}(t,x)dx\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{\iota_{i}}}\rho_{\iota_{i},0}u_{\iota_{i},0}^{2}dx+\frac{1}{\gamma-1}\int_{\Omega_{\iota_{i}}}\rho_{\iota_{i},0}^{\gamma}(x)dx.\end{split}

Letting
\iota_{i}\to 0
and using (
335
), (
347
), and (
280
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\Omega}\rho(t,x)dx=\int_{\Omega}\rho_{0}(x)dx=M_{0};\\
&\frac{1}{\gamma-1}\int_{\Omega}\rho^{\gamma}(t,x)dx\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\rho_{0}u_{0}^{2}dx+\frac{1}{\gamma-1}\int_{\Omega}\rho_{0}^{\gamma}(x)dx=E_{0}.\end{split}

Therefore, it follows from Proposition
6.8
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}M_{0}&=\int_{\Omega}\rho(t,x)dx=\int_{\{\rho(t)>0\}}\rho(t,x)dx\\
&\leq\left(\int_{\Omega}\rho^{\gamma}(t,x)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}|\{\rho(t)>0\}|^{\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}}\\
&=((\gamma-1)E_{0})^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\left(\omega_{N}(R^{N}-(\underline{r}(t))^{N})\right)^{\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}},\end{split}

(371)

which implies that

\displaystyle\underline{r}(t)\leq\left(R^{N}-\frac{M_{0}^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}}{\omega_{N}((\gamma-1)E_{0})^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{N}}.

(372)

This completes the proof of Proposition
6.13
.
∎

7
Appendix

In this section we collect several lemmas required in the proof.

Lemma 7.1
.

The function
\alpha_{2,-}(x)
introduced in Definition
2.2
is strictly increasing on
(1,\infty)
with

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{x\to 1^{+}}\alpha_{2,-}(x)=\frac{1}{2},\quad\lim\limits_{x\to\infty}\alpha_{2,-}(x)=1;

(373)

while
\alpha_{2,+}(x)
is strictly decreasing on
(1,\infty)
with

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{x\to 1^{+}}\alpha_{2,+}(x)=+\infty,\quad\lim\limits_{x\to\infty}\alpha_{2,+}(x)=1.

(374)

Proof.

By L’Hospital rule, (
373
) is easily verified.
To show that
\alpha_{2,-}(\cdot)
is strictly increasing on
(1,\infty)
, we compute directly:

\displaystyle\alpha_{2,-}^{\prime}(x)=\frac{1}{(x-1)^{3}\sqrt{2x-1}}\left(x^{2}-2x\sqrt{2x-1}+2x-1\right),\quad x>1.

Define the auxiliary function
f_{1}(x)=x^{2}-2x\sqrt{2x-1}+2x-1
. Then a direct computation gives

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\prime}(x)=2\left(1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2x-1}}\right)\left(x-\sqrt{2x-1}\right)>0,\quad x>1,

which together with
f_{1}(1)=0
shows that
f_{1}(x)>0
for every
x\in(1,\infty)
. Hence
\alpha_{2,-}^{\prime}(x)>0
for all
x\in(1,\infty)
, so
\alpha_{2,-}(\cdot)
is strictly increasing. The corresponding statement for
\alpha_{2,+}(\cdot)
is obtained in the same way and its proof is omitted.
∎

Lemma 7.2
.

The function
\alpha_{3,-}(x)
introduced in Definition
2.4
is strictly increasing on
(1,\infty)
with

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{x\to 1^{+}}\alpha_{3,-}(x)=\frac{2}{3},\quad\lim\limits_{x\to\infty}\alpha_{3,-}(x)=1;

(375)

while
\alpha_{3,+}(x)
is strictly decreasing on
(1,\infty)
with

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{x\to 1^{+}}\alpha_{3,+}(x)=+\infty,\quad\lim\limits_{x\to\infty}\alpha_{3,+}(x)=1.

(376)

Proof.

We only prove the properties of
\alpha_{3,+}(\cdot)
. Those of
\alpha_{3,-}(\cdot)
are analogous. L’Hospital rule immediately verifies (
376
). A direct computation gives

\displaystyle\alpha_{3,+}^{\prime}(x)=\frac{-4x^{3}-10x^{2}+5x-3x\sqrt{16x^{3}-4x^{2}-4x+1}}{2(x-1)^{3}\sqrt{16x^{3}-4x^{2}-4x+1}}<0,\quad x>1,

which shows that
\alpha_{3,+}(x)
is strictly decreasing on
(1,\infty)
. The proof is complete.
∎

Below we prove a technical elementary inequality.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
s\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
. Then there exists
\varepsilon>0
such that

\displaystyle a(a+b)^{1+\frac{2s}{2k+1}}\geq\varepsilon|a|^{2+\frac{2s}{2k+1}}-|b|^{2+\frac{2s}{2k+1}},\quad\forall a,b\in\mathbb{R}.

Proof.

Assume without loss of generality that
a\neq 0
, the inequality is equivalent to

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{b}{a}\right)^{1+\frac{2s}{2k+1}}+\left|\frac{b}{a}\right|^{2+\frac{2s}{2k+1}}\geq\varepsilon.

Consider the function
f(t)=\left(1+t\right)^{1+\frac{2s}{2k+1}}+t^{2+\frac{2s}{2k+1}}
, as
t\to\infty
,
f(t)\to+\infty
. Hence
f
possesses a positive lower bound if and only if it has no zeros. We claim that
f
never vanishes. Otherwise, there would exist a
t_{0}
such that

\displaystyle f(t_{0})=0\Longleftrightarrow t_{0}^{\frac{4k+2s+2}{2k+2s+1}}+t_{0}+1=0.

Define
g(t)=t^{\frac{d}{c}}+t+1
, where
d>c
,
d
is even, and
c
is odd. A simple computation gives

\displaystyle g^{\prime}(t)=\frac{d}{c}t^{\frac{d-c}{c}}+1.

Thus
g
first decreases and then increases. Its minimum is attained at
t_{1}=\left(-\frac{c}{d}\right)^{\frac{c}{d-c}}
, and we have

\displaystyle g(t_{1})=\left(\frac{c}{d}\right)^{\frac{d}{d-c}}+\left(-\frac{c}{d}\right)^{\frac{c}{d-c}}+1>0.

This contradicts the assumption that
f
has a zero.
∎

Lemma 7.4
.

Assuming
N=2
and
\mathbf{v}(x)=v(r)\frac{x}{r}
satisfies the boundary conditions
v(0)=v(R)=0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C\left\|\nabla\mathbf{v}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

Proof.

For any
r\in(0,R)
, a direct computation shows that

\displaystyle v(r)^{2}

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{r}v\partial_{r}vdr\leq 2\int_{0}^{R}|\partial_{r}v|\left|\frac{v}{r}\right|rdr

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{R}\left(|\partial_{r}v|^{2}+\left|\frac{v}{r}\right|^{2}\right)rdr\leq C\left\|\nabla\mathbf{v}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

This completes the proof.
∎
